Summary

When Kate goes home with Clint to see his family she receives a surprising Christmas present which will turn all the relationships on their ends.

Kate discovers Clint and Laura are her bio parents and they all have to deal with that.

In the morning I'll be with you - hiatus

    Chapter 1

    “Clint, somebody is taking notes on your family.”

    There was a beat of silence. “What?”

    “Clint Barton, wife: Laura, daughter: Lila, age 14, son, Cooper, age 16, son, Nate age 6, daughter, deceased- Ni-” ".

    “Stop.” Kate could hear him swallow and the fear in his voice he was trying to hide.

    “What, how’d…? No, it doesn’t - Kate, get out of there now .”

    Clint wasn’t used to Kate being so quiet, normally she was talking fast, about everything, constantly, but as they sat on the back of the ambulance she was silent.

    He didn’t blame her, not at all, her mom had just been taken away, it was clear that they weren’t close, Clint hadn’t liked Eleanor from how she talked to Kate, and that was before they found out she was what Kate would call a ‘bad guy’, but she was Kate’s mom.

    Clint gently nudged her with his shoulder, when she looked up at him she didn’t have her usual stubbornness, her fight, she looked so young, but then she was little more than a kid. “You did good, kid,” he told her, not for the first time. He knew his face had several cuts on it, but he’d had that before, Kate had some and that worried him more, she had several cuts on her face, and the paramedic had said she had a concussion. “You sure you don’t need to go get checked out by the docs?”

    She shook her head and grimaced, “I’m good.”

    “That’s not exactly what they said, they just said that they couldn’t force you to go.”

    “Clint, seriously, I’m okay. Well, not okay okay, but I’m okay… you know what I mean.”

    Clint frowned, but as he had also declined to go to the hospital it wasn’t like he could take her, or even force her to go. “I’m proud of you, Katie, not for refusing to get check out, but for the rest of it… thank you.”

    Kate ducked her head to hide the smile which took over her face, she forced herself to her feet, “We should go walk Pizza Dog.”

    Kate took Lucky on a walk by herself when they go back to the ‘safehouse’, telling Clint he should probably pack, he had a long drive back, she was hoping that when she got back he would be gone, a note would be nice, but she wasn’t sure if she’d be able to stay strong saying bye to him in person.

    She tried not to think too hard as she walked, but like that ever worked, she tried not to focus on Clint, instead she forced herself to think of less heartbreaking matters, like wondering where Yelena was now, and where Kate would live once her distant aunt returned, if her bank cards would work now her mother was arrested, maybe she could find a place another another Pizzeria, or she could go for different takeout, Thai, or Chinese perhaps?

    Lucky stopped walking before Kate did, the latter not realising that they were home again, or that Clint was leant against the wall near to a car, frowning at her.

    He held his hand out and Kate realised it was for the dog’s leash, of course he was taking the dog, the dog preferred Clint, anyway.

    “I thought you’d be gon-”

    “You were gone for a while.”

    Kate was cut off by Clint, who seemed to be determined to get an answer despite not asking a question, he was still frowning, Kate gave a half shrug, “Yeah, I just, I think I needed some time, I guess.”

    This seemed to be enough for Clint who was still staring at her, as though lost in the past, “I’m guessing you should probably go, you can probably still make it in time for Christmas.” “We .”

    Kate paused, confused, “Oh yeah, I meant like you and Lucky, I just-”

    “And you .”

    He spoke slowly as though worried she’d withstood some serious brain damage or something, she tilted her head to the side, not disproving that theory.

    “What? Do you think I’d leave you alone on Christmas? While you’ve got a concussion, and who knows what else? Nah, you’re coming with.”

    She opened her mouth to protest and he held up his hand.

    “Laura will literally kill me if I don’t bring you... Maybe when you’re feeling better I can help train you, the world needs more heroes, kid.”

    Kate stood wordlessly for a second, then let out an honest to god squeal, “You’re serious? You’ll train me? And I can spend Christmas at your house and-?”

    “Katie,”

    “Don’t call me that,” she said out of habit.

    Clint smirked mischievously, “Katie , go pack up, I got most of it, but you’re messy so go check make sure I haven’t left anything. If you want to come back here when you’re aunt comes back I can drive you, or… whatever.”

    Kate didn’t bother to figure out what ‘whatever’ meant, and worried if she stood there for much longer she’d talk Clint out of taking her.

    Clint shotgunned a coffee as he waited for Kate, she shouldn’t take too long, he’d grabbed anything of hers she’d left, they had already collected things from her old apartment the other day, so there shouldn’t be anything, except maybe from her old house, but he could bring her back in the new year for that. He sent a text to Laura, telling her that he’d convinced Kate to come back, and was somewhat surprised when she called him back.

    “Hey babe, I thought you’d be asleep.”

    “I wanted to make sure you convinced her. How is she? ”

    He smiled even though she couldn’t see him, “A bit injured, a concussion but I’ll keep an eye on her as we drive, I think as soon as she stops moving she’ll pass out for the drive,” he bent to stroke Lucky as he spoke, careful to keep an ear out for Kate coming down.

    “How about things with her mom? How’s she taking that?”

    Clint sighed this time, “I’m not sure. I think part of her knew something was going on, like it was inevitable. I don’t… I didn’t like her, Kate’s mom… there was just something about her.”

    “That could be your training, or it could be the fact that Kate’s how old Nicky would be.” “Hmm.” Was all Clint managed to get out, but Laura knew what the grunt meant.

    All these years later the kidnap and death of their daughter was hard to talk about, they’d both been on mission, leaving their daughter with a friend… a friend who turned out to be hydra.. Nicole Barton’s body had been found in Mozambique, she wasn’t even two yet.

    “ Babe, I know that she’s not but- ”

    “Hey Clint were these up there when you were packing- oh shit sorry you’re on the phone.” Clint turned to see Kate emerging down the stairs, a wrapped present in her hand, he sent her a quick smile, “Laur, we’re going to set off in a minute, I know what you were going to say but… yeah…yeah,” he said in answer to what Laura was saying, “I love you too, kiss the kids for me. Yeah the trackers on. I love you.”

    He hung up and turned his attention to Kate again, who was looking particularly sheepish, presumably not used to seeing people act loving, her mother had framed her fiance, and her dad had died… not really a surprise she wasn’t used to it, she was a bit like Natasha in that way.

    Kate was holding onto a box present with a bow on the front, “Never seen it before, what’s the tag say?”

    “Merry Christmas Kate Bishop, do not open these before Christmas Morning or face Santa’s wrath’... So it’s definitely from Yelena.”

    Clint could have sworn that she blushed a little, but decided not to tease her… just yet… “I guess you shouldn’t open it then.”

    “... But it’s a present-”

    “Yeah, for Christmas ,” he held out his hand for it, she sighed and handed it over, “ Thank you , get in the car, kid, I’ll put this safe and get the dog in.”

    “... So you’re fine with Yelena just sneaking into the apartment in the minutes we weren’t in it?” Asked once they were in the car.

    He shrugged, “I’m used to it from her sister. Why don’t you try get some sleep while I drive?”

    She shook her head, “I don’t think I’ll sleep for a week,” she admitted, Clint looked like he was going to say something but just shrugged again, before beginning to drive, flicking on some old soft rock within a minute.

    Minutes later Kate’s eyes were closed, and Clint drove them home.

    Christmas Present Chapter Summary Kate meets Laura and is in her new home for christmas night Chapter Notes Thank you all so much for the previous comments and kudos' they melted my heart <3 I hope yall enjoy this (Clint is very much trying not to be a dad in this chapter but when isn't he)

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate woke up alone in the parking lot of a service station, the car was silent, no Clint or Lucky, which unsettled her more than she expected it would.

    She shrugged off the blanket which was wrapped around her, she hadn’t put it around herself so it must have been Clint, or whoever kidnapped him and their dog, she leant forward staring out of the windscreen until she spotted a short man and a dog.

    “Good,” she mumbled to herself, “I’m too tired to save his ass.” Not that she would have minded really, especially when she had ruined his Christmas enough already.

    She pulled down the sun visor and stared at her shadowed face in the mirror, “Kate Bishop you are not going to outstay your welcome, or be a burden, or cause anymore trouble…” her eyes drifted to the glove compartment near her knees… where Clint had put the present before she got in…

    She looked back up at the mirror, she rolled her eyes at the reflection in front of her, “Oh, don’t look at me like that, it’s my present anyway.”

    If Kate was quick she could get it unwrapped and then wrapped again before Clint was back. She stared down at the tag again, even without her signing her name Kate knew it was just so Yelena, the humour was so her, calling Kate ‘ Kate Bishop’ , not that Kate was mentally reading it in Yelena’s accent… and voice… Maybe when Kate got back to the city she could see if Yelena was still around, Kate owed Yelena a Christmas present after all…

    She was careful as she opened the wrapping paper… or she tried to be, she had nearly succeeded when the passenger side door opened, making her rip the paper, drop it in her lap, and pull her hand back ready to punch.

    Clint purposely stood far enough away not to get punched, and was looking distinctly amused.

    “What the fuck Clint! I nearly punched you!”

    “Nah, your arms are too short. Whatcha up to Katie?”

    Kate frowned at him, it was good to see him so lighthearted, even if that meant he was teasing her, “I was about to get away with opening this and sticking it back without you realising.”

    Clint let out a snort of laughter, “Kate I have four kids, I was always gonna know”

    He plucked it from her lap and closed her door, the backdoor opened and Clint settled Lucky back into his place, then popped the present in the trunk, before getting back into the driver's seat. “We’re about two hours out, but Pizza Dog needed a whizz. Oh, and I got some drinks earlier.”

    Kate reached for the energy drink in the cup holder between them, “Thank god, I need caffeine after you scared me half to death.”

    Clint smiled but kept his eyes on the road, “Yeah, no way, that’s mine. Yours is the smoothie. I’ve never seen you drinking a Monster and I never will.”

    “You just hate fun,” Kate mumbled but uncapped the smoothie, it was the same brand and flavour she had drunk in front of Clint before… he had clearly paid attention. It was sweet… even if she wasn’t getting caffeine. “I would always open my Christmas presents early when I was a kid, and when I was a not-kid too, I guess.”

    “Not kid…. Aren’t you like ten?” Clint laughed at his own joke, then checked to see that Kate was smiling, she still hadn’t said anything about her mom, this was getting dangerously close to talking about her. “Why’d you open them early?”

    Kate shrugged, “I don’t know, I guess I was impatient, I stopped believing in Santa when I was pretty young, my… my mom didn’t want to carry on the charade with my dad gone, so the presents would be under one of the trees for like a month. I’d go down on Christmas Eve to see if there were any more added to the pile, I wanted to make sure I had all the right faces practised, y’know to make sure that I had the perfect ‘shocked and definitely not disappointed’ face.”

    Clint was quiet, his eyebrows furrowed as he looked at the road, lost in his thoughts, or trying not to show his thoughts about it, for once.

    “Hey Clint?”

    “Hmm?”

    “You don’t have to say anything, I mean you don’t have to talk about it, if you don’t want to, but the papers about your family said, well your family, but you also just said-”

    “Kate? Just say that you want to ask about my kid.”

    Kate bit her lip, “I didn’t want to be blunt about it.”

    Clint held back a sigh, not at her but at his own thoughts, “She was… she was amazing , she was called Nicole, but everyone called her Nicky, after a family friend, she was our first kid, she was so clever, and had this smile when you’d tell her off for something, she was only young, she hadn't long had her first birthday... but I could just tell she was either going to be the smartest, kindest, funniest, bravest kid….”

    Clint clenched his jaw until the road stopped looking so blurry.

    “What happened to her?”

    Clint looked over at Kate, concerned at how small her voice was, he gave her a small soft smile, “Laur and I had to work, normally we could figure it out so only the usual people were with Nicky, but this time we couldn’t swing it, so we left her with a friend, a good friend, it was meant to be for a few hours, just until Laura’s mother could get to our place in the city were we used to live.

    When her mom got there she found our friend tied up and knocked out, bleeding… and Nicky was gone. Missing. When he came round he said that someone broke in. We searched for months, we searched everywhere, eventually a SHIELD team found her, she’d been dead a while. Mozambique. She was so far from home, that was one of the hardest bits, that she was so far away from us…

    When Nat found out all the names of people in Hydra she came to me, told me that he was on the list… Sadly he was already dead..”

    “I’m so sorry Clint. I can’t even imagine… shit I shouldn’t have asked, I’m sorry.”

    “Kate, it’s fine, if I didn’t want to tell you I would have said… She would have been about your age, which is so fuckin’ scary.”

    “Because you’re old?”

    Clint let out a snort of laughter, “I’m not that old.”

    “…. Sure Clint….” She smiled at him and he smiled back, she bit her lip again, but couldn’t hold back, “Are they sure it’s her?”

    “They did tests.”

    “But were the tests done by Hydra scientists though?”

    Clint sighed, “...I don’t know, they were run by several different teams, it’s very unlikely they were solely Hydra, and there were no reports of any other girls missing who were her age and looked like her anywhere in the world, we made sure… that with the tests were pretty conclusive.”

    “I’m sorry.”

    “It’s okay Kate, it was a long time ago now, a whole other lifetime.”

    “Woah! Is this it?”

    Clint tore his eyes away from the dirt road to Kate. He had seen the museum Kate had grown up in, in comparison the farmhouse was practically a hovel, he hadn’t known how she’d react to the place, but he should have known with Kate it was going to be awe.

    “Yeah, we started to build it nearly twenty years ago, it was finished when Coop was a toddler, but we update it nearly all the time. I updated the kitchen before I took the kids to the city, but Laura always finds new things for me to do... or destroy,” he finished with a slight laugh, keeping an eye on the road so he didn’t hit any of the kids things.

    “You really love her, don’t you?”

    He looked back at Kate, tilting his head to the side slightly, “Of course I do, she’s my wife.” Kate smiled sadly, “Looks like someone’s still up,” she nodded her head to the bottom floor where a light was on, and a human shaped shadow stood watching.

    “Yeah, that’ll be Laur, I did tell her not to stay up, but she clearly wanted to meet you.” Clint parked the car next to the basketball hoop, his hand went to his door when Kate grabbed his other arm.

    “What’s up kid?” He knew no one was around who shouldn’t be, he’d been checking to make sure, out of habit, but Kate looked terrified.

    “What if… she’s not gonna like… hate me? Is she?”

    Clint laughed, but then saw Kate was serious, “Kate she wants to meet you, she would have made me drive back to get you if I turned up alone. She gets why I stayed, and staying was my choice. My friend once built an evil robot who tried to destroy the world and he was still given a bed here. Now c’mon, if you don’t get to bed Santa won’t come.”

    “I’m not seven, Clint.”

    Clint didn’t bother to disagree, instead he just opened the door and left the car.

    Kate took a few breaths as she heard Clint grab their stuff from the trunk. She forced herself out, leashed Lucky, and got him out the car, then stood hidden by the car, but peering through the windows at Laura in the house.

    “You coming?”

    “Yeah. Come on Lucky. Are you sure this is okay?”

    Clint let out a loud laugh, “C’mon Katie.”

    “You made it!”

    Clint walked over to his wife who was dressed in her pyjamas, family themed of course, and wrapped her in an all-encompassing hug, “Told ya I would. I missed you.”

    “I missed you more”

    Clint heard the excited bark of Lucky and turned in time to see Kate being dragged inside by the dog, he smiled at the sight, he held his arm out in introduction, “Here’s the strays I brought with me, this is Lucky, and that's Kate.”

    Laura moved away from Clint and over to Kate, wrapping her too in a hug, “It’s so nice to finally meet you, Clint’s told me all about you.”

    Kate’s face turned to panic, but she tried to play it cool, “Yeah he’s probably lied about a whole lot, I’d ignore it, I’m Kate, but you know that, hi, this is nice,” she knew she was rambling and should shut up, especially about the hug, but the hug was nice.

    Laura moved back to look at Kate, taking in her injuries, “How’re you feeling, does your head hurt at all?”

    “Oh, erm, I’ll be okay, thank you though. And thank you for having me here.”

    Laura smiled at her, “You’re more than welcome here, and I’m getting you some Advil, you’ll be aching in the morning, it should help a little.”

    She turned to face her husband, who was watching them with a curious expression on his face, she knew everything about him, but she wasn’t familiar with that face, “Clint,” she called, breaking him from his trace, “honey, do you want to show her to the blue guest room?”

    “Sure, c’mon kid, Lucky can sleep in your room if you like?”

    “Yeah, er, thanks, hi again Mrs er-” she saw the look Laura was giving her and gave her a sheepish smile, “Laura? C’mon Pizza Dog.”

    Clint led Kate to one of their several guest rooms, Laura had chosen the biggest for Kate, and by the looks of it the kids had put up Christmas decorations for her, Clint smiled as he spotted the dog bed on the floor. Of course Laura had known that Clint would end up bringing them both home with him.

    “Woah, this is so nice.”

    “I know it’s not as big as the one back in the museum.”

    “I like this one more, and not just because it doesn’t have my mom, this one is more homely, kinda like my apartment, I mean also not at all because no pizza smell and-”

    “Hey honey, here’s the tablets,” Laura announced as she walked in, handing Kate two small pills and a bottle of water, “Oh and I wasn’t sure how many of your clothes survived your apartment blowing up, I found some for you, but after the holidays we can get you more.”

    “That’s… that’s so kind of you, thank you so much, I… thank you, for everything,” Kate took the medicine and looked away as she tried not to cry.

    Laura said goodnight to them and Clint busied himself separating his and Kate’s clothes from the bag he had packed hours earlier.

    He stood up again as Kate was absentmindedly running her fingers across the new clothes, “You doin’ okay Katie?”

    Kate’s head snapped up, her eyes sparkling, but she nodded, “Yeah, yeah, thanks, I just…

    Your wife is so nice, and so are you, letting me stay with you, I promise it’ll just be for Christmas-”

    “-Kate, you don’t need to worry about that right now,” He closed the gap and pulled her into a hug, “Tonight was a lot, but I’m so proud of you, I meant it when I said it. Get some sleep, kid, I’m sure the kiddos will be wanting to wake you up at the crack of dawn.” He pulled back a little, “You gonna be okay in here?”

    Kate smiled, “Yeah, I’m all good, thanks Clint.”

    “Our room’s at the end of the hall if you need us.”

    Kate nodded.

    “Okay night Kate.”

    Kate watched as he began to walk out, only for him to turn back when he reached the door. “No sneaking out to open your presents.”

    Kate rolled her eyes then stopped, “Wait, presents? Multiple? I’m getting presents?”

    Clint gave her a knowing smile, tapped the side of his nose, "Merry Christmas Katie," he nodded at the bag he left for her, then left.

    Kate looked over at Lucky, who had made himself at home in the cushioned dog bed, Kate followed the dog’s lead and got ready for bed: truthfully she could pass out where she stood, but she changed into the Christmas pj’s Laura had found her and downed some water.

    She was about to climb into bed when she thought about Clint’s nod to the bag, she walked over to it and rooted through.

    She smiled as her hands came into contact with the wrapping paper she had torn earlier.

    She hooked it out of the bag and dropped onto the bed with it.

    This time she ripped the paper off, opened the box and laughed.

    The box contained a staggering amount of candy, a pair of fuzzy socks which looked like Lucky (Yelena had even taken off one of the eyes), another smaller box, and most importantly a whole set of cutlery.

    Lucky watched her laugh, “I think Yelena’s invited herself around for dinner,” she explained to the dog, picking up the smaller box while looking at the dog, Lucky let out a sigh and led back down, as though done with Kate’s drama.

    Kate was barely paying attention as she opened the smaller box, there was a file, with a sticky note on the front, ‘My phone number is in your phone when you need my help.’

    Kate didn’t understand as she flicked through the file. The papers were official looking, with some older sticky notes in Eleanor Bishop’s handwriting, and photographs of Kate, going back to a very young Kate, but she had never seen that photograph before, she didn’t have a lot of photos when she was younger, something about mould in a cellar or something…

    “What the fu…” she mumbled…

    Certificate of Adoption.

    ‘This is to certify that Katherine Elizabeth Bishop born on March 15th 2002 has been adopted into the Bishop family on the 21st of May 2004 by’

    Kate couldn’t read anymore, she couldn’t take it in, her brain was a storm brewing, she grabbed her phone pressed on the number which she hadn’t put in there, glaring at the ‘yelena :)’ name.

    ‘Where the fuck are you?’

    She stared at it, waiting for the dots to come up, but no response came.

    Kate was no longer tired, she was wide awake, she sat back up and poured over the various documents and photographs, all of which focused on her .

    Chapter Notes Yooo i wasn't well yesterday so heres a double length chapter today :)

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate must have fallen asleep at some point because she heard some voices and shot up in the bed, knocking papers everywhere, and photos fell off the bed onto the wooden floorboards. She had only just gotten used to waking up in the safehouse, so it took her a few minutes to realise that she was at Clint’s home, his real home, not the one they had made over the past week.

    The room was pretty simple, a metal framed double bed, pale blue walls, a white desk, bedside table, and a wardrobe, all a little rustic, which Kate thought suited the room.

    “Kate?”

    Kate knew Clint’s voice off by heart now, her eyes flickered to Yelena’s ‘gift’... she was so not ready to deal with this, or talk to Clint about it, she didn’t think he’d ever admit it but they were a lot alike, and she knew that if the situations were reversed she’d push until Clint snapped, in one way or another.

    “Y-Yeah one second.”

    She pushed everything under her rumbled pillow, and kicked a small passport photo underneath the bed.

    She rushed to the door which Lucky was whining at, the dog jumped up at Clint, who was wearing Christmas pjs and a Santa hat, he greeted the dog with a massive smile.

    “That’s just favouritism,” she grumbled, yawning as she rang her hand through her hair, wincing as her finger brushed the bump on her head.

    “It’s ‘cause I got him T-R-E-A-T-S, instead of just feeding him pizza.”

    Kate pulled a face, “How’s he supposed to live up to the name Pizza Dog without eating pizza.”

    Clint shook his head ducking his head to try and hide his smile, when he looked back up he evaluated her with his trained eyes; her eyes were rimmed with dark circles as though she hadn’t slept, her cuts were still covered by steri-strips, but a couple had a little dried blood, Clint figured she’d been tossing and turning, if she slept at all. She was in some Christmas pyjamas, similar to the ones the rest of the family were wearing, god he loved Laura for that. “How’re you feeling, kid?”

    “Er,” she crouched down to give the dog a fuss instead of looking at him, “I’m okay, a little sore. Laura was right, those Advil helped, I think.”

    Clint looked down at her, waiting for more, but that was it. He knew he was overreacting, but there was just something a little… off…, “That all? I don’t get a whole dream analysis from you?” He teased, watching as she stood up, “You sure you’re okay?”

    “Yeah, just tired. Is everyone up?”

    “Yeah, the kids were up maybe an hour ago, I’ve had to stop all of them from waking you up about eight times, but I figured I should check on you.”

    Kate smiled at him, “Aww you missed me.”

    “No, I had just got used to you buzzing in my ear all hours of the day.”

    “Nah you missed me,” she teased, her eyes flicking over his shoulder towards the stairs.

    “You ready to meet everyone?”

    “Y-yea- I mean… they’re not gonna like… hate me?”

    Clint tilted his head, “Why do you think everyone’s going to hate you?”

    “I kept their dad from them at Christmas while he was meant to be spending quality time with them.” She gave him a ‘are you dumb’ look.

    “You got me back to them for Christmas, that’s all they care about, that and you brought a dog with you.” He assured her, “They already love you and they haven’t met you yet.

    Kate didn’t quite believe him but she followed him to the living room, making sure that the guest bedroom door was closed, just in case anyone stumbled upon the file, Clint talked as they walked but Kate didn’t take anything in, she was about to ask what he’d said when they got to the bottom of the stairs, when she was attacked.

    Okay perhaps she wasn’t technically ‘attacked’, but Nate’s arms around her was a shock, that he was hugging her was a shock.

    “Er, hi, child,” she awkwardly patted his head twice, blushing when she saw Laura holding back a laugh, and Clint sniggering behind her.

    “He doesn’t bite,” he teased in a whisper.

    Kate tried to ignore the world's most annoying Avenger.

    “You brought home a dog!” Nate practically shouted, “That’s so cool!”

    Kate smiled at Nate after he unwrapped his arms and looked up at her adoringly, his hand stroking the dog’s golden fur, “Yeah he’s pretty cool, he’s Lucky, it’s his name, and he is just, lucky,” she finished somewhat awkwardly.

    “Is he called lucky because you rescued him from the road?”

    “How do you know that?” Clint asked disapprovingly.

    “YouTube, dad,” the girl shrugged before smiling at Kate, giving her a look which showed her amusement at how Clint tried to shield her still, “Hi I’m Lila, you’re an archer too right? It’s so cool, maybe we can practice together?”

    Kate smiled at her, more used to people who were older than Nate, Lila was only a few years younger than her, she supposed if Lila hadn’t been dusted she’d be even closer in age, “That sounds like fun, you’ll be way more fun to practice with than your dad.”

    There was a laugh from Lila and a snort from the older teenager.

    “Hey, I’m Cooper, I’m sorry you had to put up with my dad’s snoring and his cooking.”

    Kate let out a laugh, “Yeah he has a thing about making breakfast food doesn’t he? That’s why me and Pizza Dog lived off pizza.”

    “How did this turn into a roast for me?” Clint playfully whined, “I make great breakfasts,” he heaved his youngest into his arms, “don’t I Nate the Great?”

    “I like pizza more.”

    “Traitor,” Nate let out giggled as Clint tickled him.

    “Breakfast is the only thing he knows how to make,” Laura smiled at Kate as warmly as she had the night before, her hand reached out, gently brushing the worst of her cuts, “How’re you doing?”

    “I’m okay, good, thank you, and thank you so much for the pyjamas, and the other bits, it was very kind of you.”

    “Its was nothing,” Laura brushed off, she gestured to her children to head back to the couches and none of them protested, Clint settled into an armchair and pulled Nate onto his lap, “We thought you should sleep in a little,” she placed her hand on Kate’s back as she slowly led her to where the rest of the Bartons were gathered, “the kids opened some of their presents from Santa but there’s still presents from Clint and I to give-”

    “-Oh I, I don’t want to intur-”

    “ Kate, we need you here so we can give you yours too.”

    Kate halted, looking into Laura’s brown eyes which were so full of love… unlike her own mother’s… or not mother… fuck.

    “You- you really didn’t have to,” she stuttered, “I mean I didn’t, couldn’t get you guys any, and you’ve already welcomed me into your home and-”

    “-Honey,” Laura cut her off, “you got Clint home to us, and you’re more than welcome here, we want you to be here. And it’s Christmas, of course we got you presents.”

    “C’mon you two,” Clint hollered over to them, “we wanna open presents, hurry up!”

    “He’s worse than the kids,” Laura chuckled, it was enough to get Kate moving.

    Kate was shocked by all the presents she got; including a couple of hoodies, a pair of boots which were perfect for the snow covered yard outside, some gloves and arm guards for when she was doing archery, more chocolate, a couple more pieces of clothes, a lavender beanie, more pyjamas (which Kate was planning on living in), and a few books.

    She cradled a mug of coffee as she watched the kids unwrapping theirs, and smiled and chatted to all of them as they showed them all the things they got, Lila and Cooper saying ‘Santa’ while giving Kate a look to show that they were doing it for Nate’s benefit.

    Kate subtly slipped her phone out of her pocket as it buzzed.

    ‘hello Kate Bishop :) i am in new york city still i wanted to see the big tree but it was knocked dwn by someone…

    i am guessing you would like to talk? :)’

    Kate was going to kill Yelena.

    ‘ Cut the bullshit. Tell me it’s not real.’

    ‘why would i lie? it is real. congratulations you are not the daughter of that criminal!

    *officecelebration.gif*

    ‘Wait, Kate Bishop, are you not happy about this?’

    Kate forced herself not to growl at her phone.

    ‘Of course I’m not happy about it!

    Everything in my life is completely fucked up!

    Literally everything I thought I knew was a lie .

    You have no idea what it’s like to have something as big as that turn out to be a lie. ’

    Kate clenched her teeth as she saw the dots appear, then disappear.

    “Last presents you guys,” Clint announced, “I know I was late… so….” he handed out the gift Kate had seen him carrying from the car the night before, “Coop, Lila… Nate…” he counted off as he handed out the presents, “My lovely wife who puts up with so much and is the love of my life.”

    “Gross, get a room!” Lila hid her face behind her boxed present.

    “No don’t, three kids is enough .”

    Kate snorted at Cooper’s joking complaint, but even she ducked her head “And most importantly… Lucky.” Clint handed the dog’s present to Kate, he felt bad when her face lit up, she really wasn’t expecting anything more, he passed her a small envelope,”Like I’d forget you.”

    “Wha-,” she cut herself off from protesting knowing that Clint and Laura would once again stop her, “Woah, thanks Clint.”

    She dropped her phone into her lap so she could focus on the presents, it wasn’t like she could kill Yelena from far away, or even get a reply. She couldn’t stare at her phone or Clint and probably Laura would cotton on to something being up, she was determined to not ruin all of Christmas for the Bartons… not until she knew all the facts anyway.

    “Hey Lucky, lets see whatcha got,” Kate bent down in her seat to show Lucky the present, “You don’t have opposable thumbs so I’ll help you,” she told the dog as she listened to the Bartons opening their gifts.

    She beamed as she opened the gift, it was a new collar for Lucky, it was light purple with pizzas dotted around it, and a pizza shaped name tag proclaiming ‘ Lucky: Pizza Dog’ .

    “Wait, where did you get this? We were together for most of the time!”

    Clint shrugged, “I couldn’t sleep and New York has everything,” he nodded at the envelope in Kate’s lap as he opened Nate’s new toy.

    Kate opened the envelope to find a handmade card token.

    This token gives Katherine Bishop the power to give Clint F Barton One (1) heart attack without him complaining *terms and conditions apply*.

    Kate let out a laugh.

    “What did you get?” Lila asked, bending across and smiling with the token, mentally adding it to her next Christmas list.

    Cooper did the same, laughing at it when he saw it.

    “What is it? What is it?” Nate bounced.

    Kate looked at the youngest Barton, “I can annoy your daddy a little bit without him shouting at me.”

    “Aw man I want one of those!”

    “Clint,” Laura chastised gently, trying not to smile, “Please tell me that’s not all you got her.” “Oh no, Laura, this is, this is great, seriously, wait -,” she looked at Clint, “does this include me jumping off of buildings?”

    “ Who jumped off a building?!” Laura’s eyes bored into her husband’s, as though it was his fault.

    “Oh no, no!” Kate shot in before Clint could answer, “I didn’t really jump off a building, that would be so dumb, I wouldn’t do that, I more fell off-.”

    Clint through Kate grimaced, “Sorry,” she mouthed at Clint.

    “Kate?”

    “Yeah Nate?” Kate tried not to look scared of the child.

    “Will you build a Lego city with me? Please? I got loads of new Legos from Santa.

    Pleaaaase.”

    “Er,” Kate bit her lip, “yeah, I can try, it’s been a while since I played with them,” she tried to hide her fear as the child took her hand, she tried to send a ‘help I don’t want to break him’ look but he was too busy being in trouble.

    “Hey, can I help too, Nate?”

    Kate breathed a sigh of relief, and sent a grateful look to Lila.

    “Okay,” Nate sighed, keeping hold of Kate’s hand, and moving to stand between Kate and Lila, “but you can’t hog Kate, I want to play with her too.”

    “Dude she’s a human,” Cooper reminded him, “and I’ll help too, I’m an expert at Lego cities.”

    Nate looked at his brother and sister then at Kate, biting his lip.

    Kate bit her lip then sent him a conspiratorial look, “Maybe me and you can work on a superhero tower?”

    This placated Nate, who beamed and pulled her over to a clear space, with Cooper and Lila sitting on either side of Kate and Nate.

    The Barton Christmas was somehow busy and chill at the same time. Kate managed to relax while around Nate, who seemed to want to be around her all the time.

    Kate with the Barton children made a whole Lego city, towards lunch Clint switched with Cooper, the teenager going to help his mom in the kitchen, and Clint showing off with his illegal building techniques.

    The lunch seemed to have every food Kate could think of, and enough to feed a small army; ham, veggies, salads, chick nuggets, waffle fries, lasagna, meatballs, various breads, even a pizza.

    Kate grabbed a slice, and held it to her side, pretending that Lucky was definitely not eating the pizza.

    Clint sent Kate a look, but there was amusement on his face which broke into a whole smile as Kate held her now empty hands up to protest her innocence.

    Back in the Bishop household their Christmas dinners were about eight courses, all of them cost enough that they could feed hundreds of people, they were very much just for looks, and their dinners were typically quiet, compared to the joking rowdiness of the Barton family.

    The rest of their Christmas consisted of pulling crackers and playing games, eating buffet food for dinner, and drinking hot cocoa while watching The Polar Express.

    Kate was nearly asleep while watching another Christmas movie, when Clint nudged her with his shoulder and gestured for her to follow him outside.

    “Can you help me with somethin’ real quick?” he asked, and of course Kate agreed, because this was Clint .

    Watching the Ronin suit burn was… weird.

    It was good, of course it was, it marked Clint’s darkest period, both in what he had lost and what he had become. And the suit had caused both Clint and Kate a lot of trouble when they were back, but it had also brought Clint into Kate’s life, and the rest of the Barton family, and had brought her the truth, which was hard, but Kate knew that it was right, to have the truth. “So…,” she asked largely to get out of her own thoughts, “Should I say a few words?”

    Clint let out a bark of laughter, and wrapped his arm around Kate’s shoulders, “Nah, let’s get back inside, we can have one more mug of hot chocolate before bed, if you can stay up that long.”

    Kate rolled her eyes but kept his arm around her, “I couldn’t sleep last night, I’m fine.” She sucked in a breath as she stared as the snow blanketed ground, “Can I ask you a question”

    “That was a question.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, fondly this time. Clint was an avenger, a shield agent… he would know what to do next about her mom, her not-mom she guessed…. But if she was going to be Hawkeye one day she would have to learn how to do it on her own.

    “What’s up, Katie?”

    She sucked in another breath….

    “What do you think of "Lady Hawk"?” She forced out, refusing to ask for his help just yet. “Yeah, that's terrible. “ "Hawk Eve"?

    “No, that's worse. “ “Damn, okay. Hawk Shot. Like "hot shot". But you know, "Hawk". “‘ “No.”

    “"Lady Arrow"? None of those. “ “Okay, fine.” She sighed, he was right, not that she’s let on about that, but he had told her he’d train her, she needed a name to become a hero like her.

    “Hawkeye sounds pretty good, we could always just share the name.”

    Her face lit up.

    An hour or so later Kate sleepily made her way up the stairs to the guest room, leaving only Clint and Laura downstairs, the rest of the kids had all made their ways up to bed, Cooper sleepily talking to someone on his phone, Lila had been gently shook awake by Laura and sent up, and Nate had been carried up.

    “Night guys,” Kate called to them, “Thanks for today.”

    “Night kid,” Clint yawned.

    “Thank you for being here, we all really enjoyed it, honey,” Laura told her. “Sleep tight.” “Don’t let the bed bugs-”

    “-bite,” Kate finished off Clint’s sentence, “You’re such a dad.”

    Laura looked at her husband who was still watching Kate go up the stairs, as he did whenever their kids would sleepily go up or down them, Cooper’s fault for falling down them when sleepily walking when he was six and breaking his arm, Laura led her head down on her husband’s shoulder, his arm going around her.

    Kate smiled as she walked down the landing and pushed open her door.

    “What’s on your mind?” Clint asked her, his eyes on the tv flickering a Christmas movie (Die Hard) in front of them.

    Laura shook her head slightly, “Just that I’m glad you brought Kate home, another stray to add to your tally.”

    Clint rolled his eyes, “There’s only three; Natasha, Wanda, and not Kate.”

    “And Lucky,” Laura teased, her eyes darting to the dog stretched out in front of the fire. “Lucky’s the best one outta them,” Clint teased, pressing a kiss to his wife’s cheek. “Was that all you were thinking about?”

    “Yeah…. And that Kate is a lot like you, y’know? Just how she does some things, her mannerisms, and her eyes, those are unusual, blue eyes with dark hair… I guess you rubbed off on her while you were together, it’s good, I’m glad you had someone with you, and I think she was lucky to get you too.”

    Clint hummed slightly, he had been thinking about her eyes since he’d met her in the alley, it’s what had stopped him, originally, before he realised that she was a kid (despite her claiming that 20 was not a kid), they were so like…. They were so unusual, that’s all, they were usual, but not an impossible combination.

    “I think she was already like that, the mannerisms, she talks a lot more than me when she’s nervous though… She’s a good kid, Laur.”

    “I know,” she replied softly.

    “She doesn’t realise how young she is, she’s insistent she’s some adult, which is barely true,” he shook his head, “She’s…” he cut himself off, unable to tell Laura how like her Kate looked, and acted, not to be mean, but to be kind, both of them were clearly thinking about Nicky, it felt too much to say how alike they were. “She likes you, she teases you a lot less than she teases me.”

    “She teases you because she likes you, she’s comfortable with you.”

    “She threw a sprout at me!”

    Laura laughed, “Yeah: I really like her.”

    Kate threw her hand over her mouth as a silhouette moved towards her from the window.

    She quickly raised her hand ready to protect herself, her bow was under the bed, she kicked the door closed, she knew that the Barton kids were all asleep upstairs, she kept herself like a guard in front of the door. She would protect them.

    “Hello Kate Bishop,” Yelena Belova smiled, “I am hungry, do you have any candy?”

    Kate dropped her hands, rushing over to the Russian assassin, “What the fuck Yelena!” She hissed a whisper, “Wait, are you in a Christmas sweater? Wait, that doesn’t matter, how did you sneak into Clint farm?”

    “It is Christmas, no? I have seen in many television shows and movies the ugly Christmas sweaters, this has a llama, I’m not sure what it had to do with Christmas, but it is cute, no?” “Yes you look cute,” Kate snapped, “ how did you get on Clint’s farm ?”

    “Oh, that was easy, my sister helped him with a lot of the protections, I do everything she does, did , but I do it better, and as you pointed out, cuter .”

    Kate blushed furiously, she walked over and dropped onto the bed, with a loud sigh, Yelena cautiously copied her, “Yelena I need you to tell me the truth… Was I really adopted?”

    Yelena nodded, “Yes, you were. When I told you this earlier you said I didn’t understand what it was like to realise your life was a lie… I very much do, Kate, I grew up in the Red Room, but before that I lived in Ohio, with my sister, and my parents, they were not really my family, none of them told me… then I was thrown back into the red room to become an assassin.” She told her matter of factly, “I am…. I apologise, I thought you would like to learn this news in private, that is why I hid it in your Christmas present. I thought if I simply told you then you would not have believed me. I thought you would like to know that you were not like her, I saw enough of her to think that would be a good thing,” she spoke quietly, tilting her head as she looked into Kate’s blue eyes.

    Kate shifted on the bed, her toes wiggling closer to the assassin. “How did you even find it out?” Kate folded her arms over her chest, “I mean… couldn’t you have told me about it when you were making us mac and cheese?”

    “I did not know about it then. Would you like some more macaroni and cheese? I am sure that there is some here, it is American staple, no?”

    “No it’s, okay yes it is, but I’m not hungry. I want to know how the hell you found this out.” “The files were in your mother’s safe,” Yelena rooted through the box of candy, pulling out a pixy stix, and pouring it into her mouth, with a big smile. “She kept them safe, I did research as well as I could, to see if they were a prank of some kind. There was no trace of you in the New York foster system, or states around it, but there was no sign of Eleanor or Derek Bishop having any biological children. There is no real sign of you.”

    “But I have like, I have a social security number, I’ve been on planes, I’ve been, I’m in college, I- how could I not exist?” Yelena took Kate’s hands, Kate was surprised to find that they were warm.

    “ You exist. You are right here, Kate Bishop, you exist . You are technically in the system, but you were placed in there by an outside source; by a person relating to Wilson Fisk’s and to Hydra, the people my sister took down. I am still trying to find out more information, however it is Christmas, there are less people to ask .”

    “I’m going to help you. We can work together, two heads are better than one, and we’re not threatening anyone, got it?”

    “But where is the fun in that, Kate Bishop?”

    Kate rolled her eyes, but a smile came on her face, which mirrored Yelena's. “Where are you staying? Do you have a place nearby? Or I’m sure if you asked Clint and Laura they’d let you stay, your sister stayed here a lot.”

    Yelena tilted her head, “Are you going to tell Clint Barton about your parentage?”

    “I, er, I don’t know. I mean he could help, I guess… I don’t know… Do you think that I should?”

    “Hmm, strategically it could be helpful, he has experience inside Shield, and he did track down many people to kill while we were all gone.”

    “... I didn’t go… I was still here, the world was…” Kate shook her head, “Clint shouldn’t have done all the things he did, but I get it, the world was… it was a mess,” Kate wrapped a blanket around herself, as though protecting herself from how cruel some people became during that time. “What if Clint looks at me differently? I don’t want him pitying me.”

    Yelena took in a slow breath, “Clint Barton is… he is not bad.”

    “That’s an improvement on you trying to kill him.”

    Yelena smiled softly at Kate’s teasing, “You helped to change my mind, Kate Bishop, after all you like me, so you must have exceptional taste.”

    Kate smiled, looking down at their hands, which were still together, she didn’t pull her hand away, “I think I’ll tell him, I’ll tell him soon, I mean he welcomed me into his home, I shouldn’t keep this from him, he asked me if I was okay like thirty times today already.” “And are you okay, Kate Bishop?”

    Kate opened her mouth to answer when a knock at the door came.

    “Hey Katie? Lucky’s been whining to come in,” Clint called as he opened the door, expecting to find Kate snoring on the bed…

    He started when he saw Kate on the bed, sat up, holding hands with a trained black widow. Yelena, he tried to remind himself, but his shoulders still squared. “Yelena? Kid?”

    Yelena dropped Kate’s hand, “I shall go make macaroni,” she sent Kate a look, “Yes?”

    Kate bit her lip hard and nodded, “Yes.”

    Clint turned back to face Kate, his eyes flying over her to make sure there were no new injuries, then just raised his eyebrows.

    “I, I’m really really sorry Clint, I did not know that she was going to just, just turn up, I would have told her not to come, but she didn’t respond to my texts,” she ran her hand through her hair, trying to hold herself together.

    “Kate, it’s, it’s okay, I mean I would have appreciated a heads up, but I believe you that she just turned up. It’s something Nat would have done, I guess they’re more alike than I realised.”

    Kate sent him a tiny smile of appreciation, then looked down at her lap, squeezing her eyes shut trying to find the words to talk to Clint, her eyes opened, flicked to the pillow where the file lay hidden, then back at her lap.

    “Katie?” Clint called softly.

    Kate looked up, Clint had walked so the door was closed over and he was leant casually against the desk, his arms crossed over his chest, his ankles crossed over each other. He was really the perfect picture of casual interest… or he would have, had he not been frowning slightly, with eyes full of concern.

    Kate immediately felt bad, she shook her head slightly, “Sorry, it’s nothing, I can deal with it-”

    “ Kate ,” Clint’s voice was slightly sterner now, “Stop trying to take the world on by yourself. What’s going on kiddo?”

    Kate swallowed, the sound of it filled the silent room, “Yelena went to check things out on my mom, Eleanor, I think she was just trying to make sure I’d be okay? That no one was going to come after me, or whatever.”

    Clint nodded slowly, encouraging her to go on as gently as possible; secretly relieved that Yelena had not caused Kate to be in such a state.

    “She found some things, well a thing…. a file.” she paused for a second, before slipping her hand beneath her pillow, she listened to the wooden floor creak and the bed dip next to her, she slowly pulled out the file. This was going to change everything, she just knew it. But she couldn’t hide it now.

    She held it out to him, and he took it, staring at her still, “I… I’m adopted? Kind of.”

    Clint’s composure broke, allowing a look of surprise to show on his face, “Kind of?”

    “Yeah, erm, there’s loads of documents there, I don’t even know what half of it is, but…

    it’s… Yelena looked, Clint, I wasn’t in any system, I’m not,” her voice cracked but she persevered, “I’m not who I am. She… They lied to me,” she gritted her teeth as a tear sprung up in her eye, just one small tear, she could will it away.

    But Clint’s arms wrapped around her in a second, and Kate’s composure fell for the first time since she had learnt that everything was a lie.

    More tears joined the first, and soon she had wrapped her arms around Clint in return, her fingers holding onto his holiday sweater. He smelt how he always did, better than when they’d been in New York, but it was just Clint , he smelt familiar, as solid as a rock, nothing would change.

    He didn’t tell her it would be okay, and she appreciated that, he wasn’t about to lie to her, not when he didn’t know the details, and it wasn’t something a bandaid would fix, he just held her, and Kate allowed herself to be held.

    Eventually she was all cried out, she pulled away, wiping her face and nose on her sleeve, and let out a sheepish laugh which was boarding on embarrassed, “Sorry, I just, I feel so stupid I mean I should have, I should have known, like she never was that attached to me, even though she should have been, like she probably paid for me,” her hands moved as she talked, as she said ‘paid’ her hands moved too much, flicking the file off of Clint’s lap. “Shit, sor-”

    “Kate it’s fine, I’ve got it,” he slipped off the bed, shoving papers and photos from the floor, and one small photo from under the bed into the file, “and quit sayin’ that word.”

    Kate blushed a little, “Sor-”

    “I meant ‘sorry’, as long as you don’t swear around Nate and Lila then you can swear, I do it all the time, but you don’t have to apologise for everything. This is a lot,” he moved so he was crouching on the floor beside the bed, holding onto the file, “I wouldn’t be taking this so well.”

    “You and Laura both figured out something was going on today so I’m not taking it that well.”

    “I figured it was about your, about Eleanor gettin’ sent to jail, and taking on the Kingpin all on your own, I didn’t think anything like this was happening. I would have been taking this way worse.”

    Kate sent him a look.

    Clint rolled his eyes with a small smile, “Okay, okay… so if I found it out about my dad I would’a probably been celebrating,” Kate let out a snort of laughter and Clint smiled, “but I would have still have been showing that something had happened.”

    Kate smiled at him, having guessed that how Clint acted around his kids was a conscious decision, her smile turned sad, slowly. “I thought… I thought my dad was the best person ever, I didn’t think he ever did anything wrong, but even now, knowing he hid this from me too, that he got my mom, my whatever involved in the tracksuit mafia, that he wasn’t a good guy, I mean how could he be when he was involved with all of those… Clint, I want to hate him. I should hate him.” Lucky whined at them from the floor, and Kate wiped a stray tear from her cheek.

    Clint shook his head, he hadn’t looked away from Kate, “No, Katie, you shouldn’t hate him, should feel however you feel, there’s not shoulda coulda woulda here. And the reason you don’t hate him is because you’re the most caring kid I’ve met, you care about everyone you meet, you walked into traffic to rescue a mutt you’d never seen before, then after getting your ass whooped still took him on a walk, because you put others first. That’s a good thing, that's the thing which makes you a hero, not the branding, but what you said when we first met: you wear your heart of your sleeve, you’re sincere, that’s what I missed when I become Ronin kid, so don’t try and squash that heart of yours.”

    “You’re a lot nicer to me now than when we first met,” she tried to joke.

    “You’re less of a pain in the ass, and it’s Christmas, I’ll start again tomorrow,” he shot back, reaching to squeeze her hand.

    “Clint… I don’t know what I should do now.”

    “What does your heart tell you?”

    “It tells me to find out who my birth parents are, and what the fuck happened to me.”

    Clint nodded once, strong and determined, “Then that’s what we’ll do. Together. I have some contacts, and I know people who have contacts, we’ll find them. Are you okay with me telling Laura all this? I don’t want to keep secrets from my wife, but if you want to keep it between us then I get that.”

    Kate shook her head, “No, no Laura can know, of course. And I think Yelena might need somewhere to stay, she wants to help, if it’s okay with you?”

    Clint smiled knowingly, “I guarantee that Laura is both feeding her and encouraging her to spend the night, or three. But she’s not getting added to my strays tally, she’s on yours.” “But I’m on yours, so Lucky and Yelena are on like a sub-tally of yours, and Yelena will kinda be on her sister’s so she counts double, so you’ve got even more on your tally.”

    Clint rolled his eyes, letting out a groan as though wounded, “It must have struck midnight, you’re back to being a pain in the ass!”

    He smiled at Kate as she laughed, his eyes scanned her, noting everything- how her eyes were red from crying but that was fading, and how her eyes were no longer water but back to looking sleepy, there was a little sadness in the blue eyes, but a genuine, if small, smile on her face, her breathing was levelled out again.

    Kate was going to be okay, at least for tonight, Clint could tell.

    And yet, he still asked; “You gonna be okay? I can stay a bit longer if you like?”

    Kate smiled at him and shook her head, “I’m… I’m good.”

    “Good.” Clint told her, standing. He lifted the stuffed files, “I’ll go through these, and talk to Laur… if you’re good?”

    “I’m good.”

    “Good,” Clint nodded, walking back towards the door, “Good, yeah, good.”

    “Good….” Kate said again, before letting out a laugh and shaking her head, “This is awkward right?”

    “Yes this is very awkward,” came a voice from behind the closed door.

    Kate bit her lip as Clint rolled his eyes and pulled open the door, trying not to laugh as Yelena appeared on the other side, grinning, with a bowl in her hands.

    “You are done now, yes?”

    Clint looked to Kate who gave him a small nod.

    “Yeah, we’re done,” he turned to Yelena, smiling, “did Laura tell you what room you’ll be staying in?”

    “Yes, the room next to this one. Your wife is very nice, very much out of your league, congratulations on getting her to marry you.”

    Clint rolled his eyes again, “Thanks Yelena. Right, I’m gonna go see Laura, try get some sleep,” his eyes flickered to Kate, trying his best to believe her when she said she’d be okay, he looked away quickly, “both of you. Goodnight.”

    “Night Clint,” Kate smiled, watching him until he left, pulling the “Goodnight, Clinton.”

    “Wait, his name is Clinton?!”

    “You’re not calling me that, Kate,” Clint called through the closed door.

    “I made some macaroni and cheese, Kate Bishop would you like some?”

    Kate shook her head, moving underneath the duvet and quilt on the bed, and led down, she pulled the covers up so that only the top of her head was peeking out..

    “Oh… of course, you are very tired, I shall leave you to sleep,” Yelena turned to leave.

    “Wait,” Kate’s word stopped her from leaving, the Russian turned and looked at her, tilting her head slightly to the side, “You could stay, y’know if you like, until you’re finished eating, I might fall asleep, but you can stay.”

    Yelena looked at her for a second before walking to sit at the foot of the bed.

    Kate fell asleep listening to Yelena give her an entire monologue on the pros and cons of nearly every major brand of boxed Mac’n’Cheese.

    The File II Chapter Summary Clint and Laura look through Kate's file to try and help her, but make a 'surprising'

    revelation Chapter Notes Heyyyyy whaddup heres my current favourite parental figures See the end of the chapter for more notes Clint made his way downstairs, heading straight to the kitchen where he heard Laura puttering about.

    “Tea?” She asked as soon as he entered the room.

    “Yeah, thanks,” really he wanted a coffee and to stay awake all night trying to figure out what was going on, but Laura’s tone was clear.

    Laura placed the pot on the hob and turned to face her husband. His eyebrows were furrowed, his mouth a thin line, and his eyes were staring down at the file he had placed on the island, Laura gently took hold of his hands, his blue eyes snapped up to her brown ones.

    “Did Yelena tell you what’s going on?”

    Laura shook her head, “Is it something you can tell me?” They were both in the same business, it was how they met, she knew that sometimes there were things he couldn’t tell her, and vice versa, it was hard, she wouldn’t lie, but she understood.

    Clint nodded slowly, “Yeah, it’s to do with Kate, she said I can tell you, and I think I’ll need your help,” he gave her a look and she nodded, “I know you got out of this life…”

    Laura laughed, “When are we ever really out ?” She pulled away to pour the hot water into the mugs for their tea.

    Clint watched her as she made the mugs of tea, he dropped into a seat at the nearby table, this was the same kitchen he had sat with the rest of the original team years ago, when they were arguing a little, and all alive. Now the kitchen had been completely gutted and changed, as he always promised he would, and the team was split up, gone, dead, or just gone, there were new heroes, better ones than he had ever been.

    Before he knew it Laura had placed a mug in front of him, and sat on the other side of the table, staring at him, purposely ignoring the file, though he could see her fingers twitching for it.

    “Yelena broke into Kate’s mom’s safe, after Eleanor Bishop was arrested, Kate says it was a kind gesture,” he shook his head a little, a small smile on his face remembering what Nat had told him about her, once she had brought him back from being Ronin, that she was trouble, but good trouble, mostly.

    Laura nodded, “Yeah, Yelena said she was here to help Kate, she cares about her.”

    Clint nodded his head, purposely ignoring the little spikes of protectiveness he felt for Kate, he blamed Kate for that, her persistence forced him to open his heart to her. “Yeah. Anyway, I met Eleanor Bishop before she was trying to kill us, or stop us from being killed, I’m not sure which, but she was… difficult.”

    “Was she cruel?”

    Clint frowned, “I don’t… she didn’t show anything outwardly, but Kate was different around her, more nervous, quieter, and then trying too hard, she was… I really don’t know, it was just a feeling.”

    Laura nodded her head at him, to show she understood and to urge him to continue.

    “This file was in the safe, Yelena thought that Kate should have it, I think she gave it to her in a present before we left New York. Kate opened it last night, and… I’ve not looked through it yet, but Katie said that it’s not good. She was adopted.”

    Laura’s eyebrows raised slightly, clearly not expecting it, “I’m guessing Kate was never told.”

    Clint shook his head, “Never. It’s a little more complicated than that, Yelena checked, and Kate was never in the system, she was never privately adopted, she wasn’t a private adoption, she came from nowhere, or that’s what Yelena found so far. Kate… she’s processing it, or not processing it, but she asked for my help.”

    “I’ll help too, anything she needs.”

    “Thanks, babe, I think we might need you, this is something Yelena would normally be able to find quickly… I don’t like that she’s met roadblocks.”

    Laura nodded her head, slowly, absently. Her fingers brushed the file, “Is this all the information she had?”

    Clint nodded his head, “Yup,” he leant back in this chair, cradling his mug of earl grey, he watched her open the file and begin to sort the papers and photos into different piles.

    He allowed his mind to wander as he watched her, he couldn’t imagine how Kate was feeling, and there was what she had said about her adoptive father, that she couldn’t hate him, which implied it was easy to hate her adoptive mother, or already did hate her.

    Clint and Kate were very different, his father had been a bad person, and like a lot of people, his mother hadn’t had a choice but to put up with it. Harold Barton hadn’t been a good person, and he had been cruel to Edith Barton, and he had hated Clint and his older brother Barney. It had made Clint quiet, focused, it had made him not the best person, he had been recruited to shield and had changed his ways, but he kept that quiet focus.

    Kate was the opposite; she hadn’t grown small like he had, but big. Everything she did was big, the way she’d talk for hours about anything she was interested in, with great enthusiasm, she had done this with her mother, even when it was clear that she wasn’t exactly comfortable with it. She was so brave, only twenty two, she was so young and so brave, she was so brave that it scared Clint, and it took his breath away. It was impossible to not like Kate Bishop, only Eleanor had come close to achieving this- hell Kate had made a connection with a tracksuit Bro and helped him reconnect with his girlfriend!

    He wondered how his life would have gone, had he turned out more like Kate than like himself.

    “Clint .”

    Laura’s voice chilled his spine, he was up on his feet in less than a second, his posture squared, his hand reaching for a kitchen knife as he glanced around at all of the exits and entrances, looking for the threat.

    “It’s her. ”

    Clint glanced down at Laura, she wasn’t readying to fight, and there were no signs of a physical threat, she was just sat there, staring at a tiny photograph, but something in her voice still had him wanting to run, to flee, hell to simply play dead, but he slowly retook his seat, “What are you talking about Laur?”

    She finally looked up at him, her eyes sharp and sparkling at the same time, “ Clint, it’s her .” She pushed the photograph across the tables but Clint didn’t dare look at it, he didn’t know why, other than some feeling of… being on a precipice, everything was about to change, and he didn’t know if he was up for the challenge.

    He looked anyway, because he was Clint Barton, one of the last Avengers, he was not the coward his father had made him.

    The child in the photograph was Kate, he knew that before looking, and when he did he saw her.

    No.

    He saw… her .

    “Nicky ,” he wasn’t sure if he had said it just in his head, or out loud, and couldn’t bring himself to tell the difference between the two right then.

    The girl had Nicky’s dark brown hair, longer than Clint had expected her to have when she was only young, he could just about make out the blue eyes, the way her chin was all pointed, exactly as Laura’s mom had shown him photo’s of when his wife was a baby.

    Then there was the most chilling thing, the fact that in this photograph, the one they had never taken, Nicky was wearing the same pumpkin playsuit that Laura had bought for her days before she was taken. She hadn’t worn it yet.

    This was Nicky.

    Nicole Barton.

    And Kate Barton.

    “How can it be her Clint? How?!” Laura demanded, now up on her feet pacing the kitchen.

    Clint was still.

    “They did tests! They did so many tests, they found… they found the baby, our baby, they made sure, we saw the,” she swallowed, looking a little sick, “the remains .”

    She had practically whispered that word.

    Clint found himself back in the car, driving with Kate beside him.

    “Are they sure it’s her?”

    “They did tests.” he said, not looking away from the road, he willed himself to look at Kate, to examine her face, to see for himself if it was really her.

    “But were the tests done by Hydra scientists though?”

    “But… Shield wasn’t just Shield then… we thought…” he met his wife’s eyes, “We talked about it, we talked to each other, to other people, to Nat, and Fury, and… when Shield fell we asked if there was any chance that the tests could have got screwed up, we all agreed it was unlikely… but not impossible.”

    “Fuck,” the swear came out in a breath as she dropped to the seat beside Clint’s, her hand curling around his own, “Do you… how could it be her… we gave up looking for her, we,”

    she swallowed down a sob, shaking her head to try and rid herself of unwanted thoughts, “How can it just be her, appearing in our lives by coincidence? She steals your suit from an auction while you happen to be in town, not knowing it was you, she convinces you to help train her, to let her help, she comes home. What the hell are the chances of it?”

    Clint shook his head, trying not to think of all the times in New York where he still could have lost Kate, Nicky? “I don’t know… I don’t… We need to make sure it’s her.”

    Laura nodded firmly, “Do we tell her? I don’t want her to think we’re just keeping her here in case she’s our long lost daughter, which we’re not, but I don’t want to lie to her. I mean this isn’t TV, DNA tests take time .”

    Clint shrugged, “I really don’t know. But I can help with the DNA part. I’ll call Coulson, he poached the top two scientists from the academy when he died.”

    “You aren’t supposed to know he’s alive.”

    A small smile appeared on his lips, tugged at the corner, just slightly, “Neither are you.” She let out a laugh, shaking her head, “This is just… Clint, how can it be her? I know you don’t know I just… this is completely ridiculous,” she laughed again, and Clint joined her. When they finally stopped laughing Clint pulled her into his arms. “If it’s not her,” he began quietly.

    “Then it’s not her,” She told him simply, “We’ve been through this before.”

    “We haven’t been through it in a while.”

    She nodded her head slightly, then looked into his blue eyes; so like Kate’s… she had thought it before, but hadn’t put it together, “But this time if it’s not her then we still have Kate, and we’ll still help her. Because regardless Kate came from somewhere, and there’s a big gap between her birth and her being ‘adopted’.”

    Clint nodded his head, and tried his hardest to push down the hope which was brewing inside of him, “So we… shouldn’t go up and check on her… right?”

    Laura bit her lip, clearly tempted, “We… shouldn’t… I…” she shook her head, “No, we really shouldn’t.”

    “Right.”

    “Right.”

    On their way up to their bedroom an hour later they both couldn’t resist but to peek their heads into the room: Kate was fast asleep, she was half hanging over the side of the bed, her fingers just above Lucky’s fur, there was no sign of Yelena, somewhat surprisingly, so Laura left Clint’s side at the door and tiptoed in.

    She gently moved Kate so she was no longer about to fall out. She hovered for just a second, trying to see Kate as just Kate, not the mix she had been seeing since that photograph, but she couldn’t do it.

    She tore herself away, with great effort, and out of the room, as Kate mumbled in her sleep and turned over.

    Clint kept his eyes on Kate, until Lucky woke to look at him, he gently shut the door, and took Laura’s hand, willing each other to step away.
