    She pried open her eyes and jumped back in the bed until her head hit the wall, away from the dark figure looming over her, “Shiii-”

    “You’re awake!”

    “Fu-” Kate’s hand flew to her chest, her heart was beating so fast, she tried to breathe to slow it, and tried her best not to glare at Nathaniel Barton who was staring at her with a big grin on his face, “Hey, Nate? What’s, are you okay?”

    “Uh huh, I’m good, are you okay?”

    “Yeaaah,” Kate said slowly, her eyes skimming the boy; his brown hair was messy, but probably tidier than hers, he had sparkling hazel eyes, with flecks of blue in, and a smile which Kate recognised as Clint’s. He was also dressed in what looked like snow gear, complete with a hat, wellies, and a scarf. “Damn, did I sleep for that long?” She asked, mostly to herself.

    “Everyone’s still asleep,” he told her, still smiling, “I’m hungry but I’m not allowed to make breakfast alone.”

    “Oh, erm,” Kate pushed herself to sit up, wiping the crusted sleep from her eyes, “do you want me to wake your dad or mom up?”

    Nate shook his head, then his eyes grew pleading, his head tilted sideways ever so slightly, “Can you help me make it? Pretty please?”

    Kate knew that look as she had used it many times… but that didn’t mean that it didn’t work… “I can make cereal? Maybe toast?” She offered, kind of hoping he’d choose to wake up one of his siblings instead of her, but his face broke into a smile and he nodded, holding his hand out to her, “Okay, guess I’m getting up now.”

    The house was pretty in the dark, with Christmas lights twinkling everywhere as the central heating creaked as it turned on and warmed the place up, the windows had frost on the panes, and outside she could see the thick blanket of snow which settled across the land, illuminated by the upcoming snow.

    Nate chatted about every single thing which came to his mind, Kate didn’t know most of the things he was on about, but she tried to ask him questions, eventually he began to talk all about superheroes, and Kate found that she could talk to him all about it, and he seemed to love when she told him random facts she had picked up.

    Kate and Nate sat on the floor in front of all the cereals, Kate was reading all of the names of them, and Nate seemed to be copying her, “So, which cereal dude? Spidey-shapes, Coco-

    Starks, Cap'n Crunch… are all of the cereals superhero themed?”

    “Uh huh, Daddy let me choose some, Lila and Cooper don’t really eat cereal, I mean Cooper sometimes eats it, but Lila’s too boring for it.”

    Kate held back a laugh, she had been Lila’s age not too long ago, and she knew the desperation to distance yourself from anything which could be deemed as immature, apparently including sugary superhero cereal.

    “Well,” she told Nate, gathering the boxes of cereal into her arms, “I love this sorta thing, I basically lived on it in college.”

    “I wish I was allowed to live off cereal,” Nate threw his head back a little dramatically. “Hey if you go to college when you’re older you get to, I had a whole week where I only ate cookies,” she laughed when Nate’s eyes went wide, she was fairly certain she had just become the coolest person in the world to Nate.

    And sure, she may still be a little scared around him, but it wasn’t like he was going to bite… hopefully.

    Clint Barton woke up with a groan, he had slept ever so slightly awkwardly and now there was a crick in his neck, growing older was not his favourite thing, he decided, though he was sure that part of his body’s advanced aging was doing stupid things like jumping from planes and slipping in ceiling vents for the last few decades.

    He rubbed the sleep from his eyes, letting out another yawn. He was still so tired, and Nate hadn’t come to wake them up yet, so he could probably get some more sleep if he wanted…

    Then he remembered the night before.

    Kate’s file.

    Kate.

    Nicky.

    Fuck.

    He could feel his heart beating faster, he glanced at Laura, worried the noise would wake her up, but she was fast asleep. He sucked in a slow breath, as he had taught himself to do as a child, to slow his heart, to slow his fear, he wasn’t having a heart attack, he was just… worried? Or was this the fear from a couple of decades finally catching up with him?

    He slipped out from under Laura’s arm, she should sleep a little longer, she needed it just as much as he did, it wasn’t like either of them had slept the night before.

    After slipping in his hearing aid, he forwent his usual routine of showering then checking on all of the kids. He knew he shouldn’t, but he couldn’t help but walk slowly down the corridor to Kate’s door.

    She had never had her own room in this place, she would have done, he would have made it look however she had wanted, he bit his lip as he thought that, trying to keep away any what- ifs, but as he did so he realised that Kate did the exact same thing, it was a common enough gesture, but they shared it.

    “Kate?” He called softly, he waited a beat before gently knocking on the door, he called her name again, to no answer, that wasn’t really a surprise, he knew Kate liked to sleep late. He needed her up, he and Laura had come up with a plan the night before, he was going to take Kate to get her DNA tested, and do a little sleuthing, then be back home tomorrow, he knew that Laura didn’t want Kate out her eyes just as much as he didn’t, whether she was Nicky or not she was going through a lot.

    Still, he opened the door, instead of shouting her name, he just wanted to see her, plus it would mean he knew she was actually awake, yup Clint, totally believable, he told himself. The room was how it had been the night before, the bag they had brought from New York was still packed, with some clothes spilling out, and on the desk sat all of the presents Kate had received from them for Christmas, she had been so surprised by it all, he smiled at the thought.

    It took him, admittedly, way too long to notice what was off about the room.

    Kate wasn’t in it.

    The bed had clearly been slept in, but Lucky was still led on the floor beside it, where he had been the night before, and there were no signs that Kate had gotten dressed.

    He clenched his hands into fists at his sides to stop them from shaking, it didn’t work. He took a slow steady breath. “Kate?”

    “Kate!” He called louder, but couldn’t hear her, his hand went to his hearing aid, clicking next to his ear to test that it was on and working, the noise was clear, but there was still no sound of Kate.

    He began searching the room, though there weren’t a lot of places for him to search, after not finding her under the bed or in the wardrobe he left the room.

    He made his way to the bathroom, banging on the door, only for Cooper to come out, “Have you seen Kate?” He asked her before Cooper could ask why he was trying to knock the door down.

    “Er, no? I think she’s still asleep?”

    “She’s not,” He told him, his heart was beating in his ears, he could barely hear anything, he couldn’t breathe.

    “Dad?”

    “Dad?”

    “What is-?”

    Clint couldn’t hear, or breathe, he tried to breathe, but he couldn’t… he couldn’t go through this again, he wasn’t strong enough… A hand on his arm made him jump, he just about restrained himself from reacting on instinct when he saw Laura’s face, and the worry on it. All around him were worried faces; Laura’s, Cooper’s, Lila’s, and Yelena’s, though the latter three seemed to be confused too, more than Laura.

    “I can’t find her, Laura, she’s not, she’s not there. We need to… to…”

    Laura’s face had drained of colour, but she nodded, “I’ll go make sure Nate’s still playing in his room, Yelena can you check the roof, you need to be careful, it’ll be icy- Clint you searched everywhere downstairs?”

    Clint stilled, “Fuck.”

    He pulled free of Laura’s hand, and away from the group gathering, and practically flew down the stairs, calling Kate’s name as he did.

    This time she called back to him, and he raced to the kitchen, not able to breathe until he set eyes on her.

    Kate was sat at the kitchen island with Nate, both of them looked confused, as they sat eating cereal, looking like she didn’t have a care in the world.

    He let out a heavy breath.

    “Hey Clint, what’s up?”

    “What’s up? What’s up! I couldn’t find you! You can’t just disappear like that Kate, I went to wake you up and you weren’t there, I had no idea where you were! You can’t just- just-!”

    He struggled to find the words, or any words, especially when facing Kate who seemed to shrink into herself at his anger. It wasn’t even anger, it was fear, but she didn’t know that, and he wasn’t showing it: he was fucking up with Kate for approximately the hundredth time.

    “Cooper, Lila, will you get dressed and take Nate outside to play? I’ll make you guys some breakfast in a little while.”

    Laura’s voice game as a surprise to Clint, he managed to tear his eyes off of Kate to see that everyone had followed him downstairs, Laura’s eyes met his and he knew that she understood how he felt.

    Cooper and Lila shared a look, the latter rolled her eyes, and the two headed upstairs to change clothes.

    “I will take Nate out to make snow monsters, if he can teach me how to make them?”

    Yelena’s voice was calm and steady.

    Clint met her eyes and nodded, he knew that Yelena wouldn’t harm his son, and Nate looked like he was nervous at being in trouble.

    “I’m good at building,” Nate told her, climbing off the stool to the Russian's side when his mother gave him an encouraging nod.

    Clint dropped his head to look at the floor as he listened to everyone bar Laura and Kate left the kitchen, once they were all dressed and outside he finally looked up, Kate had her eyes on him, her arms crossed, and trying to hide how hurt she clearly was.

    “ Katie -” he started.

    “Don’t ‘ Katie ’ me. What the hell is going on?”

    Kate couldn’t take her eyes off Clint, even a few days ago when he was shouting that they weren’t partners he hadn’t been that angry, or when he first met her and she was wearing the Ronin suit.

    “ Katie-

    “Why don’t we go somewhere a bit more private?”

    “I know, Kate,” Laura told her, she and Clint shared a look, and he nodded tersely, “But I think we all need to talk, and I’m sure none of us want the kids to walk into this. Please Kate?”

    Kate held in an aggravated sigh, and nodded.

    Laura led them to a hidden office, it was simple; a few bookcases lined one wall, two desks with top range computers on either desk, several bows on the wall, with some quivers full of arrows in a bin beneath them, there were several safes, one built into either desk, a couple of small ones in each bookcase, and from what Kate could tell one or two built into the wall. The office of two Shield agents.

    Kate was directed into the chair of the messier desk, Clint’s (unsurprisingly), and she took a seat, Laura rolled her own office chair so she was a little in front of Kate, while Clint settled for leaning against a wall, her eyes flickered to him, he look… ashamed?

    “I was just downstairs, Nate woke me up, asking me to make breakfast with him, so we made breakfast.”

    Clint bit his lip and nodded, “I know… I was an idiot not checking downstairs, I am really sorry.”

    Kate turned the words over in her mind a few times, she knew that her mother would never have apologised to her so sincerely, or at all, let alone look so ashamed of acting that way. So she gave him a small smile, “I accept your apology, if it doesn’t count towards the ‘giving you a heart attack’ token.”

    Clint let out a snort of laughter, and settled on the desk beside her instead of keeping his distance, “Deal, but how come I have a feeling I’m going to regret giving that thing to you?” “Because I gotta make it worth it,” Kate told him with a shrug, but movement from Laura drew her attention, she placed Kate’s file on the desk, “What’s…. Did you guys find something?”

    “Maybe,” Clint said, a the same time that Laura said-

    “Kinda.”

    Kate kind of wanted to flee then, if they found something it made all of this so very real, she wished she was back in the kitchen with Nate, having a debate over cereals. “What’s that mean? Did you… did you find my parents.”

    Laura’s face tightened, but her fingers were light on the photograph as she barely managed to push it across the desk to Kate, “Is that you?”

    Kate had seen the photograph when she first looked through the file, it was her, she knew that much- it was probably the oldest of the photos she had never seen in the file, so it was easy to recognise the child as her, she had photos which must have been taken maybe a year later, perhaps a little more or less, she didn’t look much different in them.

    “Yeah, I hadn’t seen that before but it’s me, a little younger than the youngest photos of me, but I guess that makes sense if I wasn’t a Bishop yet.”

    Laura’s face somehow tightened even more, she barely touched the next photograph as she slid it across the table, it stopped halfway between them, so Kate had to grab it.

    “Oh, I didn’t see this one last time, was it stuck between pages or something?”

    Laura could only shake her head.

    “Kate,” Clint’s voice was soft, as though talking to an animal who might spook any second, “is that you?”

    “Yeah,” Kate told them with a shrug, “I don’t get it, what’s wrong, I thought you’d found something?”

    Clint moved away from her, and Katie tried not to feel nervous at his absence, so she watched him as he walked to the bookcase and pulled out a book, part of Kate was hoping it was about to open another secret room, but he just picked it up and walked over, placing the book in front of Kate open on the front page.

    Kate frowned at the photograph, it was her, she knew it, the same way she knew how to breathe, it was just something she knew , but she hadn’t seen his one either, it was her sat on someone's shoulders, but the way it was taken all she could see were her chubby little hands holding onto hair with a smile on her face.

    She flicked to the next page- her playing with teddies, serving them all cake, a game she had loved as a child.

    The next page had her on the beach, her knees all sandy, a massive smile as she tucked into the ice cream, she was sitting on a man's lap… Clint’s lap.

    “What the fuck?” She whispered, she flicked through more pages, it was her, she knew it, especially as the girl in the photographs grew a little older in each, it was always her, usually smiling, with Clint in some, Laura in others, both in some, and other people she had never met, or never remembered meeting.

    “What?” The word came out breathily, she looked from Laura who seemed to turn herself to a statue to stop from breaking down, but failed when it came to her weepy eyes, she looked to Clint who was frowning, his eyes sparkling with tears too.

    “I don’t… I don’t understand, I don’t get it… what’s, Clint what the hells going on? You guys knew me when I was younger? It doesn’t make any sense!”

    Clint moved slowly so not to spook her as he reached out, past her, to the book, he closed it, allowing the front to show the name:

    Nicole Elizabeth Barton 14th July 2002.

    “Clint?” Kate said desperately, like his name alone was a life raft in a storm, she looked at him, not bothering to throw up a disguise, she was too scared to do so.

    “We’re not one hundred percent sure, kid, but… you look so much like her… it’s something we have to consider.”

    “But… it’s impossible, right?” she looked to Laura, “I mean, they found her, she wasn’t me .” “They found a body,” Laura’s voice shook, a stray tear leaking down her cheek, “but… she was barely a body there Kate, they relied on DNA to test that she was Nicky.”

    “That’s… I’m so sorry… but they did tests, you said -” the words were an almost accusation at Clint.

    “-I know, but like you said, we couldn’t rule out that they worked for Hydra, and with the company Eleanor kept, and my and Laura’s jobs… it’s possible.”

    Kate shook her head, repeatedly, she got to her feet and began to pace, “But… it’s not possible I’m her, I mean we looked alike, back then, but everyone under like eight looks exactly the same, I just have one of those faces-”

    “-Do you have a birthmark on the left side of your chest?”

    Kate stopped immediately, turning to look at Laura who looked almost like she was in pain, “How- how do you know that?”

    “So did Nicky,” Laura told her simply. “Kate… I think-”

    “But what happens when it turns out that I’m not, I’m really sorry, but I can’t be her, I wouldn’t have ended up with Eleanor if I was yours, it just wouldn’t have… It doesn’t make any sense!” she practically shouted that, her words dying as she fell back into the office chair, her heart pounding as she stared at the album cover.

    “Kate, listen to me kid, breathe, okay?” Clint’s voice was firm as he turned her chair to face him, “I know this is beyond,,, anything, but I’mma need you to breathe, copy me, ‘kay?”

    Kate nodded, breathing faster now she realised she wasn’t breathing correctly, she copied Clint as he took calming breaths, until she was able to do it on her own again.

    “Kate,” Laura had managed to collect herself as Kate broke down, “If you’re not Nicky we’re still going to help you, we’ll track down your birth parents, we’ll help you find out what happened, and be here for you whether you want to get to know them or not.”

    “Hell, you can stay, we’ve planned that the whole time, if you like being here, you could live here, do your classes online, if you can,” Clint added.

    “Right,” she said, “And if you are then we’ll do the exact same thing, except the finding your parents bit.

    Kate clenched her jaw, not wanting to believe that she was their dead daughter, all she had missed out on and could have been, it was almost too much to bare, and if she wasn’t her would they ever stop seeing her in Kate’s face, would they ever stop wishing that she was Nicole.

    “How do we do this? One of those mail your DNA to a big Corporation thing?”

    Clint shook his head, standing up straight, “No, I know someone who owes me a favour, he knows two amazing scientists, they’ll be able to help us do the DNA test, but a lot quicker than the standard ones, and we might be able to do a little sleuthing, both on all of the Shield databases and we can see if Eleanor was hiding anything else.”

    Kate nodded, then nodded again, for reasons unknown to her.”So we just go see them? When can we go, I mean I know most places are shut for the holidays, but is there a way we can maybe-”

    “We’re going to drive down to New York today, once you’re dressed and I’ve had some coffee, we’ll stay overnight, be back home the next day, hopefully with/ information.”

    Kate nodded, but couldn’t help but look at Laura, she did notice the similar chin, the same dark hair… common things, but they stood out.

    “I’ll be searching from here, and keeping an eye out to make sure you two don’t get into trouble like the last time,” She sent Kate a smile which somehow apologised for leaving her, and tried to will a smile from Kate.

    Kate sent her a tiny one, “I just… I don’t want you guys to be disappointed.” Or herself, she thought in her mind.

    “That’s impossible,” Laura told her.

    “Either way, “ Clint added, “And either way we’re going to help you,” his blue eyes locked with Kate’s identical ones, he didn’t say anything, but tipped his head forward slightly, until she gave him a small smile and nodded her head.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes The drive to New York was quiet and tense, Clint and Kate barely spoke, the silence was filled with music on the radio. Clint tried to talk to her a couple of times, only to look over and see her ‘sleeping’, he didn’t need to be a trained spy to know that she wasn’t sleeping, but he didn’t push, she didn’t want to speak to him,and he couldn’t blame her.

    Eventually, despite his patience, Clint sighed, “I know you’re not sleeping Katie, we’re nearly there.”

    Kate was silent for a second, before pretending to wake up. She looked at Clint, he was watching the road, but his eyes were strained, the same squinting way they looked when he was concentrating. The drive had been too long, it gave her too much time to think, to spiral and then force herself to un-spiral. She looked out of the windows. Seeing the place they had driven not even two days previous in the light for the first time.

    “Where,” the word came out a little horse from not speaking, “where are we?”

    “Just outside of New York.”

    “It didn’t used to look like this, I used to come this way for competitions, when I was a kid,” she kept staring out of the window even when she felt Clint’s eyes on her.

    “Yeah, it was rebuilt during the Snap, a lot of people wanted out of the city, a lotta these eco houses were built, but we’re heading a little away from here,” as he said that he indicated, and they turned towards a large forest. “The base we’re going to is hidden, it’s protected too, so when we get there I’mma need you to stay in the car while I talk to some people.”

    “Are the people dangerous?”

    “Yes… but not to us.”

    “Then why are you lookin’ all stressy?” She asked “I mean other than, well, yeah everything.”

    A small smile flickered onto his face, “I don’t look stressed.”

    “Yeah, you do, you’re all frowny and quiet and glaring at the road, and you’re holding onto the wheel pretty tight, and you-”

    Clint rolled his eyes, “Yeah, yeah…,” he let out a sigh, “The guy in charge of Shield is called Phil Coulson, he brought me into Shield, he was my friend, I worked with him for years, he was your godfather, Nicky’s godfather,” he corrected, “he helped bring the Avengers together, and he was killed by Loki, when aliens took over New York.”

    “Shit,” Kate whispered, “I’m sorry, wait, how are we going to see him if he’s…?”

    “He was brought back to life, I don’t think it was pleasant, from what Laura’s heard, he still runs Shield, secretly protecting the world, and pretending to be dead.”

    Kate nodded slowly, watching Clint’s face carefully, “He doesn’t know that you know, does he?”

    “Nope,” Clint said, popping the ‘p’ in an attempt to sound cool about it, he looked over at Kate, “It’s okay, he’ll help us, he owes me.”

    “Because he pretended to be dead,” she nodded slowly.

    “Nah, he owed me anyway for something, something a long,” he dragged out the word, “time ago.”

    Kate nodded again, and stared out the window, watching as the forest got thicker and thicker, until Clint stopped the car. She felt her heart in her stomach, she needed answers but she suddenly didn’t want them all at the same time. She fidgeted in her seat, she wanted to get up, to pace, or to run around a track, hell she’d rather be fighting than just sitting there. “Stay here.”

    “Yeah, yeah, I heard you.”

    “ Kate .”

    Kate finally looked at Clint, his face was as stern as his voice had been, she sank down in her seat just a little, “Okay, I’ll stay in the car.”

    “Good. This shouldn’t take long, I’ll come and get you when we’re ready… you okay?”

    “Yeah that plan’s okay,” she tried not to sound like she was grumbling too much, but she couldn’t help it.

    “No I meant,” Clint’s eyes flickered out of the windshield, seeing a shadowy figure emerge from seemingly nowhere, “are you okay? I know this is a lot, I mean it’s a lot for me and Laura, I can’t imagine what it’s like for you.”

    “I just want this over with,” she told him with more certainty than she felt, “I want to know.” Clint nodded once but continued to stare at her as though he was reading a book, “Okay, I’m going to go talk to May.”

    “Who’s May?”

    Clint nodded to the figure.

    Kate actually jumped a little, “Shiii- where did she come from?”

    Clint actually laughed at that, “No one ever knows.” He opened the door of the truck, “Stay here,” he told her one last time, before leaving her alone in the truck.

    Kate watched for what seemed like forever as Clint walked over to the superspy in the shadows, she tried to watch his body language, as he had taught her back in New York, but all she could see was him standing as though he was ready to fight.

    Her hand went to the door of the truck as the lady was joined by another one, then a man… “Fuck it,” she told herself, seeing more people emerge from the shadows, she didn’t care what Clint said, he could be in danger, she didn’t even care who he was to her, he was important to her.

    She ran out of the car to Clint’s side, raising her fists in defence.

    “What the hell are you doing?”

    Kate turned to Clint, who was looking at her with an eyebrow raised.

    “This is a set up, they’re all around,” she hissed at Clint, who didn’t look bothered at all, in fact he just frowned at her.

    “This is an introduction,” the man across from them told her, he had an amused smile on his face, he looked like someone who’d work in an office, not a spy, “and an apology,”

    “I don’t think I heard an apology,” Clint told him, sounding slightly amused, before he turned to Kate once again. He crossed his arms over his chest and looked down at her, “Didn’t I tell you to stay in the car?”

    “Well, yeah, but-“ “No buts, I told you, I told you more than once .”

    “I thought you were about to get attacked, thet you needed backup,” Kate found herself glaring back at him.

    “I could have taken pretty much any of them.”

    “He wouldn’t have taken me,” the original lady, May, told her, “but I wasn’t going to attack him.”

    “And you should have stayed where I told you to,” Clint repeated again, sighing as he watched May nod to someone and the Shield team around them (who had just been introducing themselves) left.

    “You must be Kate,” a woman only a few years older than Kate broke the silence walking up to her with a smile, “I’m Daisy, I saw you in a few viral videos, you’ve got some good skills.”

    Kate smiled, flushing a little, “Er, thanks, I like your hair.”

    Daisy grinned back at her, “Thanks, er, this is Fitzsimmons.”

    Two people approached them, one wearing a labcoat, the other looking a little frazzled. Kate held up her hand, “Hi, er… Which of you is Fitzsimmons?”

    “It doesn’t really matter.”

    “We both are.”

    They said at the exact same time, Kate wasn’t sure how to react, but Clint chose that moment to step in front of her to meet the scientists. He kept his arms at his sides, seemingly relaxed, but Kate knew from experience he had weapons within easy reach while his hands were there, “What’s your clearance levels?”

    “They’re seven, Barton,” Coulson told him, nodding to him meaningfully.

    Clint hesitated then nodded back at his old handler, then looked to the nervous looking scientists, “Okay, let’s get this done then,” he looked at Kate, “Yeah?”

    Kate walked next to Clint down an underground tunnel, she was relieved that Clint was talking to that Coulson guy, because if he wasn’t he would probably hear her heart beating. She tried to hide it, knowing that if she gave it away at all Clint would want to have a conversation with her, she was starting to miss the guy who didn’t want to talk to her.

    When they finally got to the end of the tunnel and through a security door they were met with a modern living area, with a giant pool table, Kate hadn’t played in a while, other than when she was technically too drunk to remember playing it while on a night out in her first year of college.

    Coulson talked about the history of the base, as they walked, while Daisy filled in some of the more personal history of the place, mostly saying places they’d all nearly died, both trying to fill the silence, May didn’t bother with talking, as she walked at the back of the group, the front was led by a quietly bickering Fitzsimmons.

    Kate couldn’t focus on any of it, it was both too loud and quiet, too much and too little. Clint’s hand was suddenly in hers, the way he had taken it after Eleanor had been arrested, she looked down at their hands together, then up at him, she gave him a tentative smile, he gave her a more genuine one, and gently squeezed her hand.

    She looked away, she wasn’t ready for this to be over, for the test to come back negative, to still not know who she was, and to never have Clint and Laura look at her without hurting again. She bit her lip as they walked on.

    “How long will this take?” Clint asked as Simmons swiped a swab around Kate’s mouth.

    “Oh, it shouldn’t take more than a couple of minutes, top,” She told them in her happy yet clipped English accent, “We have you and the er, other, agent Barton’s DNA on file, as well as your daughter’s DNA.” She placed the bud into a hole in the lab desk.

    “I thought you were just checking it against Clint’s,” Kate asked, before taking a sip from the water bottle Simmon’s passed her, it felt so weird having the cotton bud swiped around.

    “Well, that would be a way to check it, but we wanted to make completely sure, and as we already have the three DNA samples logged on the system it’s easy enough to run it.”

    Kate nodded her head, looking down as Simmons moved to work at the computer.

    Clint gently knocked his shoulder against Kate’s, “Whatcha thinking about?”

    “Nothing,” She said, way too quickly, he gave her a look and she shrugged, “I don’t know how to feel about all this, I mean a few weeks ago I thought I knew who I was, and I didn’t get along with my, with Eleanor, but it was just normal, y’know? And now this test is going to come back saying that I’m still nobody, not a nobody, but it’ll come back that no one knows who I am, or I’ll be…”

    “Nicky… Kate, we were serious when we said that we’re going to help you get answers no matter what the answer is.”

    “It’ll still all change, I mean you’re not going to be able to look at me without thinking about the feeling you’re going to get when they say I’m not her,” she shook her head, “I shouldn’t have even shown you the file.”

    “ Hey , of course you should have shown us the file, and you’re not taking anything from us, the people who took our daughter did that, not you. I know I’m probably not objective, but I’m almost certain who you are, and regardless of any of that, you’re still Kate, you’re still my partner, the future Hawkeye, and the person who makes cereal with a kid who I’m pretty sure she’s scared of just because he asked her.”

    “Kids are terrifying,” She admitted, “I’ve not really been around them. Nate’s cute, and not as scary when I found out that he has good taste in dinosaurs.”

    Clint grinned, raising an eyebrow, “Oh and the cereal this morning, did you mix every cereal we had?” He laughed when Kate looked proud.

    “Yeah, it’s the best way to make cereal, especially when you guys have so many good cereals, how were we meant to choose?”

    Clint shook his head as he grinned, “I’m pretty sure those are nearly entirely pure sugar, we just get sent them all the time because of some deal Tony made years ago. I keep trying to send them back when they arrive, but Nate found some.”

    “I’m going to make sure he knows about them every time.”

    Clint rolled his eyes and let out a groan as he dropped his head back, “I shouldn’t have told you that, I should have known you’d say that.”

    Kate grinned and opened her mouth to answer him-

    “Oh!”

    Kate and Clint looked over at the scientist at the same time, she sent them a smile as she pulled a printed piece of paper from a silent printer.

    “That was quick, erm if you’re ready I can tell you now, or if you need some more time…?” Kate shook her head, “No, now, please,” she looked at Clint, “I mean if you’re okay with that?”

    Clint nodded his head, “No, I want to know now, please Simmons.”

    The scientist nodded again and stood in front of them, “Okay, so as you know I compared Kate, your DNA, with that of yours Agent Barton, and the other Agent Barton’s, and separately to that of the abducted Barton child. These tests are as advanced as they could possibly be, with very little chance of error, I also made them run five times, and the results are as clear as they can be. “There is a ninety nine point nine percent match between Katherine Bishop and Nicole Barton, and there is the highest parental match there could be between Katherine Bishop and L Barton and C Barton.”

    Kate was frozen, not able to react, or even take it in, she heard Clint suck in a breath. “Kate, you are Nicole Barton,” Simmons told her, her voice soft, “Agent Barton, your daughter’s finally been found.”

    Kate swallowed, then turned, tentatively to look at Clint Barton, her hero since she was a young child.

    Her father.

    Her eyes stung with tears, her arms slowly lifted to wrap around him in return, she buried her face against him, not sure what she was crying for, or what she was feeling, other than a Lot. “ Kate ,” Clint’s voice came out broken, she couldn’t see his face but she could tell that he was crying.

    A lump formed in her throat, she let out a breath she hadn’t realised she was holding, relief that he had called her that not ‘Nicky’, she allowed tears to roll down her cheeks as she silently cried against her father.

    sucker-punched-me-in <---- ✨ She ✨ See the end of the chapter for more notes It felt like Clint held her for hours, and maybe he did, and she found that she needed it, she didn’t want to look at him, she knew it was stupid or childish, but it just seemed easier. He pulled away first, with his hands still firmly on her arms, there was only an in between them in height, but Kate felt small when he looked down at her, his eyes still wet, like she had all the answers to his questions, but she was just as lost as ever.

    “I…”

    “Are you okay?”

    Kate shrugged, Clint’s hands remained on her, “I don’t… know… I don’t know what I’m meant to say in this situation, there was never a TV episode about it.”

    Clint smiled softly, “Yeah, I never, I never thought that I’d see this day, that… that we’d find you.”

    “Clint… I don’t know how to be her.”

    His fingers brushed back a strand of hair which had fallen in her face, “You are her, kid, you always have been. I saw it, when I first saw you, and then when we were home, you look just like Laur, I tried not to think that you could be you, but you just… you’re you, that’s enough. I mean if you were trying to be her you’d be figuring out how to run, and you’ve already got that down, so you’re all good.”

    Kate let out a breath of a laugh, “What do we do now? How do we track down whoever took me? Why did they take me? I don’t get it!”

    “Hey,” Clint’s voice was soothing and calm, despite the fire growing in Kate, “it’s okay, Kate, we’re going to figure this all out, I’m not sure why they took you either, but we’ll figure it out. Carefully , we’re going to track them down, but you’re not going anywhere near any of them once we’ve found them.”

    “What!” Kate exclaimed, shocked enough to move out of Clint’s reach, “but it was me they stole! I want to know, I have a right to-”

    “-And you will know, but you are not going anywhere near any of them. They stole you , they took you from us , I am not letting that happen again.”

    Kate glared at him, “I’m going to help find them, I get that at least , I’m sorry you lost your- Nicky, me, whatever , but I’m an adult, Clint, I’ve done this before-”

    “ No Katie, you haven’t. You and I dealt with a small New York mafia, the people involved with taking you are way beyond that, we’re going to need the help of Shield, and any favours anyone still owes me from the old days to do this.”

    “I took on Kingpin, by myself,” Kate could have sworn that Clint paled at that, a phantom throb pulsed anywhere that she got hurt by the behemoth of a man, which was pretty much her whole body.

    “And you did great, Katie, I’m serious, you did, and I’m proud of you, but it scared the shit out of me you doing it then, kid, I just got you back.”

    Kate let out a small sigh, and with it her anger, Clint sounded so… broken, the feeling felt familiar, “I’m still helping find them, I won’t get too involved, but… please I need to be doing something . I want to know who took me, why they took everything away from me too, they took my life,” her voice broke and she felt Clint’s hands on her, she didn’t shrug them off this time, “I want to be able to move on Clint, I’m not going to be able to do that if I can’t help, I know that, and you know that too,” she stared at him, imploring him to have noticed that in their short time together.

    Clint pulled Kate into a hug, she stiffened for only a second, out of habit, before relaxing and hugging him back, she felt his lips press a gentle kiss to her temple, and she felt a pang of homesickness for something she couldn’t remember.

    “You’re so goddamn stubborn,” but when he said that it didn’t sound like a bad thing, in fact Kate could imagine that hint of pride in his eyes while he said it, “you’re just like your mom.”

    Kate let out a laugh as she moved out of the hug, “I dare you to tell her she’s stubborn,” she laughed as he looked scared.

    “Speaking of, I better phone Laura, your mom,” he couldn’t stop himself from smiling when he said that, “let her know the test result,” he looked around at the empty lab, “Coulson said that there’s no signal down here, we’ll have to go up to the surface to call her.”

    “Erm, Clint?”

    “Yeah?”

    “Can… can I stay down here, maybe you should tell her first, just the two of you, maybe you two should talk, y’know without me there…” Kate tried to tell herself that Laura wouldn’t be super disappointed that Kate was her daughter, but there were so many other people who would have been better daughters, and she didn’t want to risk hearing things she didn’t want to.

    Clint frowned slightly, then hesitated for a second, “Are you sure, she’s really gonna wanna talk to you, Katie, she wanted to come with us for this.”

    “I, I know, it’s just… it’s a lot, all the emotions of, everything, I just feel kinda like I’m intruding, when you talk about your, erm, Nicky, I know that’s like super weird because I am Nicky, but, I’m-”

    “Kate, it’s okay, I understand, I’ll talk to Laura alone, on two conditions.”

    “Go on…” Kate told him cautiously when he stopped.

    “A lot of this base is completely off limits to you so I’ll drop you at the place Coulson said that most of his team spends time in, you’ve gotta stay there until I’m back. Coulson’s setting up a spare room for us for tonight, once I’m back from talking to Laura you can go there if you want or you can go on a tour, but just while I’m gone I want you where I know I can find you.”

    He didn’t say it but she knew he didn’t want her to have to be all alone, which she should argue with on principle, but she was kind of grateful that he insisted. So she just nodded, “What’s the second?”

    “You have to talk to Laura tonight, before we hit the hay, I can hold her off until then but she’s going to want to talk to you.”

    His eyes locked with hers and she could see that he was serious, and that he thought that she would probably need some convincing, if anything that just made her feel more nervous. So she gave a slight nod.

    “That a deal?”

    “Uh huh.”

    Clint hesitated, then nodded himself, “Okay, good.”

    The communal area Clint led Kate to was smaller than she expected, and cosier, it had all the modern furniture the rest of the base seemed to have, but this room had photographs on the wall of a few people she had already met or at least seen, there were rugs, and throws, and anything to make it seem more homely.

    Clint checked with her a couple more times that she definitely didn’t want to go with him, before he left her, promising not to take that long.

    Kate walked around the room looking at the photos, she was surprised to find that the photographs were… normal? There were a lot of selfies or group shots of the team she had met, in various places around the world, sometimes in front of weird looking artefacts, and then there were photographs taken after pranks had happened, or at birthdays and celebrations, it was all very domestic, Kate couldn’t help but smile.

    “I’ve tried taking that photo of me covered in powder down, but every time I do it’s back there within like a day.”

    Kate jumped as Daisy announced herself, “Shii-, sorry I was just... Clint dumped me here.” Daisy shrugged, “I figured you’d be here,” she placed several pizzas down onto the coffee table, “And I figured you’d want pizza.”

    “You know what the results were?” Kate guessed.

    “No, but I figured either way pizza’s good, I mean it doesn’t cure everything, but it’s pizza. Trust me, it helps.”

    Kate let out a small snort, “I’m not sure it’ll help this mess.”

    “I found out I was stolen from my parents' village, leading my dad to destroy everyone he came into contact with after he came up with some sorta steroid super soldier serum addiction, and leading my mom to turn evil, she nearly killed me once, after I found them- this was after I screwed up a couple of hundred of times here… Pizza helps.”

    “That is…. That’s a lot,” Kate said, kind of impressed, “the test said I was the presumed dead kid of my childhood hero… Is there any pepperoni?”

    Daisy smiled at her, and handed her a couple of slices on a plate.

    By the time they had both got though their first slice they were trading life stories, both in awe of each others skills, Daisy had apparently seen a video of Kate destroying the clocktower at her college, and Kate happened to have listened to a couple of Daisy’s Rising Tide podcasts which were still floating around the web.

    “Okay, I’ve got a question, what was happening in that photo on the wall, the one where you’re all green, why did you end up with powder all over you?”

    “Fitz thought I was the person who kept sneaking in and ruining his Mario Kart score.”

    “Were you?”

    “Well, yeah, but it’s funny how competitive he gets at it, and how bad he is at it.”

    Kate laughed, “You’re the type of person who picks rainbow road for fun, aren’t you?”

    Daisy looked proud at that assumption, “So are you, right?”

    “Yeah, because it’s shockingly easy, it just throws everyone because it’s in space.”

    “Exactly!” Daisy looked at her with a huge smile, “hey, give me your phone, I’ll put my details in, that way if you ever want to play online we can.”

    Kate did, because Daisy was nice, and before everything in New York happened she had a lot of acquaintances, not that many friends, maybe Daisy could be one. “Thanks,” she pocketed the phone when Daisy passed it back to her.

    “No worries. You can also use it if you just wanna talk too, to someone who gets it.

    Clint walked into the room, frowning until he saw her, then a look of relief appeared on his face: Kate wondered what was up at first, before she realised that he might have been worried she wouldn’t be there.

    “Laura says hi,” he told her, nodding at Daisy then looking back to Kate, “I told her you’d call later.”

    “Got it,” she told him quickly, “Daisy got us all pizza, good pizza.”

    “Where’d you get pizza from around here?” he asked, loading some slices onto a plate and taking a seat on the arm of the couch where Kate was sitting.

    “Lola,” she told him with a ‘it’s no big deal’ shrug.

    Clint sucked in a breath.

    “Wait, who’s Lola?”

    “A sixty-two corvette, cherry red, always in peak condition, always sparklingly clean,” Clint told his daughter, longing in his voice.

    Kate levelled him with a look, “...So a car?”

    “Yes.”

    “No!” Clint protested looking between Daisy and Kate, “It’s more than a car.”

    “Yeah, it flies too.”

    “Woah, that’s awesome!”

    “It’s awesome without the flying part.”

    Kate continued to look at Clint as though he was stupid, but there was a hint of mischief in her eyes, “Just a car.”

    “ Youths ,” Clint scoffed, noting the smile between Kate and Daisy.

    They were shortly joined by May, Fitzsimmons, and eventually Coulson, who joined them last, looking a little stressed about something, but he smiled at them as he walked in, Clint nodded back, the two had clearly talked in private.

    He looked at Kate, as though he was going to say something, but thought twice about it. He caught sight of the pizza boxes around the room, then narrowed his eyes at Daisy, “Did you take Lola again?”

    “ No ! Of course not! Is that what you really think of me? That I’m some common thief?”

    There were a few murmurs of affirmation from the rest of Coulson’s team, but Coulson kept his eyes on the agent, “Then how’d the pizza get here?”

    “DoorDash.”

    “... To our secret underground base? I didn’t see any sign of DoorDash.”

    “They were really good at the ‘dash’ part of it.”

    Everyone laughed as Coulson rolled his eyes and grabbed some pizza before settling into a seat between May and Clint.

    “Wait wait wait,” Clint held up his hand, doing his best to look hurt, “ ‘again’ , how come she’s not dead? Whenever I even thought about borrowing Lola you tried to send me to Siberia!”

    “That’s because you drive like a rally car driver,” May pointed out with a laugh, “You managed to trash every car you drove on a mission, even when you didn’t need to. For once I’m on Phil’s side.”

    “Are you kidding? He drives so slow, unless we’re in a chase.” Kate looked between May and Clint, disbelievingly.

    “ That was a necessity, which is never going to happen again,” Clint told her firmly, “and not to be mentioned to Laura.”

    There was a snort of laughter from May and a chuckle from Coulson, both very aware of Laura Barton’s shield abilities to kill someone and make it look like an accident.

    Kate bit her lip to hide a smile forming, “It’ll cost you teaching me how to drive like you used to.”

    Clint narrowed his eyes but looked amused, “I’ll teach you how to drive, so you can get a licence, final offer.”

    “But I know how to drive, I just want to learn how to drive fast enough to go across a closing bridge, like in movies.”

    “A. You’re never doing that, ever , B. You told me that you couldn’t drive.”

    “Yeah I can’t , legally, but I know how to.”

    “Ahh,” he looked surprised but understanding, “I figured you’d have gotten your licence while you were in school-”

    “-Yeaaaah,” she cut in before he could say anything super embarrassing about her being young, “my instructors didn’t like it when I went a little over the speed limit.”

    “I know nothing about how you drive and I’m already on the instructors’ sides,” Clint told her deadpan, “I’ll teach you,” he added softer to her, she smiled back at him just as softly.

    Kate relaxed slowly around Coulson’s team, they were all so familiar with each other that they made it easier, and it was nice to see Clint smiling about his past for once, even when Natasha’s name came up. Daisy, Fitz, and Simmons all told her about their various mishaps on missions, and Kate told them all how she had met Clint, and about taking on Kingpin.

    Eventually it was time to call it a night, Clint and Kate had a long drive that morning and needed an early start the next day, and the team would apparently be going on mission, though Kate couldn’t get even a hint about where they were going or what they would be doing.

    Clint led Kate to the room they’d be staying in that night.

    “This is nice, I was kinda thinking the rooms would be like a cell or something,” Kate said, dumping the bag of her things onto one of the beds, while Clint placed his on the other.

    “Yeah,” he agreed, rooting through his bag, “last time I stayed here was a long time ago, they had just put the tunnels in, I had to sleep on a makeshift hammock.”

    Kate grimaced, “Yeah, I think I’ll take this room over that.”

    Clint grinned at her, before returning his attention to his bag, he pulled out a small tablet and began to press on the screen, “This should work down here, here I’ll pull up Laura’s number,” he did so and held the tablet out to Kate, but she didn’t take it. “Kid, you promised you’d talk to her.”

    “I know I just…,” she sighed and took it, “Was she… was she okay, when you told her about… me?”

    Clint gave Kate a look she couldn’t quite name, a mix of pity, confusion, and sadness mixed into a small sad smile.

    “Kate, she’s made up, beyond made up, we both are. To have found you… we never thought we would. I know she’s looking forward to you coming home, though when we do she probably won’t want to let go of you, so be prepared to stay home until you’re about thirty- five.”

    Kate let out a small laugh, looking down at the photo of Laura on the screen. “I never… I’m not really used to having a home, like yours, I had my apartment and that was awesome, or my friends’ dorms, and the house, but yours is the first to feel, I don’t know… like in movies where the hero goes back to their house and they let out a sigh and everything’s just going to be… alright..” She finished lamely.

    Clint smiled warmly at her, “Kate, our home is always going to be your home, always .

    You’re always going to have somewhere to come home to, somewhere to live, I wish you’d had it the whole time, but you’ve got it now Kate.”

    Kate looked down to hide her face, but her smile was clear on her face as she glanced up, “I guess I need somewhere to stay for a while anyway, my apartment’s kinda destroyed,” she glanced up again and saw the smile on Clint’s face.

    Kate got dressed into her packed pjs, then settled onto the bed as Clint left to go shower. She knew that he was just leaving to give her space, it was kind of sweet of him to pretend that’s not what he was doing, though she wondered if he was better at pretending when he had been a spy.

    She let out a sigh then pressed call, the screen rang a couple of times before Laura’s face appeared, the woman broke into a smile, and Kate could have sworn she saw tears in her eyes. Kate felt a lump in her throat, looking at her mom for the first time.

    “Hey Kate,” Laura’s voice was slightly broken sounding.

    “Hey… er… Laura..?” Kate held back a grimace, this was suddenly a lot more awkward then Laura and her had ever been, she found herself wishing that Clint was there to break the ice. “I know, this isn’t… Well, it’s not what any of us expected when Clint told me he’d be bringing you home with him a few days before Christmas… but honey, I can’t even… I don’t have the words to say how happy I am to have finally found you. I’m so, so sorry that we weren’t there for years, but we’re going to find out who took you, and make them sorry.”

    Kate smiled slightly, “I’m pretty sure Clint’s banned me from going anywhere near any of them.”

    “I don’t blame him…”

    Kate’s heart sank.

    “But you have a right to find out, just as much, more even, than we do. I don’t want you anywhere near danger either, but you’re like Clint, you’re stubborn as hell, so I know us telling you that you can’t do something won’t stop you.”

    Kate gave a tiny laugh, her heart lightened that she was on her side, “He said that I was stubborn like you, earlier.”

    Laura rolled her eyes, “He’s wrong, I’m just always right.”

    Kate laughed, the awkwardness beginning to fade to the background, “Did he really used to drive dangerously?” She watched Laura grimace and begin to tell Kate about a mission the two had gone on the first time they’d met, leaving out anything which was meant to be kept secret.

    Kate just watched with a smile as Laura talked, allowing herself to lie down on the bed and just listen, imagining that she had heard Laura telling her stories years ago, her adoptive mother had told her one story - the lie that Kate was hers, but no others since Derek had died.

    “How’re you doing Kate?”

    “Huh, oh I’m okay.”

    Laura was silent for a few seconds, “You can talk to me, about anything, about how you’re feeling about all of this, you don’t have to worry about offending me, or you can,” her voice broke slightly, she cleared her throat to get it back on track, “you can talk to your father , we won’t judge anything that you’re thinking or feeling.”

    It was Kate’s turn to be silent then. “I don’t think I am… feeling anything that is, not right now, I mean I’m angry, I’m pissed someone took your, erm, me , and that I was lied to for, well, my whole life, but I’m not really… connected? to that… I just want to know what happened.”

    She couldn’t tell if Laura was relieved, or if she was hoping for a heart to heart, or a dot to dot about why Kate was so messed up, but she nodded.

    “I get that, just… just don’t keep it all locked inside of you, you’ll burn yourself from the inside out if you do that, okay?”

    “Got’it.” Kate said through a yawn.

    “I’ll let you get some sleep honey.”

    “I’m okay,” Kate protested through another small yawn.

    “It’s okay, I’ll speak to you tomorrow morning, before you two head into the city.”

    Kate could tell that wasn’t a request to talk then, but an order, “Clint still hasn’t told me who we’re going to see there, do you know?”

    “One of Clint’s friends, well, acquaintances…,” she looked like she was thinking for a second, “somewhere between those two things.”

    That didn’t clear it up at all for Kate, but before she could say so the door opened, Clint gave her a smile nodding back at the tablet, allowing Kate to have the time with her mom for the first time in over twenty years.

    “Kate?”

    Kate looked back at the screen, at her mom.

    “I know I don’t have the- any role in it, but I’m so proud of how you’ve grown up, so, so, proud ,” her voice broke again but this time she let it.

    Kate pulled the covers over her, hoping the low light would hide the tears forming in her eyes.

    “I can’t wait to get to know you, to spend time with you, but I’m just so… I’m so proud of you Kate, so happy that we found you, that you found us. I can’t wait to get to be your mom, Kate. I love you.

    Kate couldn’t find her voice, she didn’t know what to say, she was good at filling silences, but this time she couldn’t. She didn’t wipe the fallen tear as it rolled from her right eye, across her nose and onto the pillow.

    But Laura didn’t seem upset, or angry, or even hurt, at Kate’s silence, she just smiled as softly as Kate had seen her smile at Nate, “Night, honey, sweet dreams.”

    “Night Laura,” Kate managed out in a hoarse voice, she felt raw, and… just everything, at that moment in time. She reluctantly made herself hang up, and slipped the tablet onto the night stand.

    She pulled the covers up so she was only able to get air not to be seen.

    “Sleep tight Katie.”

    Clint’s voice was soft, she was barely able to hear it, though that could have been all of the blankets acting as a buffer, she pulled them tighter around her, “Night Clint,” she told him so quietly that she wasn’t sure if he would be able to hear her. She didn’t repeat it any louder. When she fell asleep that night she dreamt about Eleanor and Derek, and the house built on sand they had lived in.

    They were not pleasant dreams.

    They were not dreams at all.
