    as always thank you so much for the comments and the kudos and your support <3 See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate groaned as she was shaken awake, she tried to move away from the hands, but when they persisted she forced her eyes open, “Wha’?” She let out, trying her best not to sound too annoyed, which granted wasn’t her trying that hard.

    “You were having another nightmare,” Clint told her, perching on the side of her bed as he looked down at her.

    Kate forced the heels of her palms into her eyes, trying to scoop the sleep out, “Another?” “You’re gonna hurt your eyes,” he told her gently, pulling her hands away from them, “Yeah, another. You don’t remember me waking you up a few times?”

    Kate shook her head, “No,” looking away from him so he wouldn’t see her worry. “Did I say what happened in them?”

    Clint hesitated for only a second, “No, you said you didn’t remember them, why?”

    “Just wondering,” she sat up in the bed, “Hey, what time is it?”

    Clint just looked at her for a couple of seconds, clearly evaluating her, "It's morning, we might as well get up now, I doubt you’ll get much more sleep.”

    She nodded then sat up, yawning, “I’m all good.”

    “You barely slept, kid, I doubt you’re ‘all good’… are you sure you don’t remember any of your nightmares?”

    “ Dreams ,” Kate told him sharper than she meant to.

    “ Katie , talking about them will help, trust me.”

    “I’m just one of those people who never remembers dreams,” she told him, “I don’t think I even dream.” She pushed herself to her feet, “I think I’m gonna take a shower, are there any towels I can borrow somewhere?”

    Kate closed the door to the bathroom Clint had brought her to, and placed her back to it as she heard the locks slide into place automatically, she dropped the clean clothes and fresh towel she had been holding onto the floor without a second thought.

    She sucked in air, she had never been claustrophobic, but since waking up from that… dream … she could feel the tons of dirt piled onto the tunnels they were in.

    She forced herself to move, she couldn't just stand then, she couldn’t crumble against the door, if she fell she knew she wouldn’t get back up again, and she couldn’t let Clint find her like that.

    She walked to the sink, she ran the cold tap, and began to scoop cold water onto her face, until she was once again gasping for air, but it was fresher now.

    She finally looked at herself in the mirror, the whites of her eyes were slightly bloodshot from the lack of sleep, and there were purple bags hanging under her eyes, her skin was pale, and the small cuts littering her face from Christmas Eve were starting to heal, she gently pulled off the strips which had been pressing the skin together, and flicked them into the bin across the room.

    She sucked in another slow breath, calmer than she had been when she had entered the bathroom, she looked into her blue eyes, and could see for the first time that they were only a shade away in colour from Clint’s irises.

    “It wasn’t real,” she told herself firmly, “it was just a dream. It’s gone, it’s over with,” looking into the blues of her eyes made it slightly easier to believe that it was done, only slightly.

    She sighed as she straightened up, “It’s not like you haven’t thought about it before,” she told the mirror Kate who was still looking too scared for Kate’s liking, “it’s no different than then, we’ve gotten through it all those times, chill.”

    The reflection did not chill, and Kate rolled her eyes and turned away from it. She had dreamt about the memories many times before, that was true, and she had always gotten over them, that was also true, but all those times she had been able to leave wherever she was and do something … normally something reckless, sneaking into places she shouldn’t be, picking a fight with someone who deserved it, getting kicked out of classes for smartass comments, or slinging two arrows at a bell tower, collapsing the whole thing.

    She couldn’t disappear and do something reckless right then, and she couldn’t do anything with Clint with her, he was already suspicious enough.

    So she took a shower scalding her skin with hot water, then scrubbing every inch of it with the Shield brand shower gels, until she could have sworn that she had replaced her old skin with a new layer of skin, then she threw the shower so it would be cold, cold enough to shock her system.

    She emerged from the shower a new person than she had been when she entered it, she emerged Kate: the person who had shot arrows at Kingpin, Kate who destroyed a belltower because of a dare, Kate who had befriended Hawkeye. She was every version of Kate she could be, all versions except the one who dreamt of the past.

    She found her way to the canteen Clint had pointed out to her on the way to the shower, and found him sitting at a table, talking on the tablet she had used the night before. She walked over to their bag which he had dumped on a couch nearby, she balled up the clothes she had changed out of and shoved them deep into the bag, before heading to join him.

    “Kate!” Nate’s voice shouted from the tablet as soon as the kid saw her, blatantly ignoring his father as soon as he saw her.

    Kate couldn’t help but smile at Nate as she slid into the seat beside Clint, “Hey Nate, how’s the city comin’ along?” Her eyes flicked on the screen to Laura who was sat beside him, Kate returned the smile Laura was already giving her, but was relieved that the woman didn’t take the tablet, not sure what she would say to her.

    “Awesome!” He grinned with pride, “I’m making it even bigger, Mommy said it’s gotta go in my room though,” he pouted and Kate gave a small laugh which she tried to hide. “Are you gonna be coming back here with my dad?”

    Kate felt like all eyes were on her as she nodded, both Nate and Laura beamed, she glanced up at Clint who was trying to hide his smile.

    “Yeah bud,” Clint added, “Kate’s actually gonna live with us for a while.”

    She knew that this was an opening, an opportunity for her to try and argue (and lose), or to embrace it, “Yeah, I’m still off college for the winter break… if they’ll even let me back,” that part was more to herself than anyone else, though could see Laura’s eyes move from hers to Clint’s on the screen, “and I have to teach your dad to be as awesome at archery as I am,” she glanced at Clint who rolled his eyes with a big smile.

    She and Clint talked to Laura and Nate for a little longer, Yelena was apparently in the snowy yard training, and both Cooper and Lila were making the most of the winter morning by sleeping. Eventually they hung up, and Clint showed her how to work the food fabricator for their breakfast.

    Kate fabricated herself a plate of gummy bear waffles, and a coffee which was more sugar than anything else, she pulled a face as Clint walked over with a dish of cereal and the largest cup of black coffee she had seen.

    “Seriously, you can have anything and you get cereal .”

    “I like cereal.”

    “But you could get anything! You didn’t even get the good cereal.”

    Clint raised his eyebrows and looked at her, clearly amused by what he thought was a childish meal, “Katie, I’m over forty, I can’t eat that,” he nodded to her breakfast, “as a meal because I’m not six.”

    “You just hate joy,” Kate glared at him and took a bite out of the top waffle, “and I know that you only call me Katie to try and annoy me.”

    Clint smirked as he took a sip of his coffee, “No idea what you’re talking about, Katie.” She continued to glare at him, taking another bite out of her waffle, “Sure you don’t, old man.”

    They ate a little, both clearly tired from the nightmares Kate was denying, but the meal was pleasant enough.

    “What did you mean, about your college?”

    Kate looked up at him, wiping the sugary syrup from around her mouth, “Er, well I collapsed that tower with the Stane Bell, which was so badly named, like that dude tried to kill Tony Stark and is literally a terrorist and war profiteer, but he gets to keep the bell?”

    “Oh, so you were doing a public service?”

    A proud smile appeared on Kate’s face despite the sarcasm in Clint’s voice, “It totally was, you’re right!,” she laughed as Clint did. “But they kinda suspended me, or kicked me out… I don’t know, my mo-er, Eleanor said she’d handle it, as long as I worked and she froze my accounts… then I put on the Ronin suit… and everything else happened.”

    Clint nodded slowly, clearly thinking something through, “I’ll sort it out,” he told her. “Are you sure I should be… I mean there’s so much going on, and if I become Hawkeye then it’s not like I’m even going to need a degree, Y’know?”

    Clint smiled at her, “I’m Hawkeye, so I say you have to get a college degree to become Hawkeye.” He shook his head with a smile as she rolled her eyes, “I can probably get it so you can study from home, so you can spend more time at home, if you want that?”

    Kate caught the bit of hope Clint tried to hide, and was surprised at her own relief, he wasn’t trying to get rid of her. “That sounds good, if I can. Is it cool with you if I…?”

    “Kate, me and Laura, and the kids, all want you to stick around, especially now.”

    She gave him a smile before turning her attention back to her coffee, but the smile stayed on her face… she liked the idea of a home.

    They said a short goodbye to Phil and Jemma Simmons, the only two who were not physically on mission.

    Clint insisted they Coulson come visit soon, bringing his team if he wanted, and Coulson looked touched, and kind of surprised that Clint hadn’t punched him once, despite ‘finding out’ that he was alive.

    “Where’re we goin’?” Kate asked as Clint led the way through the streets of Hell's Kitchen. “We’re gonna go see a lawyer,” he told her, he came to a stop and pulled Kate to his side so she wouldn’t walk into the road, a car passed them, right where Kate would have been.

    “That’s counting as you trying to give me a heart attack.”

    “No it doesn't,” she protested, “this is just how we cross sidewalks here, you just live in a place where there’s not even any people, let alone sidewalks.”

    Clint gave her a Look in a sideways glance, which told Kate that he didn’t agree in the slightest.

    “Why do we need a lawyer? I thought you said that my- that Eleanor didn’t adopt me legally?”

    “This guy’s more than a lawyer,” he told her with a smile, “After New York happened I stuck around to help clean up the place, it’s the least I could do.”

    “You already helped save us all,” she told him, softer and kinder than her usual teasing. Clint swallowed, coming to a stop, with Kate stopping soon after.

    “What’s-?”

    “I was compromised in New York, Kate,” he interrupted, talking quietly and without a hint of doubt, “before then - the tesseract, it was my job to watch it, Loki came, I tried to fight him… I failed, he took control of me. It was like I was locked inside of my brain, being unmade, someone else controlling me, using my knowledge to… all I could do was keep the information about my family secret, Loki would have used them against me. I helped him set things up for New York, I didn’t know it, but everything he had me doing was to get himself captured: Coulson’s death, everything he went through after that, that’s on me. The fact he managed to get to New York at all is partially on me, Katie. That was hard enough before, but now I know that you nearly died because of that, because of me.”

    Kate was quiet for a second looking down at the floor, before looking up at Clint, frowning in confusion, “You saved me, I saw you do it. You weren’t you when you were doing that, you know that, right?” She watched him nod, “So you knew that, you helped take him down, you saved me, you saved everyone, even in the middle of the fight you were the one saving buses of people… then after all of that you still came back and helped?”

    Clint looked at her as though he was trying to figure something out, and also like he was trying not to break.

    “What?”

    “Just… You’re pretty amazing Kate.”

    Kate wasn’t sure if that fully explained his look but she let it slide as he let her down the sidewalk once again.

    “I met this guy busted up in a dumpster.”

    “Wait, he was busted up or you were?”

    Clint thought for a second, “Yeah.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, “That doesn’t explain anything, thanks.”

    Clint led her into a pretty sparse building.

    “Where-?”

    But Clint cut her off with a shout, “Hey Red, you know that your sign’s been stolen, right?” He led Kate down a hallway into an office, where a dark haired man was staring out of a window.

    The man turned, revealing a smirk, and several cuts littering his face, matching the red of the round glasses he wore, “Foggy’s sorting it… I heard you got deaf?”

    “Yup, I see you’re still blind.”

    The two were silent for a second then laughed and met in a brief hug, clapping each other on the back.

    “Kate this is Matt Murdock, Matt, this is Kate.” He told him.

    “You’re his partner?” Matt asked, holding out his hand for Kate to shake.

    “I don’t… Are we still?” She asked Clint, “‘Cause we got done with that case, and you said we were then, but now it’s all-”

    “We’re working to figure something out,” Clint said clearly, as though it cleared up a single thing.

    Matt didn’t seem too bothered by this lack of an answer, or perhaps he was used to it, instead he gestured to the seats for the three of them to sit down.

    “Can I just… Why did you two meet in a dumpster?”

    Matt let out a breath of laughter, “Clint’s a smartass who gets himself beat up by saying stupid shit. I’m guessing he’s still the same.”

    “And Matt watched too many ninja movies as a kid.”

    Matt rolled his eyes behind his glasses, he looked in Kate’s direction for long enough to tell her that he was trying to figure something out, “I’m the Daredevil, I didn’t give myself the name, but it’s kind of stuck.”

    “Holy shit! That’s so cool! Dude, that’s awesome, wait you’re… you’re blind, not that blind people can’t be superheroes, or anyone, but how do you-?”

    “- There was a nasty chemical spill when I was a kid, it blinded me, but gave me other powers, I have enhanced hearing, some other senses, I honed them as best I could. The world to me looks like it's on fire, or dripping in blood.”

    “I forgot how depressing you are to talk to,” Clint muttered.

    Kate shot him a look.

    But Matt laughed, “Yeah, Foggy’s been trying to train me out of that, he got me a knock knock book.”

    “Oh,” Kate wasn’t sure if the book was a good or a bad thing.

    “Yeah…” Matt agreed with a grim smile, before turning his attention to Clint. “What’s up? I thought you’d retired?”

    “A few times now,” Clint agreed. “I need a favour.”

    Matt nodded at him to go on.

    “I need you to try and find out if there’s any trafficking in Hell’s kitchen, or based here but happening elsewhere.”

    “There is, or there was, I put a stop to it. Have you heard something?”

    “No, nothing, the case isn’t recent.”

    Kate watched Matt grimace, then look sad, regretful, Kate knew they were losing him.

    So did Clint. “Matt, it was a kid.”

    Matt sat up straighter in his chair, looking alarmed… no… angry , “When?”

    “October 2002,” Clint told him, his voice had turned to stone from emotion, “Sixteen months old, dark shoulder length hair, blue eyes, she could talk, and she was used to being around only certain people, she’d talk their ears off but she didn’t trust strangers, she wouldn’t have been fine being with the people who took her.”

    Matt had been staring ahead at Clint, but turned his head to Kate’s still form.

    He tilted his head slightly, “Is it you?” he smiled softly as surprise ran through her, “I can’t see the colour of your hair or eyes, but you got this… burst , in your heart, it quickened as Clint spoke.”

    “Matt-”

    “And she’s your daughter, right?” Clint didn’t say anything but Matt nodded, “Your heart was too steady while you were telling me, out of training, it was unnatural, you gave yourself away.”

    “You’re right.” Kate told Matt, ignoring that Clint was trying to avoid answering the lawyer, “We only just found out, I don’t, I was too young to remember anything. My mom, the person who brought me up, she had these documents about me, and then we did a DNA test.

    I'm a Hawkeye.”

    Matt nodded slowly, allowing a small smile at her attempt at levity, “Did you ever get any hints while you were growing up? Any overheard conversations, or feelings that something was going on?”

    Kate tried to still her heart, “No.”

    Matt stared at her, silently, Kate knew he didn’t believe her in the slightest. “Okay,” he told her anyway. “Well… I’m gonna be honest with you both, this was a long time ago, most people involved in it are probably dead, or missing, or they fled during those five years we were all gone, as you know Clint.”

    “There’s gotta be someone who knows something, and if they were trafficking into New York directly Hell’s Kitchen would have been the place, I’ve got other feelers out for everywhere else, but you know this place the best… Matt, Kate’s kidnapper had something to do with Wilson Fisk… Yeah I figured that’d interest you.”

    “That’s not possible.”

    “I saw him in a photograph taken last week, and Kate fought him a few days ago.”

    “You did?”

    “Yeah, he’s… I shot arrows at him and he acted like I had just thrown glitter. I’m not his biggest fan.”

    Matt looked visibly paled, he took his glasses off, and nodded, “I can…I’ll look into it, but once Fisk is done using people he kills them, we’re going to be really lucky if anyone even knows about who took you, but I can guarantee anyone involved with it is long gone.”

    “Yeah,” Clint sighed, unsurprised but still disappointed.

    Kate frowned, “There is someone.”

    “Who?” Matt frowned, looking from Clint to Kate.

    “Eleanor Bishop.”

    Clint got to his feet, as though having a visceral reaction to her name alone, “No.”

    “What do you mean no? We need someone who knows what happened and she’s the person who knows.”

    “You’re right, but we’re not using her until we have to. And you’re not seeing her at all.” “What! But- I’m the one who thought of it!” She knew it was a childish argument the second it was out her mouth.

    “I had already thought of it, Laura, your mom, already thought of it, Phil thought of it, hell Matt probably already had the thought, but none of us said it because we all know it’s a last resort.”

    “But she’ll know, she’ll tell us!” Kate got to her feet now too.

    “No, Kate, she won’t tell us,” he kept his voice purposely calm, “and she won’t tell you at all, because you’re not seeing her.”

    Matt stood up, mumbling something about looking for foggy before leaving the pair in his office, glaring at each other.

    “I’m the only one she’ll tell. I know her, I know how to work her, I can get her to tell me!” Her voice sounded both desperate and excited to her own ears, but she couldn’t stop it, she couldn’t get herself to sound calmer like Clint did.

    “Kate she didn’t even tell you that you were adopted, she’s not going to tell you this. Any of us going to see her would be reckless, if not straight up dangerous, we don’t know who’s watching, and even if it wasn’t all of that you still would not be going. That’s it, end of story, you are not seeing her.”

    Red like Matt had described blinded her vision, but she knew there was no chemical spill around, just the wall of her temper being hit.

    “ You don’t get to suddenly come into my life, decide that you’re my ‘dad’, and suddenly tell me what I can do!” She didn’t mean to explode, she didn’t even mean all those words, okay she did but now how it sounded. She stared at him, wide eyed.

    Her stomach tied itself into knots as he didn’t speak, he just stared back at her.

    What felt like an hour later, but was probably under a minute, Clint finally spoke, his voice quiet with forced calm, “I’m not trying to come into your life and tell you what to do, Kate, I’m really not… but when the thing that my kid wants to do is go and talk to the person who kidnapped her, and then later tried to kill the pair of us, and lied to her for twenty years, I get to step in and tell my kid that she’s not going to the secure prison to talk to her. So drop it.” “She might not have even actually kidnapped me,” she didn’t know what sort of maligned loyalty made her speak up on Eleanor's behalf but she did, or maybe it was just because Clint had said the words.

    “I don’t care if she was the one who physically stole you, she held you away from us, she lied to you your whole life, she didn’t even tell you now!” For the first time he was shouting, his frustration had built up like a pressure cooker, “You can hate me all you like for telling you what to do, but you are going to be following that rule, I don’t care how old you are. You. Are. Not. Going. To. See. Her.”

    Kate felt the tears in her eyes begin to form, but both Clint and Laura were right, she was stubborn, “But I could get her to talk, I’m the only person who can, you have to trust me-” “I said no, Nicky!” Clint exploded, his eyes were wide and his brows low with his fury, it took him seconds to realise his mistake, but it was seconds too late, “Shit. Kate, Katie, I’m sorry, I didn’t mean to-”

    “It’s okay,” Kate’s voice was quiet, she kept her eyes on the ground, she was clenching her jaw as she tried to keep herself from shedding a tear.

    “It’s not, it’s really not, Kate, I-”

    “I said it’s fine, Clint. I’ve dropped it, I swear, you don’t need to do that.”

    “Kate, I’m not apologising to get you to shut up. I’m apologising because I screwed up, I shouldn’t have… can you look at me, please?”

    Kate looked up at him, with her features schooled a little too well, given that she should never have had to develop that skill, not in the life she was supposed to have led…

    “ Kid , I didn’t mean to call you that name, I know it’s, you’ve only just found out that’s your real name, and that you might not even,” he shook his head, “I don’t… I don’t know what I’m trying to say.”

    “Then, can we drop it?”

    Clint sighed, “Just… you’re Kate , I know that, and you can be anyone you want, it doesn’t change who you are to me, how important you are.”

    He stared at her, willing her to get it.

    She looked away.

    An hour later then had left Nelson and Murdock law office and had made their way to the remnant of Kate’s old apartment, they had been once, but wanted to make sure there was nothing left, now they were sat outside of Kate’s other old home, the New York style castle. “If you tell me what you want I can grab it for you, you don’t have to come in if you don’t want,” Clint offered her softly, not for the first time since he had told her he intended on stopping there to search for any information.

    Kate shook her head, quieter since their fight, hating herself for blowing up on Clint, and way too close to having to think about being Nicole Barton for her liking. “It’s okay, you’ve gotta search her office anyway, I’m just gonna grab some stuff, and say hi to Jack…, “She stared out of the window as she spoke. “I don’t really wanna come back here.”

    “Kate?” Clint’s voice was too quiet and too soft, it made Kate feel raw.

    She turned to look at him, knowing it was a bad idea to do so, but unable to stop herself. “I know you don’t like it here, but… it was okay growing up here, right?”

    Kate could see how careful he was trying to be, but there was the Spy Clint Barton in his eyes, digging into her own. But she was biologically the daughter of two spies, including him, and she had been training for this question her whole life. “I was made to go to debutant things, and wear dresses way too often, but I go to go to the archery range, so I guess it wasn’t complete hell ,” she put on extra drama and a smile, telling him it was okay, he gave her a small smile back.

    Kate packed things randomly from her room, she wasn’t really connected to the space, it had been designed by people who had never met her, and she had been made to pose in it for interior design magazines, but other than the odd night here and there, this wasn’t her room. She was grateful she had left certain things here, at least they hadn’t blown up with the rest of her old place, so she dumped a couple of photo albums, a file box of something helpfully labelled ‘Stuff’ and random other knickknacks and memories into a few of the boxes Clint had brought.

    She dumped some clothes and some of her old tech into another box, she wasn’t sure if she’d wear any of the things again, they felt tainted, but maybe Lila or Yelena would like some of them.

    A loud knock sounded at the door, making her jump.

    Jack stood, sheepishly, for possibly the first time in his life.

    They had already exchanged pleasantries when she and Clint had arrived, he was also packing up his stuff, not wanting to stay here either, Kate didn’t blame him.

    “Hey, are you done?”

    “Hmm, nearly, it’s hard going through everything,” his eyes flickered to the boxed with just random fabrics balled up, “I dare say you have the better idea.”

    She shrugged, “Yeah, I don’t know if I want any of it, I… I don’t want to come back here,” she admitted to him.

    He nodded back at her, “I must admit it is harder than I expected, I thought that it would be easier, as she did frame me for murder, but everything reminds me of the good times.”

    Kate let out a mix of a snort and a scoff, but Jack didn’t question it.

    “I hope we’ll see each other again, Katie- sorry, I know you hate that name.” he shook his head at his own stupidity.

    Kate didn’t disagree because he was right, she did hate the name… except when Clint said it, apparently, “I could always use a fencing partner, if I’m ever here.” She was rewarded with a beaming smile, and she felt bad that she had hated him.

    In another lifetime, perhaps he would have made a good father figure, or at least a step dad who didn’t get too involved and didn’t snitch on her when she was high or drunk.

    “My card,” he had magicked a card from somewhere, and passed it over to her, “my number is on there, should you ever need me Katherine.”

    “I’ll text you, Jack,” she smiled wryly.

    He turned to leave when inspiration struck.

    “Wait! Jack?”

    “Yes?”

    I don’t suppose… are you going to see her, at all?”

    He stiffened, “She has extended an invitation, I don’t think I’ll take her up on it, however.” Kate let out a disappointed sigh, before looking up at the man once more, “Jack, did she ever tell you that she adopted me?”

    His face filled with surprise, “No? When did you find this out? Did she tell you from prison?”

    She was touched that he looked appalled at the idea of her being told that way, but she shook her head, “I discovered some things about it, a friend of mine found it out. I’m trying… I’m trying to find some things out about it… I can’t go see her, but maybe you could ask her if she could email me or something? If you talk to her, or her lawyer, I don’t want to force you to-”

    “I’ll ask her directly, does she have your contact details?”

    “She should know them… Jack… why did you just agree to help me, just like that? I mean I was kinda a dick to you, I was happy you were being sent down for murder, I was all for it!” He smiled at her in the way he always did, as though she was some form of deeply amusing creature, but this time it was somehow not patronising, “I enjoyed our sparring Kate, physical and verbal.”

    Kate couldn’t help but smile, she gave him a quick hug, surprising both of them, “I’ll catch you around Jack.”

    The next knock on the door came not even ten minutes after Jack had gone, this time it was softer, and Clint stood on the doorway, not wanting to push in, “How’s it going?”

    Kate thought about lying to him but felt bad, so she just shrugged, “I’m not even sure if I’m gonna want any of this.”

    He nodded, “We can store it, you can figure it out later. I packed up some things I saw downstairs, photographs, that sort of thing, I put them in the car.”

    Kate bit her lip and nodded, him being there felt wrong, she felt the overwhelming urge to flee with Clint, instead she looked hopefully at him, “Could you help me get the last bits into the boxes?”

    It was an official olive branch and Clint took it with enthusiasm. He helped pack up everything she told him to, asking questions about the medals, then about other things, until they had both forgotten about their earlier argument.

    Clint groaned loudly, shutting his eyes, and squeezed his face together in a grimace, “You weren’t meant to hear that.”

You used to cry when I sang when you were a baby.” Kate laughed again, before her face twisted to a different one, happy, yet this was something completely alien to her, “I never heard stories about when I was a baby, I mean I guess I know why now.”

    Kate was confused for only a second, before following his sight to Laura stood outside of the door to the house, waiting for them… for her… fear ran through her spin, yet for some reason she still smiled, before looking back at Clint.

    He tilted his head to the side, “You ready for this?”

    Clint nodded slowly, “Only if you’re ready, kid, they already love you, even more knowing that you’re their sister.”

    Kate nodded again, slowly. “I guess we better go see Laura.”

    Kate had hugged her only the day before, when both Clint and Laura were completely sure that they were her parents, but now… Kate felt tears sting in her eyes, so she buried her face against Laura’s shoulder.

    She wrapped her arms around her mother.

    “I knew it, the moment I saw you with Clint, I could just tell,” Laura whispered to her. “I am so sorry we weren’t there, I’m sorry we let you get taken.”

We want to help you, don’t keep this all inside.”

    “Clint told me the same thing,” Kate admitted.

    “He can be a smart man.”

    “Dammit!”

    Kate and Laura turned to look at Clint’s shouting, and realised that he had walked into the big tree trunk.

    Laura looked back at Kate, who was raising an eyebrow at her mother.

    “He can sometimes be a smart man,” Laura amended.

    Kate let out a small laugh, “I used to think he was like an… an indestructible hero, like super cool badass hero, who nothing bad could happen to, that he never missed a shot, that he was as cool in real life as he was when he was fighting aliens.”

    “And now you’ve been disappointed?” Laura guessed with a laugh.

    “Nah… he still lives up to it, even if he is super annoying, just don’t tell him I said all those nice things.”

    “Of course,” Laura wrapped her arm around Kate’s shoulder to lead her into the house, “we don’t want to inflate his ego.

    Chapter End Notes be nice or ill cry thanks and please <3 The Roof Chapter Summary Kate ends up on the roof Chapter Notes You guys were so sweet with the last chapter thank you all so much, i love and adore each and every one of you <3 See the end of the chapter for more notes The cold night air nipped at Kate as she sat watching the stars, she had never seen so many, nor a sky so black. She knew that there were stars in New York, she had even gone to an observatory on a school trip one time , but at night out of her bedroom window the sky was lilac, beautiful in its own way, but it couldn’t beat all these stars.

    The only downside was that she was cold, she should have grabbed a jacket or coat before climbing onto the roof, granted she was fairly sure the Bartons would have some weird alarm go off when she got up there so she didn’t expect to stay for as long as she had, they must have turned any alarm off for Santa.

    She leant back against the roof tiles, staring at the stars and trying not to think, she was so tired of thinking, so so tired .

    Laura and Clint had told their children who she was earlier that night, she had insisted on not being there, not knowing how they’d react, but she couldn’t stop herself from listening in from the staircase, the same way she used to listen to Eleanor and Derek’s fights.

    The Barton children had all been confused: Nate had seemed the happiest, he didn’t get all the ins and outs of how complicated it all was, to him he had just gained another sister, who came with a dog. It was a win-win to the six year old, in fact Clint had to stop him from running up to find her, telling him that Kate needed some sleep, she wasn’t sure whether to be happy that Clint understood what she needed, or pissed that he did.

    She wasn’t used to being pissed at Clint, she didn’t like it, but it seemed that everything he did annoyed her right then. She hated that.

    Lila had been the most vocal, she hadn’t been against Kate, but she had been the most outwardly uncertain, she had asked if that was why Clint had stayed in New York, if Kate had known, seeming a little uncertain despite Clint and Laura’s assurances that Kate had only just found out. She asked if Kate would be living with them now, if she was going to be going by Nicky Barton again, who took Kate, why they took her, if they were all in danger… Most of the questions they answered with ‘I don’t knows’, except if she would be going by Nicky, Clint had said he doubted it, that she had been Kate for twenty years, they weren’t sure if she would want to change her last name, but both were up to her.

    Kate found herself relieved that they didn’t expect her to change her name, she didn’t even want to think about that right then, not yet, though she also couldn’t think about her current last name, and what, who , it stood for.

    Cooper, the oldest, the one who had been the oldest, until she turned up again, had been quiet. He had only spoken when Clint had asked him what was on his mind, what he thought Cooper had been quiet, before asking if she was okay.

    She left the staircase and went to her room when she heard that, she didn’t want to know what Laura and Clint would answer.

    She had gone to her new bedroom, she half unpacked one of the boxes of stuff Clint had brought up, before she gave up, she didn’t feel like it. Instead she led on the bed, when she heard Laura come up to check on her she pretended to sleep, she stayed there with her eyes closed trying to keep her mind clear, for hours, listening carefully as Clint tiptoed into her room, and pulled the blankets over her.

    Even when Yelena crept into her room she stayed quiet and still, she heard the sound of a plate on the bedside table, Yelena say something in Russian, then disappeared from her room once again.

    When she eventually did move she saw that Yelena had left her a piece of cake on the plate. Kate smiled at it but had no appetite, instead she walked to the window, she looked out at the stars, before she finally had the idea to climb onto the roof.

    There was a noise to her right, her head snapped, expecting Yelena, or Clint, or even Laura…. But it was Cooper, climbing onto the roof from his own window, just like she had. Like her he was dressed in pjs, though unlike her he had brought a hoodie with him, he looked across at her in surprise, but when she sent him a small smile he walked across the tiles and dropped beside her, allowing his legs to dangle off the side.

    He didn’t say anything, he just stared out at the view, as she had been.

    She shivered slightly, as she tried to think of what to say to her new brother, but she couldn’t find any words, until she realised he was holding out the hoodie to her. She took it, shrugging it on, before looking down at it, it was a grey one, with the shield eagle across the front, and ‘Barton’ on the left side of the chest.

    “When I was a kid my dad would leave me with his hoodies when he had to go away to work, because he’d usually be gone for a while,” Cooper told her, glancing at her with a small smile, before looking out over the snow covered fields, “he told me it was his favourite football team, when I would ask what it meant… I don’t know why he and mom were surprised when I figured out that they were spies when I was younger.”

    Kate let out a small laugh, “I don’t get how Shield stayed so secret, like even their cards have the eagle on it.”

    Cooper laughed, “Yeah, I don’t know, I mean my mom, er, our mom I guess, was always better at hiding it, dad kinda sucked at lying to me about it.”

    Kate pulled her knees to her chest, wrapped her arms around them, she smiled slightly at Cooper's words, but realised she could still smell Clint on the hoodie. “I guess it must be weird,” she offered, giving him an opening to talk about it, “not the spy thing, though I guess also that, but… this?”

    Cooper gave a snort of laughter, “Yeah it’s just above our parents being superspies, and just below being turned into dust and appearing again five years later.”

    Kate let out a sardonic laugh, “Yeah, I can see that being weirder,” she shook her head slightly, “I can’t imagine being gone…I wasn’t, I mean I guess you figured that.”

    “Yeah, you should have only been eighteen months older than me, I would have been an adult,” he shook his head but he was smiling slightly, the same way Kate had seen Clint do it, “I can’t imagine being that old, or having a big sister.”

    “If it helps I’m not used to having any brothers or sisters,” she shared a smile with him, “And I’m like super chill, seriously I’m not going to suddenly jump in and be some controlling older sister.”

    “Good cause Li already claimed that role, I think she forgets I’m older than her.”

    Kate smiled at the fondness in his voice, “Wait eighteen months, so your birthday’s in October?” in her head she tried to slot into place timelines.

    “Nope, December, eighteen months.”

    Kate frowned.

    “December second… your birthday is June eighth, mom was pregnant with me when you went missing.”

    “No, I’m… my birthday’s in March…. March fifteenth two thousand and two,” a look of pain appeared on his face, and she knew that she had been lied to yet again, she let out a laugh which surprised even her, “This is all so fucked!” She leant back against the tiles again, taking in the stars, and pulled down the arms of the hoodie so they were covering her hands.     Kate shook her head, “The sky’s too polluted, I went to an observatory when I was a kid, it was cool but… I like these stars better.”

    “No one really comes up here, other than me… you can share it with me, if you like?”

    Kate sent him a smile, “I might take you up on that, when I’m here.”

    “Neither am I,” she picked up the piece of cake and took a large bite, then passed it to Yelena. “I’m sorry I haven’t been around.”

    “It’s okay, I’ve got a plan… but if I tell you, don’t tell Clint, please.” She sent Yelena a hopeful look.

    Yelena frowned ever so slightly, “But I thought that you and ‘Daddy Hawk’ were a team?”

    “I will pay you to never say ‘Daddy Hawk’ ever again for as long as we both live.”

    “Your accounts are still frozen. You and he are a partnership still, right? I thought that he was helping you, and your new mother, she is a superspy, not as good as me but then who is? She is helping too, right?”

    Kate looked down, frowning, before looking up, biting her lip, “I… they are helping me, they’re…I want to contact Eleanor, she can help us, she knows everything , but then Clint got all…” She shrugged, pulling a face, “Dad like?” She shook her head, “Which I guess makes sense because… yeah, but he won’t let me, we kinda got in an argument.”

    Yelena narrowed her eyes ever so slightly, “And you disobeyed him, I assume.” She shoved more cake into her mouth.

    Kate rolled her eyes, “No, I didn’t, if I was ‘ disobeying ’ him I would have just gone to the prison where she is, but I didn’t, I just asked her ex-fiance to tell her to email me, nothing Clint can have a go at me for!” She scrunched up her face, “Right?”

    Yelena raised an eyebrow, “If you’re questioning the consequences you’re probably going to end up facing them.”

    Kate threw her head back, Yelena had just confirmed what she had thought, and she knew Clint would be mad, or worse disappointed.

    “But, Kate Bishop,”

    Kate looked at Yelena.

    “The question is, is the information you will receive worth these consequences?”

    Kate bit her lip, then nodded, “I think so, yes, it is. It’ll keep Clint and Laura safer, I don’t want them having to make deals to try and find this out, or putting themselves in danger… I don’t want you in danger either.”

    “You are… worried about me being safe?”

    “Of course, we’re like… I think you’re kind of my best friend now?”

    A small smile appeared on Yelena’s face, “We are… friends?”

    “If you want to be?” Kate tried not to sound too hopeful, but she was certain that she failed. “I would like that a lot,” Yelena beamed, she passed the plate with the last bite of cake to Kate.

    Kate happily took it, and popped it into her mouth, “What have you been up to here?”

    “I have been sorting through things which Natasha left here, Laura kept them safe. I am making sure to keep things which she wouldn’t have wanted our parents to see here, the rest I am going to take home with me.”

    “You’re,” Kate startled, “You’re going home? Like… home home , like Russia?”

    “Yes.”

    Kate felt her heart fall, “That’s er, that’s great, that’s awesome!”

    Yelena tilted her head an inch to the side, “But I will be coming back, Kate Bishop.”

    “You will?”

    “Of course, we have business to finish up, Kate Bishop, and I would like to see more of America, and I have enjoyed my time here, with you.”

    Kate ducked her head to hide how wide she was smiling, “I… good, cool, really cool Yelena whatever your last name is.”

    “Belova,” She told her, “I have a few days before I fly out, Kate Bishop, perhaps we can spend more time together?”

    Kate looked up at her, “I’d like that, Yelena Belova.”

    “You know you didn’t answer my question earlier.”

    “What question?”

    “If you are still wanting to be a Bishop.”

    Kate thought for a minute, “I’m not sure yet, I’ll let you know when I figure it out.”

    “Would you like me to stop calling you Kate Bishop, until you have figured this out?”

    Kate was surprised at the question, but it was a pleasant surprise, she took hold of Yelena’s hand, not missing the way her other hand reached for a weapon out of instinct, then stopped. She didn’t move her hand from Kate’s.

    “ You can call me Kate Bishop.”

    She couldn’t see anyone around, but she heard the sizzle of something in coming from the kitchen, so she followed the noise, she found Clint in the kitchen with his back to her, he was clearly making brunch, and Kate would be lying if she claimed not hungry as soon as she smelt the bacon.

    “Hey, Kid, I was about to wake you-,” Clint’s words faltered as he turned and saw her wearing his old hoodie, his face broke out into a smile, he tried to hide it by turning his back again, pretending to check again on the bacon, “You hungry?”

    Kate thought about the cake she had just shared with Yelena an hour before, then nodded her head, having her life turned upside down was making her feel hungry. She slipped onto a stool, grabbing the closest mug of coffee and pulling a face when she sipped the nearly cold bitter coffee, which she knew by now was Clint’s “Gross, why don’t you ever put sugar and creamer in your coffee?”

    Clint raised an eyebrow, but laughed at the face Kate pulled, “Because I’m not eight.”

    Kate, very maturely, stuck her tongue out at her father.

    “Case in point,” but he clearly couldn’t resist poking a tongue back at her, smiling as she smiled. “I forgot I had that coffee, I’ve been drinking outta the jug,” he nodded beside him, and sure enough the carafe was nearly empty of coffee.

    Kate smiled at the sheepish way he admitted that, and at the way he had yet again been not so secretly staring at the hoodie again.

    “I talked to Cooper last night, I was cold so he… he er leant me your old hoodie, it was warm and comfy, I think I kinda stole it from him… and you?”

    She couldn’t look at him as she spoke, she glanced up, and saw the way Clint’s face was still, but his eyes were shining with tears, she looked down quickly, feeling a lump building in her throat. “Is there any more coffee?”

    “Ye-,” Clint coughed to clear his voice, “ Yeah , er, Laur finished setting up that coffee maker I got her for Christmas, it’s over there, there’s some of those pods of pure sugar you like in the drawer straight under it.”

    Kate headed over to it, rolling her eyes at what a coffee purest Clint was, she grabbed a ‘Hawkeye’ mug and pressed the button to start the coffee going.

    “Y’know, if you’re still cold, or whatever, I have more hoodies… if you want some…?”

    Kate glanced over at him, but the smile on her face too was too obvious for her liking, so she stared at the coffee maker again. “Really?”

    “Yeah, of course… all my kids have at least one…”

    Kate bit her lip, but her smile was so big that she couldn’t keep it under the control of her teeth, it broke free, “Yeah, I’d… I’d like that, if you have some spares.”

    The two were quiet as the only sound in the kitchen was the cooking of the food and the coffee machine, neither of them looked at each other, the two were too busy hiding their smiles.

    When the coffee finished she walked back over the island, looking around at the kitchen, “Do you need any help?”

    “Katie I spent enough time with you to know that you’re in serious need of cooking lessons.” Kate rolled her eyes at him, making him smile, “Maybe you could teach me, sometime… I mean you’re old and retired so it’s not like you have much else to do.”

    Clint laughed, “Deal, and just so we’re clear I didn’t retire because I’m old.”

    “Uh hmm,” Kate hummed, smiling as she sipped her coffee.

    “Laura’s out front with Nate, kid’s still not sick of the snow,” Clint shook his head with a smile firmly on his face, “you go grab them, I’ll wake the other two up.”

    Kate nodded and headed out of the kitchen, she slipped on her boots which were left by the door and heard Clint heading up the stairs, his voice calling after her.

    “Put a jacket on, it’s cold out there!”

    She let out a sigh, and somehow Clint must have heard her because he called again, now at the top of the staircase.

    “Take mine, it’ll keep you warm, if you get sick Laura won’t leave your side for a second.” That helped convince her, she wasn’t sure how she would cope with being looked after while sick, she was used to doing her own thing, especially while sick, she wasn’t sure she’d be able to slip past Laura.

    She slipped on Clint’s thick winter coat then slipped out of the door.

    More snow must have fallen since she had gone inside the night before. Because the fields were once again a flat thick blanket of snow, the only blemish were footprints, she followed them.

    Laura and Nate were building what appeared to be an army of snowmen, with Lucky running around, picking snow up, and throwing it into the air. Kate smiled at the three of them, they hadn’t noticed her yet, it was nice to just see them, without them seeing her, without Laura looking worriedly at her…

    Lucky had clearly fit into the family, seamlessly in fact, and Kate had to remind herself not to be jealous of a dog… Still, it wasn’t fair, he just… fit, whereas she was like a mismatched jigsaw piece being hammered into a hole.

    Then Nate turned, and caught sight of her, and within seconds the six year old was running towards her, almost falling a few times, but eventually he barrelled into her legs.

    “Kate!”

    “Hey Nate, whatcha up to? Snow superheroes?”

    “No!” Nate laughed as though she’d got it wrong on purpose, his gloved hand took her un-

    gloved one and dragged her to where Laura and the snow monsters stood, “Look! There’s mommy, and Cooper, and Lila, and there’s me, and daddy, and that’s you. We’re gonna make Lucky, you wanna help?”

    Kate could see now which ones were which, mostly because clothes had been stolen from laundry baskets to add to the realism, her eyes stuck on the snow version of her, stood between snow-Clint and snow-Laura, a smile made out of pebbles, and a bow and arrows made out of sticks and twine, identical to snow-Clint’s. “Woah, those are awesome!”

    “Yup, it’s our family, cause you’re my sister now.”

    “Er… yeah, I guess I am.”

    “Nathaniel, we said that we don’t need to talk about that right now.”

    Kate watched Nate slump under Laura’s Mom look, and she felt bad for the kid, if a little relieved that Laura had clearly been trying to make sure the point wasn’t pushed at her, she gently nudged Nate.

    “Your, erm, our? ” She tried not to sound too doubtful because it was the truth and Nate wasn’t a kid who would leave that unchallenged, “dad made food, maybe I can help you build a snow Lucky once we’ve eaten?”

    Kate wasn’t sure if it was the promise of her help, or the food which made Nate set off towards the house, running, and calling Lucky, who happily followed the boy.

    Kate was shaking her head watching them as she felt Laura’s arm loop through her own, she sent the woman a small smile, trying not to laugh when she saw that Laura had the same look on her face that Clint did, when they had both seen her in Clint’s stolen clothes.

    Laura was better at hiding it than Clint had been, at least, “Did you sleep okay?”

    Kate nodded her head, “Yup.”

    Laura was quiet for a second, “You ended up on the roof with Coop for a while, you can’t have slept that well.”

    “What, how’d you-” Laura sent her a look and Kate half rolled her eyes, “Right, you’re a spy.”

    “Yup,” Laura looked proud, she began to walk slowly with Kate, “I did stop Clint from hiding on the other side of the roof in case you two needed him though.”

    Kate threw her head back, “I’ve been on so many roofs, loads of times, I mean it’s not like a hobby, but I’m used to it, we were up higher in New York and he didn’t freak out.”

    “I’m sure he freaked out then too, Kate,” Laura told her not unkindly, “he just knew he couldn’t pull the ‘Dad’ card that he can with you now.”

    “He thinks a dad card would work?”

    “Yeah, he's deluded, but he’s cute so I stayed married to him.”

    Kate laughed, feeling her cheeks warm just from being with Laura. She met her mother’s caring eyes, she wasn’t wrong earlier when she knew that they would be worried for her, but it wasn’t an unpleasant knowledge. “I really did sleep okay, I just needed… I watched the stars, and Cooper talked with me a little bit.”

    “I’m glad you’re getting to spend some time with him, you would have grown up so close in age you would have either been best friends or hated each other.”

    Kate squeezed Laura’s arm in hers. “He’s cool. He must be a good big brother.”

    “He is. I mean he and Lila fight like cats and dogs, but he’d protect her with his life, if he had to. I’m just glad he doesn’t have to.”

    The pair walked in silence closer to the house.

    “I er… I was kinda listening, when you and Clint were telling the others about who I am.” Laura nodded slowly, “I figured you would be,” she was clearly more confused that Kate had admitted it.

    “I… Is Lila okay, y’know with me ?”

    Laura stopped in the snow, making Kate stop too, “How much did you listen to?”

    Kate shrugged, “Until you guys told Cooper,” she nudged the snow with her boot, watching it make lines in the snow, until Laura’s hand cupped her chin, forcing her to look up at her. “Honey, Lila was just worried.

    “She’s right to be, we don’t know who took me. Me being here could be putting you all in danger-”

    “ Katherine .”

    Kate stilled at the full name, it sounded foreign from Laura’s mouth, but in the same way hearing her called Nate ‘Nathaniel’ had been.

    “ You are not putting us in any danger. And even if there are people out there your father and I can take them all on, and we will if we have to, no one is going to touch a single hair on your head, on any of your heads. So don’t even think about leaving, or worry about having to, we just got you back, you’re not leaving here for a long time. Understand ?”

    Kate swallowed. She nodded her head, then cleared her throat, meeting Laura’s dark brown eyes, they were fierce with love, it was terrifying and overwhelming in… a good way, somehow?

    It was a lot, so Kate did what she always did, she sent her a smile, and a fake salute as she said, “Yes, ma’am.”

    Laura shook her head fondly, “You are so like your father.” She brushed a strand of hair from Kate’s face. “After you stopped listening she was mostly worried about you. I think you’re both pretty similar, that maybe you both just need some time to get used to this.”

    Kate nodded, and wondered how much time Lila would need to get used to everything… and how much time she would need.

    Kate had decided to actually sort through the boxes they had packed back in New York, about five minutes into doing so she regretted it, mostly because she seemed to have just packed a whole load of crap…

    She had thrown photo albums into a cardboard box and pushed it into the wardrobe. She couldn’t look at them yet, even if she knew that she should. It felt like a betrayal of Clint and Laura to look at them with any fondness: she had so few purely happy memories of childhood that she couldn’t risk destroying any, even if it was the right thing to do. It felt wrong to even have them in the Barton house, like a dirty secret, even if Clint had helped her pack them.

    She made a pile of childhood toys she no longer wanted, but that Nate might like, even if the Nintendo DS was very dated by now.

    There were some smaller things, gifts from the aunt’s whose apartment she and Clint had taken over, things Kate had loved when growing up, and were too important to her to have gotten rid of, they had been with the stuff she had taken from her dorm, safe from the eyes of her mother, Eleanor, who had tried to trash them when she was a child.

    Kate thought of her aunt, she hadn’t really been an aunt, not technically, she was the friend of Derek’s older sister, a sister he had lost when he was in college, the friend had always been there for him, and for Kate, she had taken over the role of sister for Derek, and the role of aunt to Kate. She was more relieved than she could express that Clint and Yelena had both (separately) ruled her Aunt out of any of Eleanor and Derek’s dirty business dealings, she was just a good person, who had an expansive movie collection.

    Kate should probably still talk to her at some point, to see if she knew anything, but that could wait until the new year, before she destroyed her aunt’s reality too.

    Clothes seemed to take up most of Kate’s boxes, and nearly all of them she wanted rid of. She threw the things she was keeping onto her bed: mostly hoodies, jeans, leggings, sweatshirts, and athleisure wear - along with the suit she had worn to the party where she had borrowed the Ronin suit, she threw the dress she had worn to the party she and Clint had gone to onto the bed too, she wasn’t super girly or into dresses, but it concealed her suit well, and was always good to have a just in case.

    There was a knock at the doorway, and when Kate turned she was surprised to find Lila there, holding a tray with something on it, and when she said “Hey!” her surprise finding her there was clear.

    “Hey,” Lila gave her a small smile in return, her eyes scanning the floor full of Kate’s things, “Dad said you were sorting some things out and I was bringing myself up a hot chocolate anyway so I figured you might want one?”

    Kate’s face broke into a big smile, “Yeah, I love hot chocolate, especially when it’s ex-” “-tra chocolatey,” The two said at the same time, Lila smiling wider at their shared interest. Lila passed her one of the mugs, along with one of the two plates of gingerbread house, “How’s sorting things going?”

    “I’m realising how many of these clothes I have never worn?” Kate offered, sipping her hot chocolate, “I don’t suppose you’d like any of them?”

    “Wait… seriously? They’re all brand new, you could probably sell them for a fortune.”

    Kate shrugged, she knew that her accounts were still frozen and she should probably talk to Eleanor’s lawyer about it, but to gain something from the clothes from Eleanor would mean that Kate still owed her, that’s how Eleanor would make it feel. “I’ve seen in movies that if I don’t share my clothes with you you’ll just steal them.”

    She knew there was hope in her voice, another olive branch outheld, and like Clint had the day previous, Lila laughed, taking the olive branch and sitting beside Kate on the floor.

    It was a little awkward at first but soon Lila was telling Kate all about the things she, their brothers, and their mom had done while Kate and Clint were in New York before Christmas, and then about her own life, her friends, her school, what she wanted to do in the future. “I wanna be a graphic designer,” she admitted, “I’ve tried to explain to dad about a hundred times how it’s different than like di Vinci’s style, but he acts like he doesn’t get it to try and bug me.”

    “That’s so cool! I mean, graphic design, never Clint, I mean he can be cool but I’m not telling him that.”

    Lila smiled knowingly at that before looking a little nervous before asking, “You think so?” “ Yeah ! It’s awesome, I have a friend who’s studying graphic design in California. I can see if they can chat to you about it, if you want?”

    “Seriously? That’s so cool I’d love that!”

    Kate beamed at Lila’s wide smile, relieved that despite her fears Lila seemed happy that she was around.

    “It must be, I mean… is it weird? All this?”

    Kate slowly nodded, “Yeah, I didn’t, I didn’t know anything I thought that, well I didn’t know I’d been adopted, let alone stolen. And your, er, Clint, I saw him years ago, when the aliens came to New York, he saved me, not that he knew that.”

    “I wish he’d known that you were there then. He could have brought you home sooner.”

    Kate’s blue eyes met Lila’s brown ones, she realised she had been the only one to inherit Clint’s eyes, “Me too,” her voice was barely above a whisper, surprised when the words escaped her mouth. “But hey, at least we know each other now.” she gave Lila a smile which fell short, Lila returned it, also falling short of hopeful.

    The two sat in silence until Clint’s head popped around the doorway, he looked worried, then confused to find both of them in the room, back to worried as his eyes darted around the room.

    “Hey girls… have you kids seen my hearing aid?”

    Both Lila and Kate shook their heads.

    “You lost it again ?”

    Clint rolled his eyes but was still looking nervous, “I only put it down for a few minutes.” Kate bit her lip to stop a smile when she saw how amused Lila looked, “Have you asked Laura?”

    Clint winced.

    “No, because mom will kill him for losing more of them.”

    Kate laughed at Clint’s playful glare to his youngest daughter, Lila was more like her than she’d first thought. That being said, she felt a little bad for Clint. “Nate was making a snow family earlier, he might have decided to make yours more realistic?”

    “Dammit,” He squeezed his eyes shut looking simultaneously relieved and grimacing, “thanks Katie.”

    Lila shared a smile with Kate before grabbing an armful of the clothes Kate had given her, “I’ll go distract mom.” This wasn’t the first time she’d done it.

    “Thanks, sweetheart,” he smiled warmly at her, shaking his head slightly at all the clothes she was carrying.

    “Do you need any help finding it?” Kate asked, she remembered what he’d looked like, unable to hear Nate on the phone, how pained he looked, at least in person he could lip read, but she still worried.

    “Nah, Nate’s taken them about eight times now, I really need to stop taking them out so I can nap.”

    “Why don’t you just turn them off, like you do when I annoy you?”

    “It’ll lose the effect of me deliberately blanking you if I turn them off all the time,” he smirked as Kate rolled her eyes, then hesitated. “How’s this goin’, kid?”

    “Okay, I was getting rid of some clothes, I figured Lila might like them.”

    He nodded slowly, thinking, “You know, you don’t have to get rid of everything right away, we can store some things in the basement, they’ll be safe, don’t make any rash decisions.” “I never make rash decisions,” she tried her best to keep a straight face, but Clint’s face didn’t change, so she just nodded her head, “I’ll let you know if I need anything stored.”

    He nodded, finally smiling at her, “We’re gonna watch a movie once Laura shouts at me, you comin’?”

    “Why’s Laura gonna shout at you?”

    “She’s definitely going to find out I lost my hearing aid again,” he explained with a simple shrug, he stayed standing there as she laughed.

    “I’ll be down in a minute.”

    “‘Kay, kiddo.”

    Kate watched him leave, then began to put things into piles in boxes or on the bed, she didn’t want Lucky to accidentally eat something.

    She was dumping some old avengers figurines into a box, not wanting Clint to see them if he hadn’t already, when she caught sight of something she hadn’t even remembered throwing into the box.

    She pulled it out.

    It was her old Starkphone, she must have shoved it in a drawer or something, a year previously. She pressed a button, expecting nothing, but the phone buzzed to life, lighting up with the Stark logo. Her home screen appeared, and her mailbox began to fill up.

    She pressed it, she was sure she’d been keeping up with her emails on her own phone, only to realise that it was her college email, she had never connected it to her new cell, mostly in the hopes of having an alibi for why she skipped an assignment or two.

    The emails were mostly for societies she’d stupidly signed up for at the orientation week, she deleted them one by one, wondering what made her decide to sign up for an acapella group. Her thumb hovered above the delete button, and was about to press it when she realised that the email wasn’t from an extracurricular group, or a disgruntled professor, or even the dean. Inmate #64172.

    “Shit,” she whispered, she stood up quickly and pushed the door to her room shut. She sat down on her bed and hesitated before pressing Open .

    Jack told me what you found out, he asked that I email you: I will not.

    If you want to know then you have to meet me in person.

    You are on my visitors list, visiting hours are 2-4pm, 28th and 29th December.

    I am sure I will see you soon.

    Mom.

    Kate couldn’t breathe, her hand was shaking, she couldn’t tear her eyes away from the phone screen.

    She should go and show Clint and Laura, they’d probably turn up there without Kate, get the truth within minutes, and solve everything… but they wouldn’t tell her everything, would insist on her not being there: they’d protect her because they’d been doing that since before they knew who she was.

    She needed to know.

    She needed to see Eleanor.

    One last time.

    Kate wedged herself into one of the corner seats of the couch, Lila had come to fetch her from her room when she hadn’t emerged despite agreeing to the movie, and Kate had done a passable job of acting like she was just tired, not that she was trying not to freak out. She seemed to have fooled everyone… except Yelena, of course, but the Russian stayed quiet, if anything she helped draw attention away from Kate’s mood when she began quizzing Laura and Clint on the movie selections.

    Kate stared at the screen without watching as A Bug's Life played, and lasted about half of The Little Prince before her eyes started to droop.

    Pulling her deep into the throws of sleep.

    The Bishop family still on her mind.

    Nightmare.

    Chapter Summary Kate has a nightmare and her parents demand answers.

    (also this is different from the eleanor in the show cause shes much more evil in this, as is derek)

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate was underneath her childhood bed, covering her ears with her hands, her parents had been yelling for hours.

    She had tried to calm them down, to stop them both from drinking the whiskey they’d been drinking, but she had been yelled at, banished to her bedroom.

    It wasn’t unusual, them yelling, especially when they drank… it didn’t make it any less scary, if anything the fights were getting worse.

    She tucked herself further under the bed as she heard the voices getting closer, they were heading up the stairs.

    “I didn’t even want her! We were fine as we were!”

    “You’re just never gonna get over that huh?!”

    “You were too lazy to get rid of it! I was happy with my life!”

    “Well I didn’t see you rushing to get rid of her, you liked having her as an accessory when you went to brunch, it’s not like a pet you can just get rid of whenever, I told you that! We only had a little amount of time to get rid before people would notice. I didn’t get rid of her because you liked the idea of playing mommy!”

    Kate moved back under the bed, she didn’t want to hear this.

    She kept moving further and further back, crawling, then standing.

    A slap stung her cheek.

    She looked ahead.

    Her mother, Eleanor, after Derek’s death, her hand raised after striking Kate’s cheek.

    Kate’s hand flew to her cheek, her mouth open in a gasp, her mother’s cruelty had never extended this far.

    “Don’t you ever embarrass me like that again,” the woman hissed, they were in a private bathroom of some posh hotel.

    Kate’s face burnt.

    This was the first time Eleanor had slapped her.

    She was thrust back again, Derek in front of her, swaying from beers and whiskeys and losing a lot of money.

    “Stay outta this!”

    Kate hadn’t even said anything, her head had just poked out of the bedroom door, having heard him staggering around.

    “Don’t look at me like that! It’s cause’a you I’m still tied up with this shit… they owed me,” he gave a humourless laugh, then a drunk burp, “now I owe them again.”

    She was forced back to the bathroom, Eleanor hit her once, then again, and again, and again, and again, each time the scene around them changed.

    Kate took it, as she always did.

    She would think back, before Derek died, before they found him, back to the Chitauri heading towards her, Clint saving her. He had been so brave and so could she.

    Another slap.

    “ You little spoiled rotten bitch .”

    She knew this moment.

    Kate had thrown a party while her mom had been away, it hadn’t even been a big one, she was fourteen, they’d all drank a little, sure, but not a lot, and they’d just played Mario Kart, she’d tidied up.

    Eleanor had found out.

    “ You are so ungrateful. It’s time you realise how lucky you are.”

    Kate was pushed out into the street, in the cold and dark, only her cell phone with her.

    It was the first time she’d been locked out.

    It hadn’t been the last.

    A slap.

    Another slap.

    The same ones replaying, mixing with newer ones, Eleanor’s insults and jabbed swirled around her in a tornado, too loud to make them out properly, but each one punctured her like a knife.

    “Kate!”

    “Kate!”

    “ Katie ! ”

    Kate sucked in a breath like she had been drowning, she tried to push herself back from the hands holding onto her, her eyes darting around the place, taking in the room but not recognising it.

    “ Kid , calm down, it’s okay, you’re okay sweetheart,” Clint’s voice was firm but soft. He kept his hands holding her arms, grounding her. “You’re home, Katie. It’s me, it’s Clint, dad .”

    Kate could see everyone now, Clint in front of her, Laura on his other side. Cooper, Lila, and Nate were all standing on Laura’s left, together and worried, Yelena was on Clint’s right, her hands reaching for weapons Kate was sure she must have been carrying.

    “Shiii,” slipped out of her, barely a whisper, she shook her head, “Sorry I’m sorry, I didn’t mean to, just a bad dream. Didn’t mean to freak out.”

    Clint’s eyes left her, sharing a look with Laura, he gave an imperceptible nod of his head. “Cooper, Lila, can you help Nate take a bath please?” It wasn’t a question but the two stayed staring at Kate.

    “Are you okay?”

    Kate nodded her head at Cooper’s question, but it didn’t seem to comfort him at all.

    “Cooper, please.”

    “But mommy-!” Nate finally spoke up, whining a little, “I wanna watch another movie.”

    Kate gritted her teeth, of course she had ruined this for her littlest brother, “I-”

    “ Buddy ,” Clint spoke over her, keeping her firmly where she sat, “listen to momma, okay? We’ll all have a movie sleepover in mom and my room later. I promise. Just be good for your brother and sister, got it?”

    Kate watched Nate raise his fist up to his forehead, then raise one finger, pointing upwards, Kate only recognised it as a sign when Clint nodded at the boy.

    “Good lad.”

    “Yelena, honey,” Laura smiled at the distinctly unsmiling assassin, “can you give us a few minutes?”

    Yelena hesitated, she liked and trusted Laura, that much was clear, but she looked at Kate, “I have a wide skill set, I am very good at killing… if you give me a name I can make the nightmares go.”

    “What! No, no, you’re not killing anyone!” She looked from Laura to Clint, both of them hadn’t disagreed with her… nor had they disagreed with Yelena, she shot Clint and Laura an imploring look.

    Laura held in a sigh, “Let’s not kill anyone for now,” she requested, giving her a look which Kate didn’t miss.

    The muscle in Yelena’s jaw jumped as she clenched her teeth, she looked from Laura to Kate, she read something in Kate’s face and nodded once.

    They all stayed perfectly in place, Kate didn’t know why until Laura nodded at Clint, his hands still holding onto Kate’s arms, “She’s not listening.”

    Clint nodded once. He looked at Kate for a minute before reluctantly letting go of her arms, and perching on the coffee table, close enough that his knees would have knocked her had she put her legs down. Laura sat on the seat beside her, staring at her as though she could see inside of her head.

    “I’m, I’m really sorry, I didn’t mean to fall asleep, I-”

    “Katie, what’s going on?”

    “Wh-what are you talking about? Nothing’s-”

    “Those nightmares aren’t nothing, hon-” Laura reached to brush a hair from Kate’s face, but the moniker fell off when Kate flinched away from her hand, “...ney.”

    “Shit, Laura, I’m sorry I didn’t mean to-”

    “Kate, that’s not the first time you’ve flinched,” Laura gave her a look, glancing away for a half second when Clint shot her a questioning look, she shook her head at him, that she’d tell him later.

    “Kate, I just, I’m still wired from the fighting in New York and I’m not-”

    “, what’s wrong? What’s going on?”

    Kate clenched her teeth, she shook her head, tears springing to her eyes, “Nothing’s wrong, nothing’s going on .”

    “Kate, something’s going on,” Laura added, certainly but calmly, “let us help you.”

    Kate shook her head, swallowing down a lump in her throat, “Nothing’s… nothing’s going on, I swear.”

    “ Katie ,” Clint’s voice broke.

    Kate looked at him, it was the same look he’d had been unable to hear Nate on the phone, Kate couldn’t describe it, but it made her want to break.

    “We know you. I know you : talk to me. Please, kid, what’s going on in your dreams? What’s making you so scared?”

    Clint took her hand, he squeezed it, the same way he’d taken her hand countless times since they’d met, always guiding her, saving her, comforting her, each and every time.

    She was silent.

    Laura took her other hand, like Clint she squeezed it, and like Clint’s hand it warmed her own cold ones, it felt so… natural, as though part of her remembered this, remembered them teaching her to walk, holding her hands when she cried, when she was a newborn.

    She sucked in a shaking breath. “It’s just… it’s just a dream.”

    Her hand in Laura’s was gently squeezed, gently encouraging her.

    “It was just… it was kinda… memories?”

    “‘Kinda?’”

    Kate nodded her head, she looked down at the hands, not able to look at Clint, even as he repeated her word. “I… it was like a lot of memories together, I just… before the battle of New York, when my, Eleanor and Derek were, they fought a lot, I forgot until,” she shook her head. “I forgot, I don’t think I realised, they used to fight about me. That they’d got stuck with me,” she shook her head letting out a bitter laugh, “How the fuck did I not realise I wasn’t theirs?”

    “You were a kid, you weren’t meant to pick up on those things, honey. Did they fight a lot? The Bishops?”

    Kate shook her head, then shrugged, “I don’t know, they didn’t fight all the time, they were stressed, and they’d drink…. My dad, Derek he would get pissed when he was drunk, literally and figuratively, he yelled a lot, he got into more debt because of me…” She shrugged, “It was just when they drank a lot, they couldn’t handle their drinks, and a lot of people are angry drunks.”

    “They should never have said those things, honey, they should never ,” Laura gently shook Kate’s hand, getting her to look at her, “they should never have even thought that, Kate.” Kate shrugged slightly, “It was… It’s okay.”

    “What else?”

    Kate met Clint’s eyes, “I… nothing, nothing , that was, it was just a stupid dream.”

    “You were calling out for us in your sleep, us, not them, you sounded like you needed help,” Clint’s voice was low and soft, “We’ve both seen bad memories turned into nightmares, in ourselves, in each other, we know it when we see it.”

    Kate sighed, looking away from them, back to the hands, she hoped that they didn’t feel her hands shaking, but she knew there was little chance of them not. “It’s not a big deal, just some old memories. I, I er, wasn’t exactly the poster child of an easy child, I got in trouble, a lot, and Eleanor was stressed, Derek left debts, a lot of them. I… we were at this ball, for some charity or something, I don’t really remember. I was bored, I was trying to impress some kids there, it was stupid: I found an old bow and arrow, I was already really good… just not as good as I thought. I fired an arrow, trying to hit the toupe of some guy there… I missed, I brought down this massive chandelier… Eleanor was mad.”

    She glanced up at Laura and Clint, both were looking stoic. She continued.

    “I really embarrassed her, she was so mad, she was stressed about money and she had to pay for the chandelier and,” she swallowed, “she snapped. She, she slapped me.”

    She was silent, but slowly looked up at her parents.

    Laura’s face looked almost blank, but there was fire burning in her brown eyes. Clint’s blue eyes were the opposite of Laura’s, the light blue eyes were full of ice, his jaw clenched. Like his wife he looked purposely blank “Baby?” Laura’s voice was too soft, slow, like a creeping spider, “Did this happen again? More than once?”

    “I…” Kate shrugged, “It happened a few times, it was fine most of the time, most of the time she wasn’t there, or when I pissed her off she’d just yell at me, or I’d be kicked out and just go stay at my friend’s place. I was a difficult kid, and she was stressed, it’s-”

    “I’m going to kill her.” Clint interrupted, his voice completely certain, fierce without shouting. He stood up without dropping Kate’s hand, in fact he held it tighter.

    “Get in line.” Laura stood up, looking at her husband, “Do you know anyone at the prison she’s at?”

    “No, but it shouldn’t be too hard to get in. The kids?”

    “Maria will probably look after them, she owes you for fixing her sink.”

    “Right, when do you wanna-?”

    “ What ! What no, why would you even-” Kate spluttered, she got to her feet and pulled Clint’s hand to draw his attention back to her, Laura copying him, they both softened watching her. “You can’t kill her! She, she’s- she raised me!”

    “She abused you, honey.”

    Kate stilled, looking blankly at her mother, she shook her head slowly, “No… no she didn’t. That wasn’t… she just, it was me, I was difficult-”

    “She hit you, Katie, she hit you more than once : That is abuse.”

    “It’s not like that!” She protested.

    “It is, she shouted at you, she kicked you out? Kate, that’s not a normal thing for a parent to do, you were abused, by her, and by Derek.”

    “He- they didn’t, they’d just-”

    “They took out their problems and choices on you. Baby, you’re having nightmares about it all these years later, that shows how much it really affected you, even if you didn’t know it then.”

    “I…”

    “You flinch when I raise my hand, any time, that’s not usual, honey. It’s not right.”

    Kate swallowed, dropping to the seat once more, tears blurred her vision.

    Laura didn’t hesitate before sitting back beside her, she wrapped her arm around her, pulling her into a side hug, she pressed a kiss to her temple.

    Clint sighed and dropped back onto the coffee table, he wiped a fallen tear away from her cheek with the pad of his thumb. “If you had come here and saw that Cooper was kicked out when he didn’t behave, or Lila flinched when your mom moved her hand, or if you saw me get wasted and yell at Nate for existing, you wouldn’t think that was right, would you? You’d want to help them?”

    Kate nodded, keeping her head on her mother’s shoulder, “Well, yeah, but they’re, they’re good kids.”

    “Honey, that doesn’t matter. You weren’t a bad kid, I can almost guarantee that, and even if you were it was wrong.”

    “I never wanted you to grow up around any of that,” Clint’s voice sounded raw, “I mean, obviously, because what parent would? But I lived through that Kate, I didn’t realise how bad it was, just like you. I didn’t want you to have to go through that realisation, and I’m sorry that you’re going to have to.”

    Kate bit her lip to keep her tears down, “Don’t kill her” her voice broke, more tears rolled down her cheek, “Please, I know you don’t like her, that you say that she’s… please , we’re meant to be better than the bad guys.”

    Clint sighed, his eyes flickered to Laura, “She gets her morals from you,” he half grumbled. He sighed again and looked back at Kate, “We won’t kill her, but she’s not going anywhere near you… or my arrow may slip.”

    Kate nodded her head thinking of the email from Eleanor, she had to talk to her, she knew she had to, it was important, even if it scared her new parents.

    “Seriously, no one is ever going to hurt you again, Kate. Or they won’t just be running from your dad.”

    There was still fire in Laura’s voice, it wasn’t farfetched to imagine her as the spy she used to be.

    Kate was only able to slip away from Laura and Clint’s sides when she insisted on taking a shower, even then she could see their hesitancy.

    In the water she formulated a plan.
