    Alibi Chapter Summary Kate's plan begins Chapter Notes heeeey besties <3 4AM what a better time to update this?

    love you all!

    See the end of the chapter for more notes They had all slept in Clint and Laura’s room, Clint and Cooper had moved in some mattresses from the other rooms, and they had projected a movie onto the ceiling.

    They had watched Wall-E, or they had been meant to; but Kate led on her mattress pretending to watch, and Clint and Laura had been pretending to watch too, but she had felt them looking at her from the bed, with Nate between them.

    She had felt Yelena’s noticing all of this, and confirmed it when she caught Yelena’s eye while the movie played, Yelena nodded slightly in acknowledgement either of her or of her earlier request to not murder Eleanor, Kate wasn’t sure.

    She was the first to wake: Cooper was on his mattress at the end of their parents bed, gently snoring, Lila and her mattress was blocked by the bed, on her other side was Yelena, sleeping with her arms over her chest like a vampire, guarding the door.

    She pushed back the covers and crept to her feet, pausing when the wooden floorboard creaked: Laura turned in the bed, and pulled Nate closer to her, from his spot between her and Clint, but otherwise no one moved.

    Kate needed air, she could go up to the roof again, she liked being up high, fitting for a hawk(eye) she told herself, but she knew she needed to be doing something or she’d drive herself mad.

    It was still dark when she went out to the yard, and walked to the outhouse where Clint had stored their bows safe the first night they’d got there. She grabbed hers, and a quiver of arrows, then trudged out into the snow, yet again regretting forgoing warmer clothing.

    She marched towards the treeline stopping when she could just about make out several targets leading into the woods. She drew her first arrow; pulled the string back, her hand rested against her face, she let the arrow fly: an easy bullseye, for her at least.

    The rest of her arrows flew, seemingly fast, they hit tree after tree each time, Kate hadn’t budged from her place as she flew them, but she was certain that she’d hit bullseye on every target, even the ones she wasn’t sure were even there.

    She sighed loudly.

    “It’s easier to sling arrows when your fingers aren’t turning blue, y’know?”

    Kate turned around at Clint’s voice, somehow not surprised to find him watching her, casually leaning against a post, his head tilted slightly to the side, as he did when he was busy thinking, as she had learnt.

    She hooked her bow across her back, before looking down at her hands, and sure enough they were bright red from the cold, she held them up to him despite the darkness, “They’re just red.”

    “Then you’re all good,” he joked, he held out his coat to her, and she took it, slipping it on. “How did I wake you up, you take your hearing aid out, wait, was it your spy instincts? Can you teach me?”

    Clint smiled at her, letting out a small laugh at her enthusiasm, even this early in the morning, “I can train you but it’s mostly just something you pick up naturally,” he shrugged, “I slept with it in last night.”

    Kate tilted her head to the side, “So Laura won’t shout at you for losing it again?”

    “I don’t lose them that much,” he rolled his eyes, but smiled at Kate’s smile, he picked up one of the two quivers from the ground and passed it to Kate, “I was listening out for you, in case you needed me.”

    “You… you didn’t have to do that,” but she smiled.

    Clint picked up the other quiver and fiddled with the arrows, “Of course I did. I used to put up with nightmares alone, when you were born I swore I wouldn’t let you do the same. You won’t remember the promise but I do,” he said the words simply, as fact, then nodded at her quiver, “As you’re up, are you up for a game of hide and seek?”

    She hesitated, but decided to take the hint that Clint didn’t want to talk, for once, so asked instead, “Hide and seek?”

    “Slightly modified.”

    Kate picked up the game quickly, like Clint said it was hide and seek, technically, crossed with paintball, and arrows. The two would hide around the Barton home land, in trees, in the rundown barns, in the ditches, behind a broken down tractor, anywhere they could; then they’d slowly hunt each other, or retreat, trying to shoot their trick arrows at each other- they didn’t hurt when they hit, instead they covered the target in coloured power. As it was a place he knew well and he was Hawkeye , Kate insisted that he was only allowed to carry two arrows at a time- yet Kate still managed to end up covered in orange powder, the clear loser. It was the most fun game Kate had played.

    Clint smiled at her, breathlessly when he told her that he was out of arrows, and he was getting cold, so it was time to call it, “You did good, kid.”

    “You cheated!”

    “You’re a sore loser,” Clint laughed, wrapping his arm around her shoulders and pointing her on the route back to their house.

    “I’m an amazing loser! You know everywhere around this place, and you got me when I fell outta a tree,” she glared at him as he let out a snort of laughter. At least he had been concerned when she had initially fallen, it was only when he discovered that she was okay that he even got her.

    “Next time we need to go to someplace neither of us have been, or we could do it around the city.”

    “New York is probably annoyed at us enough for now, but… maybe… we can all go, we’ll just have to figure out how to sneak away from your mom.”

    Kate beamed, “That’d be cool, I haven’t gone on a family trip in years.” She felt him tug her closer into his side and it felt good, being there with her, thinking about a family trip, “I could show you guys around.”

    “That sounds good.”

    “Hey, Clint?”

    “Yeah?”

    “Where was I born? Was it here? Like in Iowa or..? I guess I always thought it was New York, but, I mean I also thought that my birthday was on a totally different date.”

    Clint was silent, focusing on his feet as they trudged through the snow. “No, we built this place when Coop was born, Li and Nate were both born at the local hospital, but you and Coop were born in DC.”

    “What, like Washington?”

    “Yeah, me and your mom were both working at the Shield headquarters there, we lived a little outside of the city, we still own the house, maybe one day we can go there, I don’t really know what state it’s in anymore.”

    Kate nodded slowly, “Is that where I was taken from?” She heard Clint inhale slowly.

    “Yeah, yeah it was.” They reached the stairs to the porch and he allowed her to lead the way into their home, his hand on her back as though steadying her, but she was sure he was making sure that she was there.

    “Go wash up, you can help me make breakfast.”

    Kate was smiling as she walked back into the kitchen, free of the orange chalk like substance which had sprayed her skin whenever Clint’s arrows had hit her, Clint was moving around the kitchen collecting various ingredients and dumping them on the island which now had several different bowls on top of it. Clint had clearly attempted to wash up himself, though there were still flecks of purple on his skin, she didn’t tell him he’d missed it.

    “So, what’re we making? Omelettes? Eggs benedict? Croque madame?”

    Clint looked at her as though she’d been intentionally funny, “From what I’ve heard you can and do burn water, so definitely none of those. You think you can manage toasting some bread? Our toaster makes it supposedly impossible to burn it.”

    Kate raised an eyebrow, “We’re making toast ?”

    “No, I’m just not sure whether to let you near anything with a heat source, hey you can make cereal, right?”

    Kate glared at him across the island, “I’m telling Laura you’re being mean to me.”

    Clint laughed, “Yeah, she’s not gonna let you cook either.”

    Kate balled up a dish rag and threw it at him, he easily dodged it, laughing as he did.

    “What’re we really making? C’mon, you’re not really sticking me on toast detail, are you?” “Waffles, a couple of different types.”

    “Waffles? Seriously? Yes! I love waffles, I barely ever have them.”

    “Yeah I did notice that your diet when I met you was old pizza and coffee.”

    “I’m from New York, we have good pizza,” she protested, but was met with a raised brow, she rolled her eyes at him. “I’m not gonna like, mess up and poison everyone, am I?”

    “Depends,” he said seriously, grabbing a sack of flour and weighing it out on some old scales, “are you planning on pouring some poison into it?”

    “....No?”

    “Then it’ll be fine, Katie, have a little faith. I’ve just gotta weigh all the ingredients out, then you can help, but I found that” he nodded his head to a book on the far side of the island, “there’s more in a few albums upstairs, but I remembered I put some stray photos of you in there.”

    Kate’s hand reached towards the small photo album “Of me?” She asked frowning, before it dawned on her, “Oh, oh .”

    “It’s just if you want to look, you don’t have to-”

    “I want to,” her voice was almost fierce. She knew he was trying to be sensitive to her feelings, but it made her feel worse about her plan, and she did want to see.

    She settled on the stool across from him, and opened it up.

    The first photo had probably been her first ever photo- a red, squishy, tiny baby, in their mother’s arms, both on a hospital bed. Laura looked so tired but so happy, and young enough that she was probably Kate’s age, maybe a little older, Kate wasn’t sure, but she was sure that in this photo there was no arguing that Laura was her mother, they looked nearly identical. “You scared us at first,” Clint told her, not taking his eyes off of the sugar he was weighing, “you didn’t cry for way too long.”

    Kate ran her finger across the face of baby-Kate, “I’ll make sure I talk even more now, just to make up for it,” she smiled as Clint laughed.

    He was in the next photograph; holding the baby, her , like she was a bomb, he looked terrified, but at the same time… completely in love. It felt weird, seeing Clint like that, so unguarded, before losing everyone at least once, even now she was back he was so careful, for her , she wondered if he would look at her like that again, if she would let him.

    The next photo was still in the hospital, Laura, Clint, and Kate all in the bed, Clint looking less scared but still at her, around them were various people, who looked vaguely familiar. “Wait, are those Coulson and May?”

    “Yeah, they were two of your god parents, they had trained me and your mom, they were one of the few who even knew we were married. That’s Maria Hill, she was one of your mom’s best friends, she used to beat my ass in every sparring session; so she was your other god mother.”

    “Who’s the pirate?”

    “Fury, he was the head of Shield.”

    “Oh yeah! I saw him in all those files online.”

    “You read those?”

    Kate shrugged, “I got bored in class, I found them and started to read them- I ended up missing about eight assignments, but hey I still graduated with a 4.0 GPA.”

    “I don’t know which to be more impressed by, that you read all of those, or the GPA, I couldn’t get through many of them and it has been my job.”

    Kate laughed, but blushed slightly at the compliment on her GPA.

    “You were named after Nick, he and Coulson found me, helped me get on path, helped me and your mom meet, helped keep everything surrounding you secret.”

    Kate bit her lip, “Is he still around ?”

    “He’s not currently around,” he told her vaguely, “but when he’s back he’ll probably kill me for not tracking him down to tell him,” he rolled his eyes. “I think you’ll like him, if you can get him to tell you any of his stories.”

    “I’ll be able to,” she told him certainly.

    “I don’t doubt it.” He sent her a warm smile She flicked past a few general baby photos, until she got to one of her and Clint again: this one Clint hadn’t realised was being taken, if Kate had to guess, he was too busy beaming at the baby, who was holding what could possibly be the world's smallest bow, maybe it had been fate that she was to be an archer.

    There were more; photos at the beach, with people Kate had not grown up knowing, a lot of her just growing, looking less potato-like, and more like her, which was to say more like Clint and Laura, that much was clear.

    Most of the photographs were her with Clint and Laura, together or separately, they always looked like she was the most precious thing in the world, they changed in the photos, some they had different hair, or glasses, or other things to hide their identities, Clint had pointed to a few saying he’d just got back from being undercover, in the last couple of photos Laura’s second pregnancy was starting to show.

    It hurt.

    It hurt more than she wanted to think about.

    Eleanor had taken her from this.

    Or someone had.

    She was going to get the answers, she would come home victorious, she would stop having nightmares of the past, and she would get to make this family her own, as they always should have been.

    She slid the last photo from the album, slipping it into her pocket, she would return it later; after she had fixed this.

    Kate found Yelena in the guest room she’d been staying in, there was a couple of packed suitcases along with some taped up boxes, she had clearly got done with packing up the things of Natasha’s she’d be taking back to Russia.

    Kate was struck by a pang of anticipatory loss, “You’re all packed?”

    “Yes,” Yelena looked at the boxes as though it was painfully obvious, which Kate admitted it was. “Your brother is giving me a ride to my plane.”

    “Yeah, he told me…” Kate bit her lip, nodded, then moved into the room, she closed the door with a gentle click.

    “What is wrong?”

    Kate hesitated, then took a deep breath. “I need to ask a massive favour.”

    Yelena was silent then nodded, “What do you need?”

    “I… I need a lift… to New York.”

    Yelena raised her eyebrow, “You’re wanting to sneak in to see your fake-mother?”

    Kate shrugged, “I don’t need to sneak in, I was invited.”

    “By…? Oh . Why are you asking for my help, why not your mother or father? They are helping on this mission, right?”

    “So are you,” Kate shrugged again, Yelena gave her a look, and Kate sighed. “Clint already told me not to, he told me to not even get in contact with her, he kinda shouted at me,” she blushed, it was embarrassing that Clint had told her off like a child, and recounting it to Yelena didn’t lessen that feeling.

    “My daddy still tells me off for things, or he tries to, he did the same to my sister, so did our mother.”

    Kate smiled softly at Yelena’s attempt at easing her embarrassment. “Yeah, well, I’m not used to it, and he’s wrong: I need to talk to Eleanor, I’m the only one who’ll be able to get anything out of her. I know that she knows what happened, I need to find out.”

    “What if she refuses to give you answers?”

    “Then we’ll go with whatever Laura or Clint are planning,” she sent Yelena a look when she saw her friend thinking, “You’re not going to punch her until she gives you answers.”

    “It would work.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, “I don’t care… that can be plan K.”

    “Plan K should stand for plan kill.”

    “No one is killing anyone, Jesus! Look, you can punch her three times in plan J, but no plan K, no killing, and you’re only punching her if I say so, got it?”

    “Fine, but I am hoping that we do plan J one day,” Yelena rolled her eyes, then added underneath her breath, “and plan K.”

    “Yelena, I really think she’ll be able to help me. And I want answers, I want to know why I was stolen, she clearly didn’t want me, so why keep me? And…,” she sighed, “I guess I need to see her one more time, I want to move on, but… It’s like when you had to talk to Clint to get the truth from him, about your sister, you needed it directly from him, I didn’t get it then, but I do now. I can’t just be told things from her by other people, it needs to be me and her, one last time.”

    Yelena watched her carefully.

    Kate rubbed her face with her hands, she rubbed hard, her hands slid together in a praying type gesture, she pointed her hands at Yelena. “I just… I need this. I really need this. I’ll never ask anything from you again, and I’ll owe you one, or twelve . Please Yelena.”

    “You will not owe you one.”

    Kate deflated at the clear brush off, she didn’t know how she’d do it now, she still needed to but it was hours to drive without Clint’s clearly tricked out truck, especially without a licence.

    “Kate Bishop, I will not owe you one because I do this for free.”

    “Wait, really?”

    “Of course, we are… friends ?”

    Kate beamed, “Yeah, we are. Yelena, I…” she shook her head and just wrapped Yelena in a tight hug, “Thank you so much Lena.” She felt Yelena’s arms tighten around her. She didn’t let go.

    Yelena had pointed out that Kate would still need an alibi, to buy a little extra time before the inevitable situation of her parents tracking her down, Kate had thought the same, and had in fact been impressed that she had managed to think of the same thing that a Black Widow had.

    That was why, as Yelena was saying goodbyes to the rest of the family, Kate knocked on Cooper’s bedroom door.

    She pushed it open when he called ‘Yeah’ back to her knock.

    Cooper’s room was messy, and smelt like Axe, there were clothes over the floor. His bed was tucked into an alcove next to one of the windows, it made sense it was so easy to climb into, considering the amount of times he’d go up to the roof. The centre of the room was something she hadn’t expected to see - a drum kit, but as she scanned the room she saw drumsticks around the place, along with various headphones, and some kind of digital drum kit on his desk next to a computer. She hadn’t known he was into drumming, or music, if truth be told.

    This was exactly why she needed to talk to Eleanor; if she could move on she could work on trying to fit into the family, trying to actually be part of it; to get to know her siblings properly, to play hide and seek with Clint, to watch a movie with Laura, to even spend time with Yelena. She needed to move on, she wanted to , but anytime she did she couldn’t help but feel like she was betraying Eleanor and Derek, even now.

    “Oh! Hey Kate,” the teen grinned, clearly surprised that she was in his room, he turned back around, walking through his room, rummaging through piles, with only one shoe on his feet. “Whatcha looking for?”

    “My other shoe, I swear I just saw it.”

    Kate scanned the room, at the same time that her brother did, “Second shelf of the bookcase,” she spotted pointing, “hanging by the shoelace.”

    “Shit, how the fu- thanks Kate,” he grabbed the shoe and hopped around as he slipped it on, “What’s up?” He grabbed a jacket and shoved it into his backpack, “I thought you’d be saying bye to Yelena?”

    “Er, yeah, I was actually kinda hoping I can get a favour from you?”

    He stopped what he was doing and stood up straight, frowning slightly, “What’s wrong?”

    “Nothing, nothing’s wrong. I was just hoping I could tag along with you, on the drive? I just need some space, I know you’re meeting with your friends, but we could drive and I can go around the town? Check out the stores, then I’ll call Clint or Laura to pick me up.”

    Cooper hesitated, “They’re really not gonna like you going around by yourself…”

    “....Yeahhhhhh….” Kate dragged out, “I was kinda hoping we could tell them that I’m hanging out with you? I know you’d be… them , if they think I’m in your town on my own.

    But I really need this time, Coop. Please? I’ll owe you.”

    Cooper let out a loud sigh, doing a good impression of Clint, a little too good, in Kate's opinion, but then he sent her a smile which was all Laura, “You don’t have to owe me. You can actually hang out with me and my friends, if you like? I’m sure they’ll like you.”

    She smiled at him but shook her head, “Thanks but I’ll leave you to break the whole ‘long lost sister’ thing without me as a special guest.” She smiled as Cooper laughed, and couldn’t help but feel a little bad, even if her lies were necessary.

    Kate followed Cooper downstairs, carrying her own small backpack, but stopped on the staircase.

    She couldn’t help but stare as she watched Yelena give Lila a side hug as she carried Nate in her arms, the boy clinging to her, the two had bonded over the last few days.

    She knew that Yelena was coming back, she would only be gone for a week, to appease her parents, then she’d be back, she had said she wanted to learn more about Natasha, that the Bartons’ had tons of stories, and maybe that was true, but Kate was pretty sure that Yelena was enjoying living here, while she figured out who she was now.

    Nate wasn’t the only one who was going to miss her for the week she was gone.

    She wondered if Yelena didn’t mind phone calls, or if she preferred texts, and what the time difference would be.

    “You’ll tell your parents that we’re so sorry for their loss?” Laura told her, phrasing it like a question but it was clearly a demand. She wrapped her arms tight around the assassin, who to her credit, only stiffened a little. “And that they’re welcome to visit, anytime.”

    “I will let them know, and how kind you’ve been to me and to my sister,” Yelena told her sincerely. She passed Nate to her arms, then gently pinched his nose, “You practice your snow city while I’m gone, and when I’m back we’ll make a massive one, deal?”

    “ Deal ,” he grinned, high fiving his new friend, his eyes flickered to the window, as he restrained himself from rushing outside to start practising.

    Clint waited for Yelena to be standing alone before he approached her, his voice was low, Kate had to strain to hear him.

    “Tell your parents that I’m so sorry, that I… I loved her, I tried, she was always the better fighter though.”

    Yelena was quiet before she softly said, “I will make sure they know it is not your fault… that she loved you too.”

    “I appreciate that. And… Thank you, you found out the truth for Kate. Thank you for bringing my daughter home. You’re a lot like your sister.”

    Kate swallowed, the sound of Clint’s voice reminded her so much of the photographs, the fear and love, one of her hands went to her pocket, where it brushed against the gloss of the photograph, she felt calmer, feeling it there.

    “Hey.” She smiled at both of them, pretending to have not been listening in, Yelena looked grateful to have an excuse to escape this conversation, though Kate could have sworn that her eyes were glistening with tears from Clint’s words.

    Clint cleared his throat, looking away from Yelena, down at the floor as he cleared his expression, then up at his daughter with a smile, “Hey kiddo,” his smile slowly dimmed to confusion, “What’s with the bag?”

    Kate could see his question had piqued Laura’s interest too, “Erm, I’m gonna hang out with Cooper and his friends for a couple of hours, after I see Yelena off.”

    Clint’s confusion turned to a frown, and Laura walked over to his side, also looking confused. “I’m not sure that’s such a good idea, kid.”

    “It’s a small town, and I need to get out, just clear my head, and this way I can y’know, like, bond with Cooper. And it means I can say a proper bye to Yelena, she’s going halfway across the world.”

    “She’s only gonna be gone for a week.”

    “Kate your father’s right, I’m not sure this is a good idea. You’ve had a long few days, and yesterday was pretty stressful, I was hoping we could talk about that.”

    “We can talk later, I swear. But I need this, just to, y’know’, get some air, I’m a city girl, I’m not used to all the fresh air.” She tried to sound casual but a strike of panic hit through her as Clin and Laura shared a look. “Look, it’s not like I’m gonna have another nightmare, cause I’m not going to sleep, and I won’t be gone for that long. I just… I need this.” She looked him dead in the eye as she spoke, trying not to technically lie, which she managed.

    Clint didn’t look convinced.

    He opened his mouth but stopped when Laura’s hand went to his arm and squeezed it.

    “Babe, she is an adult, it’s not like we can lock her up.”

    “She’s barely not a kid.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, even if she was used to Clint’s ideas of age by now, “I’m twenty-two.” “Uh huh,” Clint was yet again unconvinced by this fact.

    Laura rolled her eyes this time, but stood a little in front of Clint now, “You’ve got your phone and all of our numbers? It’s on and charged? And you know that you can call us if you want to come home before your brother does, one of us will come pick you up?”

    Kate bit back a smile at Laura being… so television Mom, or maybe this was what all moms were meant to be like, Kate didn’t know anymore. “My cell’s in my pocket,” she assured her, “I’ll be okay.” She nodded slightly as she said it, looking at Laura, then at Clint.

    Clint sighed, and turned to a very bored looking Cooper, “Bud drive safe, the snow should be cleared but just go careful, gotit?” He smiled and shook his head as Cooper gave a sarcastic salute, his fault for having kids with any of his genes clearly.

    “Okay, honey,” Laura pulled her into a hug, “I’ll see you in a couple of hours.”

    “Yeah,” Kate whispered into Laura’s hug, the guilt she’d felt earlier was only growing, this was the first time Laura had to say bye to her Clint pulled her into a hug next, his hand going to the back of her head instinctively, “Try not to destroy this town, okay Katie?”

    “I only destroyed a small part of New York,” she protested, hugging him back, “I’ll be fine.” Clint moved back, staring at her, for a second he seemed like he might change his mind again, and put his foot down that he didn’t want her leaving, but he just slowly nodded. “I know you will. Do you still have my number?”

    “Of course, how could I delete the famous Hawkeye’s number.”

    Clint rolled his eyes. “Have a good time, kid.”

    Kate nodded, knowing that the time she would have would probably not technically be classed as ‘good’, but it wasn’t like she could tell him that.

    Kate followed Cooper and Yelena out of the door, pausing only to stroke Lucky goodbye, promising him she’d be back.

    Kate sent him a grateful smile but looked away before she could feel too guilty, “No, thanks, I’m good. You should go hang out with your friends, I’m gonna facetime one of my friends then walk for a little while.”

    Cooper hesitated but nodded, “Okay… you’ve got mom’s number, right?”

    “Yeah, and your dad’s,” she tilted her head slightly to the side in brief consideration, “ our … yeah, still weird. Anyway, I’m gonna be okay, I’m used to bigger places than here. I’ll be fine Cooper.”

    He held up his hands in surrender, then checked his phone, “I’ve gotta go, I’m running late,” he began to head off, turning around to face her as he did, “you good?”

    “ Coop ,” she couldn’t help but laugh, he was still trying to be a good brother, despite clearly wanting to go somewhere.

    “Sorry, sorry! Later Kate!”

    Kate pulled out her phone as she watched to make sure that he was truly gone, then she doubled back through the parking lot.

    It was easy enough to get to the field they’d dropped Yelena off in, it turned out that the main places to hang out in the town were essentially down one long road, so she just carried on walking.

    It was only ten minutes until she hit the field. There were plenty of fields in Iowa, but this one was the only one she’d seen with a plane parked in it.

    “Are you ready, Kate Bishop?”

    Of course, Yelena didn’t even need to turn around to see it was her, Kate was getting used to that.

    “I’m ready,” she parked herself in the passenger seat, glaring at the elaborate seatbelt, before figuring out how to probably do it.

    Yelena flicked several switches on the dash as Kate slipped on the pair of headphones which had been on her seat.

    The plane left the ground.

    “Hey, you have, like, a licence to fly this thing, right?”

    “No, but my daddy let me fly in the garden when I was a child, that was a model plane but there is not much of a difference.”

    There was silence, Kate held tight to her seat, and swallowed.

    Yelena laughed. “I am just joking, of course I have been trained in this!”

    “Oh, you’ve got jokes now?” Kate grumbled.

    “Of course, I am very funny.”

    Kate rolled her eyes but smiled.

    She watched the town get smaller.

    “What are you planning on doing, once this is resolved?”

    Kate was surprised at the question, especially as Yelena had been surprisingly quiet, presumably focusing on flying the plane, and it wasn’t their usual bantering talk. “I don’t… I don’t really know, I hadn’t thought that far. I guess… I’ll stick around, train, get to know them… until they get sick of me?

    “Why do you think they’ll get sick of you?”

    Kate gave her a look.

    “I am serious.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, “So am I. I… I annoy people, it’s kinda a skill, one of my best talents actually.”

    “The only thing annoying about you is your insistence to do everything on your own,” Yelena told her matter-of-factly, “I mean, I don’t mind it so much. I think your parents are not sick of it, unless they kill you after they find out about this, of course.”

    “Does that mean you’re not going to protect me?” Kate teased, sending her a fake pout.

    Yelena let out a snort of laughter, “Of course not, your mother is a very famous spy you know? I understand why my sister loved her so much. She would have made a great Black Widow, if she had been bad, not good.”

    Kate’s gaze softened on her. “I don’t think you’re so bad.”

    “I tried to kill your father.”

    Kate shrugged, “I think he gets that a lot, so you get a pass.” She was quiet for a minute. Yelena clearly didn’t believe that she was right, but Kate knew differently. “You helped me, you keep helping me… maybe you’re good in training.”

    Yelena smiled softly, “Okay… that means I can still commit some minor crimes, correct?”

    Kate laughed, “Sure, things like petty theft, maybe some breaking and entering on deserted properties.”

    “Piracy, with movies, not with a boat, though that would be fun, I am good with swords.”

    “Pirating movies isn’t a crime, it’s common sense,” she shared a mischievous smile with her friend. Kate was struck by how pretty Yelena was, not for the first time. “I think you’d look good as a pirate.”

    Yelena raised her eyebrow, Kate looked away, hoping that she wasn’t blushing.

    “Perhaps you could be my first mate.”

    Kate looked back at her, not caring about the blush on her cheeks, “Deal.”

    “I didn’t ask you, you asked me earlier, but what’re you planning, y’know once we’ve figured all of this out.”

    “I do not know… I lost a lot of time with my parents, when I was in the Red Room, and then a Widow. I had some time with them and my sister after we took the Red Room down, but not enough.”

    Kate nodded slowly, “It sounds like you all need it, time to get to know each other again.” She knew that her words weren’t just about Yelena, but about herself. She needed to find out the truth, then she wanted to know who she was now, she wanted to feel like part of her new family.

    Yelena nodded, as though she could read Kate’s thoughts, maybe she could, they were two sides of the the same coin “I would also like to spend time in America. I loved all the movies about it when I was in the Red Room. I loved living here when I was very small.”

    “You lived here ?”

    “My family was undercover, I didn’t know it then, of course. We lived in Ohio.”

    “I’m so sorry.”

    Yelena smiled at her, “I could use a tour guide… if you know any?”

    Kate beamed, “I’ll do it, if you don’t mind getting lost everywhere we go?”

    Yelena’s smile outshone even Kates, “I do not mind that at all.”

    It had taken a little bit of persuading but Kate had convinced Yelena to let her go by herself; namely because this was something she had to do; but also because Yelena had already been dragging out her return to Russia despite how much she clearly wanted to go, Kate didn’t want to be the reason she was missing her family.

    She had been dropped off on the top of a random building, because of course Yelena didn’t see an issue in that. Kate just smiled at the confused, elderly, aviator sunglasses-wearing security guard, who didn’t do much to guard the roof, from what she could tell.

    She sat on the subway, finding that she missed having Clint beside her, as he had been before Christmas, she smiled remembering that he had called her one of the world’s greatest archers, it had been before they’d found out who he was in relation to her, so he must have meant it. God, had it been only that morning that they’d played hide and seek? It felt like a lifetime ago.

    She reached her hand into her pocket for her phone, as most people her age did for comfort, thinking about calming her nerves, but her hand pulled out the old phone she had brought with her. She’d been so smart, in her opinion, and hadn’t brought the phone everyone had her number for, it would make her slightly harder to track, if only for a little while. She pulled out her old phone, hesitated, then pulled up the message app.

    Most of the messages from Eleanor had been nice, she hadn’t been mean all the time, which was what she’d tried to explain to Laura and Clint, sure she’d been a strict parent, with high expectations, but that was just what she was like, Kate had been used to it.

    Her heart froze as she hit a message: Do not let me down, Mom.

    It had been the day she’d been taking a test, it hadn’t even been an important test, but the son of someone she knew had been taking it too, Kate had got the same mark as the guy.

    It hadn’t been good enough for her.

    She jumped as the voice on the subway announced her stop.

    She shoved the phone into her pocket and got to her feet.

    It was time to face the dragon.

    She had never been to visit someone in jail, like the vast majority of people, she supposed, and she couldn’t help but wish that she had someone where with her, her mom, her real mom, Laura would have known what to do, and would have made her feel better.

    Laura probably would also kill Eleanor on sight, so maybe it was a good thing that she wasn’t there.

    She shoved her backpack into a locker, carrying only a notebook and pen which she’d already asked the guard was okay to take in.

    It was kind of like an airport; she had to take off her shoes, and empty her pockets, her phone was in the locker with her bag so there wasn’t anything in her pockets… except the token Clint had given her for Christmas… she hadn’t even realised it was in there…

    He was going to hate her when he realised she’d lied to him.

    Her quest for answers might have screwed her out of having a family.

    But it was too late, she thought as she walked through the body scan, she was this far, she had already screwed herself, so she might as well get her answers.

    She was led to a small room, inside was completely grey, the bricks, the bars on the window, the table, the seats, everything except for Eleanor Bishop, who looked the same as she had when Kate had last seen her, the only difference was that she was wearing an orange jumpsuit, with a prison number on it and everything.

    Kate slipped into the seat she was told to, listened to the warnings from the warden, then they were left alone.

    “I’m surprised that you came.”

    Kate nodded slowly, “I am too. But I had to.”

    “Is anyone else here with you? A certain archer, by any chance?”

    Kate bit the inside of her cheek to stop herself from shouting at Eleanor. “Did you know who he was the whole time? That he’s my dad?”

    Eleanor looked amused, tilted her head side to side as though she was thinking, and pursed her lips. “Not the whole time. Not when you first came to me, but after your father’s funeral, when you took after the family business, someone made a joke; I put two and two together.” Kate furrowed her brows, “Who made the joke?”

    Eleanor was quiet for a minute, “So this is what we’re doing now, is it? You come in here demanding answers, without even asking me how I am?”

    Her voice was quiet, calm, but it chilled Kate to the bone. She reminded herself that Eleanor was shackled to the table, it didn’t help.

    She heard a voice in her head, Clint’s, she thought, telling her that Kate owed her nothing . She listened to that voice.

    “You lied to me for my entire life, this isn’t a social visit. And you tried to kill my dad.” “So you are with him now, Clinton Francis Barton.”

    “It’s not his fault he’s got a stupid name,” Kate told her, spurred on by the purr of Eleanor’s smile. “Are you going to tell me what happened, or was all of this a waste of my time?”

    “Mind your temper, Katherine.”

    Kate sucked in a breath, a shiver ran down her spine and she sat up straighter. “Why did you invite me here?” Her voice was low, and she could hear the fear in it, she could only hope that Eleanor didn’t hear it too.

    “Kate, I was your mother for years, I want to give you the truth, and a warning.”

    “What’s the warning?”

    Eleanor let out a single tut. “I’ll tell you the truth first.”

    “The warning kinda sounds important, maybe we should start with that.”

    Eleanor shot her a look, then turned to the door, “Guard?”

    “Wait! Fuck ,” Kate mumbled to herself, “stop, I’ll, your right, tell me in whatever order. Please Eleanor .”

    Eleanor turned back to face her, “Just like that I’m no longer ‘mom’?”

    Kate was silent, not wanting to tell her that she hadn’t been ‘mom’ in Kate’s mind for a long time, that had only intensified when she had met Laura, her real mom , not because of biology, but because she loved Kate for who she was.

    “Hmm,” Eleanor let out, then hummed.

    The were both quiet for a minute, Kate picked at the skin around her thumb until it split, trying her “I didn’t know a thing about you until he brought you home with him one day. You were young, very small, scared. You cowarded away from both of us for days, you cried so much,” she shook her head with a slight roll of her eyes, “I told Derek I didn’t need to know where exactly you came from, but to take you back. I didn’t want to be a mother, I never had that… instinct , and you were just so… difficult. He told me he couldn’t take you away.”

    “It was only then that he told me how much debt he’d amounted, you know that he had grown up wealthy? He wasn’t used to budgeting, he got greedy, and stupid. So he’d been given a job: The Kingpin was working with Hydra, certain members of it anyway. They’d acquired a child, and the child’s parents were Shield agents, scouring the world for their missing offspring, Hydra had been doing tests on the child, she was the baby of two highly feared spies, two who had more blood on their hands than most of us: there was an idea that this child would become the next Winter Soldier, or Black Widow: a highly trained and skilled assassin, without needing much chemical modification.

    Hydra was still hiding, and the Hand was still new, the organisation your father was part of. They were up and coming, but just as hidden as Hydra, only people in the know knew about them.”

    “If I was supposed to be part of Hydra then why did I end up being me ?”

    “You were defective,” Eleanor told her breezily, “a detected health condition, I suppose whomever was in charge of you decided you were more trouble than you were worth.”

    Her voice told Kate that Eleanor agreed implicitly. “Then why am I even alive, why not just kill me?”

    Eleanor shrugged, “I’m not sure, truly, I was never told, maybe it was a mistake, a child was found dead and presumed to be Nicole Barton, perhaps there was simply a mixup.”

    Kate tried to swallow but her throat was dry: she was alive by mistake.

    “You were too young to be useful to The Hand, from what your father told me, but he volunteered to take you, that, if they gave him funds, he could raise you to become useful. They believed him.”

    Kate thought back to her childhood, when Derek was still alive, “All the games, the competitions…”

    “He was trying to bide time, to train you to be athletic, intelligent, to follow orders; he only failed on the last one.”

    Kate couldn’t help but glare, it seemed to amuse Eleanor.

    But then her face fell, “He took advantage of the company, ran up debts, gambling, other things,” she shook her head, “he shouldn’t have done that.”

    “Why?”

    “He died.”

    “Yeah, in the attack on New York, a wall fell on him, you-”

    “-told you that, I did. His neck was slit, he had been warned, he had involved me in the company by then…”

    Kate shook her head, she couldn’t listen to the words too closely, she didn’t want to put it together, “No, we were… we were at home, no one was there, I’d been listening, it was just us, and the aliens, we were going to play checkers-”

    “The noise of the aliens turned out to be the best disguise.” She shook her head again. “I loved him, Kate, who he had been, before all his schemes. He tried to drag me down with him. He made me into a ‘mother’, stranded me with you, and with his debts. You could never do as you were told, ever.

    I could .”

    Kate pushed her chair back, her breathing breathless, “ You … you killed him?”

    “ I did what I had to do to survive .”

    Kate was frozen by Eleanor’s steely tone.

    “I took you on too, continued his work, tried to make you into what you could be. I should have been harder, made you harder.” She let out a bitter laugh, “I guess I can’t fight those Hawkeye genes.”

    Tears were in Kate’s eyes as she glared at Eleanor, her teeth clenched, “You’re right, you can’t. Because you’re not shit to me anymore. And you’re gonna rot in here, like you deserve.”

    Eleanor leant across the table, her eyes were dead; “Just because I’m in here right now, Katherine, doesn’t mean I always will be. So if I were you, I’d run, run away from the Barton family, and hide, you’ve proven yourself to The Hand and to Hydra. And I’m not going to forget who put me in here .”

    Before Kate could open her mouth to say anything the door to the room swung open.

    “This visit is over,” Laura Barton snarled at Eleanor Bishop, behind her Clint looked just as livid.

    “Get her out of here.”

    Clint took Kate by the arm and marched her out of the room, leaving Laura alone with Eleanor.

    They skipped the body scanners, and the security guards had clearly already dealt with Clint and Laura as they pressed themselves against the wall to allow him to pass with his daughter. “Clint, I-”

    “ No .” Clint cut her off. He pressed a button on a door and they were buzzed out of the building.

    “Bu-”

    “ Katherine,” he used her full name for the first time since they had met, he was clearly trying to stay calm and quiet, his teeth clenched together.

    She had to admit that it scared her, but not in the scared-of kind of way Eleanor saying it did. “I love you, but I advise you to not talk right now,” his voice was measured purposely toneless in his attempt to stay calm, “at all.”

    She didn’t dare try again.

    She was led to a car, parked haphazardly in front of the prison, it was an older car, the type with only two doors, Clint kept his hand on her arm as her opened the door and pulled a lever, then pushed the chair so there was a gap to get into the backseat.

    “Get in.”

    Kate was happy for the opportunity to get away from him, she wasn’t used to this form of disappointing someone, disappointing Eleanor had been part of her day to day life- Clint cared about her, he was her dad .

    She climbed into the back of the car, taking longer than she should, in hopes of hiding the tears in her eyes. She sat down, clenched her teeth, and watched broken hearted as Clint shoved the chair back into place, then slammed the door, waiting outside, away from her.

    She had fucked up.

    She had fucked up so badly.

    Clint was so mad.

    Laura was so mad.

    God only knows what she was doing in that room.

    She found this childish whim inside, she didn’t want Laura to be doing anything too bad, she didn’t want her to kill Eleanor for the very childish reason of her mom was a good guy, good guys don’t kill people. She knew that wasn’t even true, but she couldn’t help but hope that she could have one mother who wouldn’t kill another one of her parents.

    It seemed to take forever but finally Clint opened the door again, he sat in the seat in front of her.

    “Look-”

    “Put your seatbelt on, your mom’s on her way.” Clint’s voice was firm.

    She could tell from even how he was sat that he was angry. and he completely ignored her words, she wondered if he was purposely ignoring her or if she had annoyed him enough that he had turned his hearing aids off.

    She bit the inside of her cheek, if she could focus on that pain she could ignore the fact that she felt like crying.

    Kate watched through the window as Laura stormed towards their car, in her hands was the bag Kate had taken with her.

    Laura climbed into the car, glanced back at Kate then looked at Clint. “Let’s go. Kate , put your seatbelt on.”

    This time she listened, once it clicked into place Clint started the car and sped out of the lot, paying little attention to the ‘ Have a nice day ’ sign at the exit.

    They were silent as Clint drove, Laura seemed to be staring ahead, but Kate could see her checking all of the mirrors constantly, and Clint was doing the same, as though they were expecting someone.

    She opened her mouth to speak a few times, but she couldn’t find words which would fix this, like she had always tried to do with Eleanor and Derek, and the car felt too small for the ensuing fight. She stayed in the backseat, sunk down into herself, as though trying to hide in the hoodie of Clint’s she had claimed.

    Clint spoke first, his voice dropping low so it would be harder for her to hear, not that she could understand what he was saying as he spoke in Russian.

    "Как это прошло?"

    “Я ударил ее несколько раз, и у меня есть копии записей, но я сомневаюсь, что мы что-

    то пропустили во время просмотра.”

    Laura spoke just as perfect Russian as Clint apparently did, but with less of an American accent, Kate was sure that if she tried Laura could sound as Russian as Yelena.

    “Она еще жива?”

    “Только потому, что наша дочь попросила меня не убивать ее. Если она снова приблизится к Кейт: я ее убью.”

    Kate forced herself not to react when she heard her mom say her name, a good call, as she could feel her mom’s eyes on her through the rear-view mirror.

    “Можешь встать в очередь за мной” Clint practically growled back to whatever Laura had said, but it didn’t seem to phase her, so it couldn’t have been at her.

    They were silent again, Kate watched them, hoping they’d forget she was there and would talk in English, but they didn’t.

    "Куда нам идти...?"

    “Квартира твоего брата рядом, пойдем туда.”

    “Почему мы не можем пойти куда-нибудь еще?”

    “Там безопасно. Он поймет. Давай спрячемся там ненадолго, пока разберемся, и убедимся, что нас никто не преследует.”

    They were silent, Clint made a couple of rights until they were heading back where they had been, but before they got too far he took a left.

    They were both silent.

    Kate sat picking at the skin around her thumb again, ignoring the blood. She just had to do that, and completely ignore all the thoughts in her head.

    “Нам нужно поговорить с ней. Мы не можем пройти через это снова.”

    "...Ага."

    They both sighed as they spoke, there was anger behind the words, and Kate just knew it was about her.

    They parked up outside of a block of apartments, Laura was out of the car first, her hands resting on two sticks at her side as she glanced around, then nodded at Clint.

    Clint took the signal and climbed out of the car, he pulled back the seat and nodded at Kate, “Let’s go.”

    “Where are we?”

    “Safe house.” He nodded again and she climbed out of the car.

    Clint’s hand on her arm as soon as she was out of the door, and she felt guilty enough that she couldn’t even roll her eyes, of course he won’t trust her anymore.

    She was led through the lobby, her mom behind then, Clint’s right hand on her arm, but his left hand was by his side, Kate was pretty sure that he had his Ronin sword with him tucked in on itself, ready to be used if needed.

    The three were silent in the elevator to the seventeenth floor, the silence continued as her parents took up their same positions as she was taken across the corridor, Laura produced a key and unlocked the door, Clint took Kate in first, then Laura entered, shutting the door behind them.

    Clint finally let go of Kate’s arm, and Kate stood sheepishly in front of her parents.

    They didn’t say anything, clearly waiting for Kate, she didn’t know what to say though, whether to apologise or to argue her point. All her dealings with parents had relied on her knowing just what to say, what combination of things to stay safe, but with them she didn’t know what to say, they weren’t Eleanor and Derek, their love was a lot more complicated.

    “How did you find me?” she looked away from them as she asked it, she only looked up when she heard Clint suck in a breath.

    “Your brother was playing on your phone he said that Lucky found it, so I, stupidly , thought you’d just left it, so I started to drive into town to give it to you, I called Cooper, who was certain that you had a phone with you. So he left his friends who he had been going to hang out with to search for you, in any place we could think of.”

    He clenched his teeth and the muscle in his jaw jumped with his barely contained anger. “We couldn’t find you… But we had an idea of where you might have decided to go, we just hoped that we were wrong,” he let out a bitter laugh, “we felt bad about thinking the worse of you that you’d act so recklessly.... So your mom searched for any old cell phone numbers connected to you, we hoped you’d just gone somewhere nearby, but she found the latest one and the location was flying over Indiana.”

    He shook his head, “I’m guessing you got Yelena to help you with this little plan?”

    “It’s not… it wasn’t her fault, it was all me,” fear shot through Kate that Yelena wouldn’t be invited back after all of this, ever, “I just made her.”

    “No one can just make a Widow do anything,” Laura told her firmly. She shook her head, “Why did you do this Kate? Why not come to us?”

    “Because he already,” she flung her arm out in Clint’s direction, “ ordered me not to!” She was surprised to find herself shouting, and glaring at both of her parents, but she was seeing red again.

    Clint’s anger met Kate’s, “ Because it’s dangerous , Kate!”

    “You can’t just treat me like a kid-”

    “You’re young Kate, I know you’re not a kid, but you’ve not been an adult for long, and you haven’t got anywhere near the experience we do! Do you know what your siblings are doing right now?”

    She waited for him to continue but when he didn’t she gave a half hearted shrug, still glaring.

    “They’re travelling with one of the best spies in the world to a hidden location, even we don’t know.”

    “Why?” Kate frowned in concern, “They were fine-”

    “Because the people who took you have been watching Eleanor in prison,” Laura’s voice wasn’t as heated as her husband and daughter’s, but there was steel in her tone, “because that’s what they do. They’ve seen what you can do live on TV, and now they’ll want to eliminate you as a threat, or they’ll take you again: Eleanor was the perfect bait. And this type of people won’t care who they use or hurt to get you to go with them.”

    “Which is exactly why I told you not to do this.” Clint added. “I told you and you agreed , I said it was dangerous, did you not hear me or something? Because that’s the only semi-valid reason I can think of why you did this.”

    “I had to get information from her! I had to know the truth, and I got it. By myself .”

    Clint rolled his eyes, “So it was out of stubbornness, that’s great Kate.”

    “You don’t get it!” This time her shout tore out of her, burning her throat, “You don’t fucking get it!”

    “I get it, Kate.” Clint glared his voice low. “I get it more than you know, which is why I’ve tried to help you not make mistakes I made. But anytime I even try to help you, you pull the other way, when are you gonna get that you don’t have to do shit alone anymore?”

    “You weren’t getting shit!”

    “ Jesus Kate! We were on it! We have people working on it, Matt, Shield, other people, who were all trying to help us with this: Connections who had to stop doing to get us a plane to get to you, by the way - instead of searching for any members of Hydra who slivered away!” Clint pinched the bridge of his nose with his finger and thumb.

    Laura’s hand went to his other hand and squeezed it, she looked sadly at Kate.

    He let out a sigh, his anger flooding from him. “We’ve only known for a few days, Kate, we were on it . What’s it gonna take to get you to trust me? To realise that for better or worse you’re part of our family: that you’ve got to trust us. That’s the only way this is going to work.”

    “Well, maybe it’s not working.” The fire was still in Kate, she begged herself to let it go, but she just wasn’t… every bit of information Eleanor had “ What ?”

    “You should have just left me alone. I haven’t had a dad for years, I don’t need one now. We were better off before we knew each other!”

    She could see Clint’s heart break.

    Because of her .

    She sucked in her breath, she shook her head, “I-”

    She cut herself off as the door to the apartment flung open.

    “Did you miss me already, Clinton?” A man sauntered in, yawning and carrying bags of groceries, he didn’t bother to look at any of them, “Or are you in trouble? Actually it's you, I think we both know the answer to that.”

    Clint finally tore his eyes away from Kate, looking towards Laura without meeting her eyes. His voice was monotone but there were tears in his eyes, he didn't bother to acknowledge the man, though didn't speak to or look at him. “I’m gonna go keep lookout.”

    “Babe-” Laura tried but he was already out of the nearest window.

    She sighed then looked over at Kate, she opened her mouth to say something, she was saying something, but Kate couldn't hear her.

    Kate couldn't hear.

    She couldn't speak.

    She couldn’t breathe.

    She rushed from the room they were in, into the closest room, a bathroom, and collapsed onto the floor, her head in her hands, she began to sob.

    i love you all <3 The Bathroom Floor Chapter Summary Kate and Laura sit in a bathroom and talk Chapter Notes theres a panic attack in this but its not in detail, but just a warning if you're sensitive to that sorta thing, or its been A Day and that'll not help your mood, skip that little bit Alsooooo thank you all for the very thoughtful comments, a lot of you were balanced in both Clint and Kate’s side which tbh is where I’m at, though I also see the people who were more for one than the other, these next few chapters have some talks in and apologies because communication is key in any relationship See the end of the chapter for more notes Sobs overtook Kate’s body as she sat on the tiled floor, she held her head in her hands and when that didn’t feel like enough she wrapped her fingertips in the roots of her hair, pulling slightly, trying to ground herself.

    The first time she realised that her mom was in the room was when Laura’s hands took hers, gently yet firmly taking her hands away from pulling at her hair.

    Kate shook her head, “I, I can’t-” she tried to force words out between her sobs, but the right words wouldn’t come.

    “You’re having a panic attack,” Laura told her calmly, “It’s okay, I’m here, I’m gonna help you. I need you to copy my breathing for me, okay?”

    Kate wanted to tell her that there was no air in the room, but Laura either didn’t notice, or she was right when she told Kate that she was having a panic attack.

    So Kate copied her breathing, until eventually she was breathing better, granted she was still holding off tears ( badly holding off tears she had to admit), but no longer having a panic attack.

    Laura stayed close to her, she passed her a tissue she’d got from somewhere, Kate blew her nose on it, then threw it at the open toilet, she didn’t miss. Like she hadn’t missed all with her verbal blow to Clint.

    She’d fucked up.

    She swallowed but the lump in her throat persisted, she looked at Laura, her watery eyes blurring the image of her mother. “I’m sorry, I’m so sorry.”

    “It’s okay, honey, you didn’t do anything wrong,”

    Kate shook her head, “I fucked up, I, I-” Laura moved her hand and Kate flinched, she watched her mom’s face crumple before Laura could hide it. Kate banged her head back against the tiled wall with a sob, “ Fuck. I’m… so… fuck. ”

    “I shouldn’t have moved so fast when you’re feeling like this, I’m sorry honey.”

    “You shouldn’t have to be sorry!” Laura didn’t react to the sudden change in her volume, instead just looked at her patiently, “I shouldn’t be…! I shouldn’t be like this! There’s something wrong with me ! What if they… What did they do to me? I didn’t become a Widow, or a hydra soldier, but they must have done something! What if I snap and do something?! If I hurt someone, like actually hurt them- more than I, fuck, I fucked up so bad with Clint, fuck, he’s never gonna…” She clenched her teeth, but a tear still escaped her eye, “...she was right…”

    “Hey!” Laura took hold of her shoulders, her voice was unyielding as she stared deeply into Kate’s eyes, “You are not damaged or defective or anything else she said you are.

    You are smart, and kind, and funny, and a goddamn hero Kate. You’re an amazing sister, and a great dog owner, and a perfect daughter, all by just being you .

    You saved your father and me before you could even talk, by giving us someone to fight for, and you’ve saved your very overprotective father so many times since you found him. You’re more perfect than I could ever imagine, and I’ve imagined what you’d be like a lot. I love you. We all love you. You deserve so much, Kate, you deserve the world, you are not broken.”

    Tears were rolling down Kate’s face by the time Laura had called her a hero, when Laura told her she wasn’t broken, Kate threw herself into Laura’s arms: her mom caught her, and held her as she sobbed.

    Kate wasn’t sure how long she cried for, all she knew was by the time she’d stopped she was exhausted. Even once she was done sobbing Laura held her, tight enough that she was there for her, but loose enough that Kate could wiggle out of it, if she wanted.

    Kate didn’t want to, she wanted to be held, to feel someone who loved her right there, because Laura did love her, because she was her mom, a real mom. Kate loved nearly everyone she met, but someone loving her ?… it was rarer, in her experience.

    So she leant against Laura, the person who had come here to find her, because she was worried, not because she wanted to control her.

    She sucked in a breath, Laura’s hand was stroking her hair, it was nice, she’d never had that done before, or maybe she simply didn’t remember it happening.

    “I’m so tired, mom.”

    She felt Laura’s hand stop for a brief moment, then continue to run through her hair, just as naturally.

    “I know you are, baby.”

    Laura pressed a kiss against Kate’s temple, and Kate wanted to cry all over again. She pressed her face deeper against Laura’s shoulder.

    “We’re going to get answers for you, and kill whoever hurt you. And if there’s anything wrong, if you’re sick, or anything, we’ll get you any help you need.”

    “But…” Kate mumbled against Laura, “what if I’m… what if I turn all murderous and… I’m serious,” she pulled herself back so she could look into Laura’s eyes, “I don’t want to hurt any of you.”

    “And you won’t, not because we’ve had more experience fighting, but because you are you , I know that you’ll fight it, and you won’t hurt anyone; and me and your dad will be right there to help you. We’re not leaving your side.”

    A flicker of a smile appeared on Kate’s face, then she sighed, breaking the moment, Laura moved from in front of her to her side, Kate’s left side pressed against Laura’s right side, there was something comforting about having her just there.

    “I’m sorry that I took off without telling you guys, it was… it was reckless, and childish, and I didn’t mean to scare you. I… I figured you’d be pissed, if anything.”

    “I… I know, we were a little pissed, but only when we realised that you’d done it on purpose. At first it was just like we were back….” she shook her head.

    “What happened… I mean, I know a little, but I don’t know what it was like for you.”

    Laura took in a deep breath, “We were in a briefing, we were on the same mission, which was rare since we had you, but they needed specialists, and we were the best. Then the phone rang, there was this big phone, really old, basically a relic; it only rang when it was urgent and bad, I had given my mom the number, when I joined Shield, I forgot she even had it.

    Nick answered it, I’ve never seen him look panicked in my life, but he did then…. He told us, and gave us a plane to fly home, as fast as we could. When we got home Darren, our friend, the one who was secretly in Hydra, he was in hospital, the cops were there, and you weren’t. I didn’t breathe again until they told us it was over, that they’d found you dead…. Losing you changed us, as parents and as people, and it made us scared, very scared.”

    Kate linked her fingers through Laura’s, her mom squeezed her hands in return.

    “I’m sorry, honey. I really am. We both are… Your father and I are just petrified that we’ll lose you, so scared, apparently, that we’re pushing you away. I’m sorry, Kate. We never wanted a life for you where you’d be in any danger, ever.” she let out a laugh, “I think I’m one of the few moms in the world who was made up when my son told me he wanted to be a drummer.”

    Kate smiled, she would have to ask Cooper to show her what he could do, she also needed to apologise for scaring him, and ruining his day out with his friends.

    “We were both thinking about leaving the business, after you were born, did you know that?” Kate shook her head. “We had a whole plan: we’d get normal jobs, move to Iowa, we’d build a house and have normal lives…. but you were taken… After that we realised that we needed to help other people, other families, families who would never know our names, or even that we helped, it’s just who we are. It’s who you are too, Kate, I recognise that, and I respect it, I’m sorry if I ever made you think any different. I mean, I don’t like how scared it makes me feel, but that’s my problem not yours.”

    “It’s… I’m not used to this, talking out arguments, or having people who want to be protective over me,” Kate told her, quietly and without her usual joking to take some of the vulnerability away, “I don’t mind it, I mean I do sometimes, but not often. I.. that sounded so convoluted,” she admitted sheepishly, “What I mean is I don’t mind it, sometimes, maybe dialled back a couple of notches, but you and Clint,” she shrugged, “you’re my mom and dad, you get a pass at being a little overprotective… when it’s not in front of anyone I will be embarrassed seeing the whole mama bear act.”

    Laura laughed, and after a second Kate lauded too, though the last part of her admittance was not a complete joke.

    “How about we get up off of the floor, we can have some tea, and we can talk some more, and you can meet your uncle.”

    “Oh that guy who walked in?”

    “Yep.”

    “Oh, I assumed he was robbing the place.”

    Laura raised one eyebrow, with amusement on her face, “And you were fine with him robbing the place?”

    Kate shrugged, “He managed to get into a place with super subtle security, he should get to take whatever he wants.”

    “You are so like Clint,” Laura laughed, shaking her head. She stood up, and held out her hand to Kate. “We can also talk about anything any of us know so far, and we can make a plan.” Kate took her hand and got to her feet, she rolled up the sleeves of the hoodie she was wearing so it wasn’t covering her hands. Clint’s hoodie. “That sounds good but I think I need to go talk to Clint first. I was kinda a dick to him.”

    Laura sent her a sympathetic smile, “You’ve been through a lot, and you’re both so alike that you’re going to end up rubbing each other the wrong way sometimes, I know he hasn’t been making it much easier. He’ll understand, honey.”

    Kate gave her a thankful, tight, and incredibly fake smile.

    The thing was, Clint had been trying to make it easier, and it had worked about eighty three percent of the time, when he didn’t take it too far. He’d tried to give her time, and to be there to talk to, and playing hide and seek to help her forget all of the shit going on.

    She hoped he would understand, and that he’d understand what Kate needed from him.

    “Hey, er, Laura, can you break the news to, er, the guy? Before I come back down here?”

    “Who? Oh, Barney? Of course.”

    “Thanks, I never know what to do when people find out who I am. Oh and; can I have coffee? Tea’s gross.”

    Laura let out a soft laugh, “Just say that to your father and he won’t care about anything you say to him, he’ll be on your side.”

    Kate smiled, then quickly hugged her mom, before leaving the bathroom.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Clint was easy to find, sat on a small wall on top of the roof, just staring out at the city, unblinking.

    He blinked as Kate sat down beside him, close enough that their shoulders skimmed without touching the whole while. Kate looked out at the same view, wondering what Clint saw, if he saw more after all the times he’d gotten into close spots, as an avenger and as an agent. She sipped her hand into her pocket, pulling out the paper token he’d given her, then held it out to him, biting her lip as she did. If she was him she wouldn’t take it. Not after what she’d said to him.

    He took it from her, a flicker of a smile on his face, he let out a breath of laughter from his nose.

    The two looked out over the view instead of each other.

    “I shouldn’t have said what I said.”

    Clint shook his head slightly, “You weren’t wrong. You haven’t had a dad for years, and I haven’t been one to you in years, I’m not sure how to do all this, to be honest.”

    “You’re doing alright,” she told him quietly, “and you are my dad. I just don’t know how to…” she trailed off into silence.

    He didn’t ask her to expand, he understood. “You’re also not wrong, when you said I treat you like a kid… I don’t mean to, I really don’t… it’s just… you were never supposed to have to go through what you went through, Katie, none of it. Hell, right now we should be home, with in jokes, and stories, and arguing over you getting an ugly tattoo, or telling you to take a gap year to travel around Europe, or telling you not to, or me grumbling to your mom that I don’t like whoever you’re dating… We should know each other.”

    “We were getting to know each other before.”

    She shrugged, “You warmed up to me eventually.”

My dad was a complete bastard, he… he drank a lot, probably more than Derek did… I don’t know if I’m just remembering him drunk all of the time, or if he really was, but he was an angry drunk.”

    “…..Like Derek and Eleanor.”

He told me that with dad at least he knew what happened, how to play the game, our mom was… she was a little erratic, and her love had to be earned half the time. The other half she was a great mom, seriously she was fun and kind and was proud of us, but she hadn’t had the best examples of motherhood, and my dad had helped with destroying what she classed as good parenting.”

    “But you went with her?”

I didn’t want her to be alone,” he said softly. “We lived out of the car, luckily this was back in the eighties so the car was pretty big, and I was only young. We travelled around the Midwest, mostly going around circuses, there were a lot of them around back then.”

It always felt wrong, she told me once, you can feel that you’re doing the right thing by listening to this,” his hand went to his heart. ”Eventually she decided it wasn’t enough, we were barely getting by, going to sleep in the car hungry, sneaking into motels to shower. So we held up a gas station.”

She told me to take the shot.”

I ended up in an orphanage, a few of them actually, eventually Barney and I ended up in the same one, but we were both different than we had been, we weren’t brothers like we had been.”

    “...I’m sorry… I didn’t know.”

But I still think about my mom, she taught me to listen to my heart above everything else… I think I’ve been ignoring it recently, by not listening to you.”

    Kate found herself leaning against his side as he spoke, “I’ve not been…. I don’t know how to be part of a family, or how to get through all of this… I’ve been searching for the answers so I don’t have to figure that out, I guess.”

    “I’m not sure how to be your dad. I want to be , you have no idea… and I was, for a while… You and your mom were the reasons I came back from missions: I swore I’d protect you from growing up like I did. That you could focus on being a kid, that I could hide all the bad bits of the world from you, that you could do what you were doing before I came along. Before the Ronin suit.

    But you’re right, you’re not a little kid, and as much as I want to protect you from everything and anything I know that’s well in the past. I’m sorry that I’ve pushed you away Katie, that you didn’t feel like you could just come and talk to me about it.”

    Kate was silent for a minute, before taking a slow breath in, her last panic attack had snuck up on her, she didn’t want to have another one, not right now. “It’s not just your fault. I… I’ve never had people to talk to, like that… I mean Eleanor and Derek weren’t really open to discussions. I’m not great at that sorta communicating, the truthful emotional kind, the real sort. I… I should have talked to you, or to Laura, I shouldn’t have disappeared. I figured you’d be mad at me, that maybe you wouldn’t forgive me… I didn’t realise you’d be scared for me .”

    Clint turned Kate to face him, he looked deadly serious, with his blue eyes shining with tears, “Kate, you never have to worry about being hurt around us, ever. You’ll never be berated, or insulted, never hit, or starved, or kicked out, or anything else that you might have in the past. You’re safe with us, you can say anything to us, we’re always gonna be on your side, Katie, always . And we’re always going to be here to find you, every single time, because we love you.”

    Kate felt her bottom lip wobble, she had thought that she’d cried out all of her tears, but apparently there were still some in her, “promise?” she asked, her voice breaking, she was raw and vulnerable and there was nothing she could do about that right then, other than let Clint see her that way.

    To be one of the few she let see like that.

    “C’mere, kid,” Clint didn’t hesitate to pull her into his arms. He pressed his lips to her temple, the opposite side than Laura’s kiss had landed, but just as loving. “I promise.”

    She hoped that he wouldn’t notice that she was crying against his jacket when he would inevitably pull away, but judging from how he rubbed a continuous circle onto her back he had noticed.

    He didn’t pull away, like Laura hadn’t, and neither did she.

    It was several minutes before either of them spoke.

    “I’m still not sure how to do this, I don’t know how to be a good daughter, I don’t want to fuck this all up” she admitted quietly, not sure if he’d hear her or not, especially when he was silent for a minute.

    “You don’t have to be anything other than you, that’s more than enough for all of us, believe me. And, look, we’re all still figuring this out, kid, how to be a family, it’s gonna take a while, I’m sure. But you gotta stop beating yourself up about this, Kid, … I think the only one who knows what they’re doing is Lucky.”

    Kate laughed at his attempt to lighten the mood, pulling back from him, her eyes red from tears, but a smile back on her face, she wiped a stray tear from her cheek with the sleeve of her hoodie, “Yeah he’s got it made.”

    “Yeah, now he’s being fed more than pizza,” he joked, looking at her with a raised brow and a smirk, the smirk quickly broke and he smiled softly at her, “I really am sorry, Katie-Kate. Just yell at me when I’m next being an idiot?”

    Kate smirked, “I can do that.”

    Clint laughed and shook his head fondly, “You’re so like your mom.”

    Kate smiled with fondness and pride, “She told me I’m like you.”

    Clint beamed at that, “She did mean that in a good way right?”

    Kate rolled her eyes at his joke but couldn’t fight off the smile, “I think so, but maybe she meant that I’m stubborn and bad at decorating christmas trees.”

    Clint laughed again, “Well, you definitely get your art skills from me.”

    “Are you an amazing artist too?” She kept her face as serious as she could, feigning ignorance over her less than stellar skills. She smiled at her father as he threw his head back and laughed.

    “We should head back inside,” he told her when he finally stopped laughing, “if you get sick you just know that your mom will mom you to death.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, but she fought the small smile at the thought of being looked after, she wasn’t sure really what that would be like, and wondered if she’d ever experience it. “Like you wouldn’t be just as bad.”

    “I make a killer soup,” he told her seriously, “though I’ve already patched you up plenty for this year.”

    “Okay, I won’t get sick or injured until next year then,” she told him sarcastically.

    “Imma hold you to that.”

    She shook her head with a laugh and started to lead the way off of the fire door.

    “Oh, hey, Katie?”

    She turned back to him, he was only a few steps behind her, but he held his hand out to her. No, not just his hand.

    The token.

    “Clean slate,” he told her, a peace offering, probably exactly what they both needed, “you get another chance to scare the shit outta me.”

    Kate took the token, she smiled down at it, she wasn’t sure she ever wanted to lose it, not because she didn’t enjoy scaring him a little (when it didn’t ruin everything), but because it was the first present from him. Her dad.

    She placed the token in her hoodie, and smiled softly at her dad. “I’ll wait ‘til next year to do that too.”

    Clint walked forward and threw his arm around her, the pair of Hawkeyes walked through the door, ready to join the rest of their family.
