    Chapter Summary Kate meets her uncle Chapter Notes ngl had no idea what to write in this because it needed a little cool down filler chapter after the last few so... yeah See the end of the chapter for more notes

    Kate sat at the table, cradling a mug of coffee in her hands, on either side of her were her parents, and across from her father was her Uncle Barney, who kept glancing at her like he was suspicious of her, it didn’t exactly make her immediately like him, but then Clint had also been prickly when she’d first met him, so perhaps she shouldn’t judge him so fast.

    Kate watched him, hoping that he wouldn’t catch her looking. He looked a little like Clint, she supposed; he had darker hair which seemed tidier than she’d ever seen Clint’s, and he was wearing a shirt which wasn’t even plaid, something she’d also never seen on her father. He was a little older than Clint, and had just as many frown lines, not as many laughter lines though. But there was a clear similarity to how alert he was, he didn’t seem to be, he was sat relaxed in the seat, sipping black coffee, but there was a sharpness in his eyes which Kate was getting used to seeing in Clint’s eyes, and Laura’s, he was obviously in a similar professional field as they were.

    Clint yawned and poured himself more coffee from the carafe on the table, he took a long sip.

    “You’re getting old.” Barney remarked, a slight smirk on his face, and Kate couldn’t help but smile at the dig too.

    “I’m younger than you!” The archer protested, “I was up early; Kate and I were training.” Kate was relieved that he hadn’t said that he was clearly trying to keep her mind occupied after the nightmares the previous day, and she supposed they were training, in a way, after all training didn’t have to be boring.

    “Hmm.” He looked at Kate properly now, for the first time since Clint and Kate had , “I saw you guys on the news: you’re good, better than my brother, anyway.”

    Kate raised her eyebrows, smiling, she glanced from Barney to Clint, then back again, “I don’t think I’m that good, yet.” She looked back at Clint, with a smile implying that she was going to beat him one day soon.

    He just smiled back at her with pride, “You’re definitely in the top two greatest archers in the world.”

    Kate blushed, looking back at her coffee.

    “So, you’re gonna be the new ‘Hawkeye’?”

    Kate studied Barney’s face, she could tell that he was mocking something, but she wasn’t sure what until she heard Clint groan loudly.

    “Again with this?”

    “I’m just saying, you don’t fly, why are you names after a fuckin’ bird man?”

    Kate laughed as Clint groaned again, and Barney smiled, laughter in the smile even if he didn’t make a noise.

    “Because I fire arrows from up high!”

    “You don’t always,” Kate pointed out, earning a smile from Barney and a glare from Clint, “What? You don’t! Maybe you can ask Captain America for some wings like his.”

    “You’re a traitor.”

    Kate beamed, she glanced at her mom who seemed to be both amused to and used to the brother’s argument. “Why are you even called that? It’s not super lame is it, ‘cause if so I’m using a different name.”

    “He watched a lot of MASH-”

    “Because I’m up high most of the time, or I used to be,” Clint said over his brother, “and I always have a good eye for how things are gonna play out. Plus I was born in Iowa, people from there used to be known as that.”

    “ I don’t call myself that,” Barney told Kate.

    Kate twitched her lips from side to side slowly, trying her best not to smile before she wound Clint up some more.

    “Wait, so, when you were on mission with the Black Widow would Phil or whoever be like,”

    she put on an imitation of Phil’s voice, “‘there goes the bird and the spider, totally normal codenames, not lame at all’?”

    “No, because it was Hawk eye, and Black Widow . Not Bird and Spider.”

    “You know what would help with the whole name thing?”

    “Don’t say it-”

    “ Branding .”

    Barney let out a snort, but was smiling as he spoke, “This is why I never got into the whole superhero shit.”

    “Sure, Barns, this is why,” Clint smirked back at his brother.

    “But you’re a spy, right?”

    Barney raised his eyebrows, “I didn’t realise your father told you,” he spoke slowly, shooting Clint a sideways look.

    “Oh, he didn’t. I guessed.”

    “You guessed?”

    Kate shrugged her shoulders, smiling slightly, “It’s… kinda obvious, y’know like how you can always tell who’s an undercover cop? You just kinda, have the spy vibes?”

    “I don’t know what that means,” He told her bluntly, but he was smiling, he glanced at Clint, with something akin to approval in his eyes, then back at Kate. “Once you’re done with college, come see me about a job.”

    “ No .”

    Kate’s head snapped to Clint, after their talk outside she didn’t expect Clint to go back on his word so fast, that he wouldn’t be too overprotective.

    “What?” Clint asked, clueless to the reason why both Kate and Laura were giving him looks, “Shield is so much better than the CIA! And the Avengers is better than both! Why are you both…?” he tilted his head to the side as both women smiled at him, he shot a helpless look at his brother.

    “You’re very cute, babe.” Laura assured him, reaching past Kate to squeeze his hand, looking completely in love with him.

    Kate was all out beaming, “You really think I could be an Avenger?”

    “Sure, I mean we already know some people who could make you a costume.” He winked.

    Kate felt that stirring of pride which she had felt when they sat on the train, after everything they’d been through especially in the past few hours.

    “You’ve gotta complete college first,” Laura told her, but she had the same pride on her face as Clint.

    She shrugged her shoulders, “That’s easy.”

    Barney let out a snort of laughter, “You’ll be the first Barton to do it so far,” he gave her a small nod, his approval, whatever it was worth to her.

    “You okay with us crashing here for a few days, Barns?”

    “Sure, Simone’s away with the boys at her parents, so it’s just me here.”

    “Who’s Simone?” Laura asked, leaning towards her brother-in-law, excited at the possible gossip, “Do you have a girlfriend?”

    “Yeah, for a year now, she stays over with the boys pretty often, she has an apartment a few floors down.”

    “Wait, you have a girlfriend?!” Clint sounded incredulous, “Why didn’t you tell me?”

    “When would I have told you, Clinton?”

    “I met up with you with the kids the other day,” Clint glared at him.

    Kate felt a tap on her hand, she looked down at Laura’s hand on hers, when she looked up at her mom she watched her tilt her head away from the table.

    Kate caught what Laura was hinting, and pushed her chair back, following Laura’s lead of leaving the two brothers.

    “For all of twenty minutes for coffee .”

    Kate felt the hair on the back of her neck stand up, the tension was palpable, she knew it was the start of an argument. But Laura took hold of her hand and gently squeezed it, reassuringly.

    Laura led her towards the archway leaving the living area.

    “We had a busy day! And I thought you were in Barcelona until about an hour before you texted me!”

    “I got back last week… and I’ve been around for the last several years, Clint, you could have seen me at any point.”

    Kate glanced at Laura, seeing her grimace.

    “They’re gonna be doing this for a while,” she told her kindly.

    She was led to an office, which was plain, if a little messy. There weren't many decorations in the room, in fact there were barely any signs of the room not being bought directly from a catalogue, only four frames on the desk Laura perched on, two on either side.

    Kate dropped onto the couch opposite the desk.

    “He’s not wrong, your uncle, I mean. Clint went all… during the snap… He didn’t reach out to anyone; not Barney, not Tasha, Steve, Tony, Bruce, Thor… any of them.” She looked sad. “I’m not sure what I would have done, if I was in his shoes, but I know why he stopped talking to them, once he started, he was ashamed.”

    Laura picked up a photo frame. She smiled at it, still looking sad, “He should have known that Barney would have understood, out of everyone.” She passed the frame over to Kate.

    Kate looked at the photo, the younger Clint she had seen in the photos earlier, this time he was beside a young version of his brother, she was stuck by how much older Barney looked than Clint, she hadn’t realised how much her supposed death, the Blip, and the deaths of so many of his other family and friends, had aged him. They both looked so happy. So did she… And Barney kept the photo up in his office, along with only three others.

    She didn’t know what it meant, but it made her feel a certain way, though she couldn’t name that either.

    “They look like they used to be close.”

    Laura thought for a second, “Their relationship has always been complicated, they had some good years, but they’re a lot like each other, they beat themselves up about things they can’t fix.”

    Kate frowned slightly.

    “I’m gonna go call Maria, are you okay sticking here? I’ll be back in a few.”

    “Of course,” Kate nodded, watching Laura walk to the door, “Hey, Laura?” Laura stopped and turned back to her. “Clint asked if we could stay here for a few days, is it because… are we in danger?”

    Laura hesitated, “Not right this moment, we’re keeping an eye out Eleanor Bishop was high up in the Hand, powerful, even more so now we know why you were taken. We’ve got to stay alert, we don’t know who’ll be after her, or after you.”

    Kate shivered, she thought that she hid it, but her mom walked over and brought her into a hug.

    “You’re safe with us, and we can all take them on,” she pulled back, placing her hands lovingly on Kate’s cheeks, “can’t we?”

    Kate smiled, reaching up to squeeze Laura’s hand, a silent thank you. “Of course. Thanks mom.” She got to see Laura’s hundred watt smile, she had to look away, but found that she was still smiling herself.

    “I’ll go make my call,” Laura repeated, smiling as she walked to the door. “We’ll sit down soon, figure out what to do next, make a plan, if we have to fight your uncle will be able to help us…” She opened the door and walked out, “... if your father doesn’t kill him first,” she closed the door behind her.

    The guilt she had felt when she had first opened her phone only increased reading all of them. If she had seen them before, if her phones had been linked, she might have stopped, or at the very least messaged them, reassuring them the best she could.

    The messages that hurt more were from Cooper and Lila, like Clint and Laura they were hoping that she was okay, but Cooper’s earlier messages were ones that he would have probably sent to Lila or Nate, used to playing the big brother; he had reassured her that he was on his way to find her.

    Mom and dad said they’ve found you… I wish you’d have told me, I could have covered for you. I hope you’re okay, Coop.

    “Kate? Are you okay?”

    She could hear the smile in his voice, and couldn’t help but smile to herself, “Hey Coop, I’m good. I’m… I’m really sorry, I shouldn’t have put you in that position, I was… I’m a really shitty big sister.”

    “You’re not really shitty,” he told her without hesitation, when he spoke again it was to whomever he was around, “I was quoting it, it doesn’t count… nah, Kate… will do..”

    As he spoke to the person Kate ran her finger along the top of the closest frame, one with a photo of Cooper, Lila, and a baby in, who she assumed was Nate, it was pretty old considering Nate barely looked a few months old in it.

    “Aunt Maria says hi, and that you’re just like dad, so she’ll kill him for being stupid, not you.”

    Kate let out a snort of laughter, “I’ll tell him… Coop,-”

    “-Don’t worry, Kate, we’re cool.”

    “Wh- how…?” she shook her head, tapping her fingers on the table as though she was playing a piano, “You don’t have to be cool with it, seriously, you can be mad at me, I’d be mad.”

    “Nah you wouldn’t,” he told her with absolute certainty, “I mean… we’re pretty alike, we’re brother and sister after all, and if I was in your shoes you wouldn’t hate me, right?”

    Kate hesitated, she guessed if it was one of her siblings in her shoes she would have their back like Cooper had hers, it was only herself she’d be mad at. “Thanks,” she murmured quietly enough that she wasn’t sure he’d hear it, she still wasn’t sure he heard it. “I’m still sorry I made you miss hanging out with your friends, mom said that you’re with Maria somewhere?”

    “Yeah, it’s chill ‘cause if I ever want her and Lila off my back I just get Nate to try and hug Maria.”

    Kate snorted, “Why?”

    “Oh, Maria, she’s our godmother, by the way, does not like kids. Seriously she’s only just starting to like me- she’s wary around Lila, and Nate scares her more than you were scared by him when you first came home.”

Faking it for their parents.

    “You should have saved it for her birthday, now you’ve still gotta get her a present.”

    “How?”

    Kate was sure that his face was lookin how hers had not long ago, “I thought that she actually hated it or… fuck, I don’t know…”

    He was silent again. “Did she smile?”

    “Hmm? Er, yeah, yeah she did, I mean I didn’t see her face the first time but when I did it she hugged me tighter than she had been, which I didn’t think was possible, but Yelena’s right she’s a badass.”

    “She’s right, and mom loved it, trust me.” Kate was quiet again, “Seriously Kate, you have no idea how happy you’ll have made her.”

    Kate cursed her brain for once again doubting her eyes, she had seen how happy Laura had been, why was it so hard to believe that she had made someone, especially a parent, happy? “How ‘bout dad?”

    “Hmm? Oh..,” she let out a sigh, “not yet.”

    “That’s okay, he’ll get it.”

    “I know he will, it’s just… he’s Clint, y’know? It makes it a lot more…” she let out another sigh this time a lot heavier. “I looked up to him my whole childhood, he’s why I became an amazing archer, why I try to help people when I can, but now he’s not just…”

    “He’s a real person now, and real to you, not just some character in a book?”

    “Shit… how the fuck did you get some wise?” She heard him laugh. “Seriously! How did you know that?”

    Cooper was silent for a second, “You're not allowed to use this against me.”

    “I swear nothing can be more embarrassing than our dad knowing I used to idolise him.”

    “I used to idolise Iron Man.”

    “That’s not as bad as mine and you know it,” she told him, rolling her eyes at him even thinking that they measured up.

    “Dad brought them home when I was younger, before Nate was born, and I woke up early and borrowed one of Tony’s pulsars, and tried to fly off the roof.”

    “Holy shit! Were you okay!?”

    “Yeah, I guess Tony heard me take them ‘cause he came and saved me, he explained to me how powerful they were, telling me everything about them, and how many times he got hurt when he first made the suit, then he got me a juice pop, and one for himself, he never told my parents, I didn’t either. But after that he wasn’t just some badass superhero, he was real, it was better, but it made it different.”

    Kate nodded her head slowly before remembering that he couldn’t see her, “yeah,” she said softly. She took a second before speaking again, “I do want to call him that, I mean, he is my dad… it’s just…”

    “There’s plenty of time.”

    “Hmm…” she let out a loud sigh. “I don’t think I want to wait years… I just want things to feel normal again. I’m tired.”

    To his credit Cooper didn’t tell her empty platitudes, he was just on the other side of the phone, and that was enough.

    “I gotta go, Auntie Maria’s getting all stressy about how to cook Dino nuggets.”

    Kate snorted, looking forward to meeting Maria one day, “I’ll see you soon, I’ll make sure we’re not here for ages… I’m sorry that I made us all… yeah.”

    “Don’t sweat it. I’ll see ya soon, Kate.”

    “Say hi to Lila and Nate from me.”

    “Sure, I’m pretty sure Lila will text you later that I’m annoying her, so you can say hi then too.”

    “Why’s she going to text me that?”

    “Because I’m planning on annoying her.”

    Kate laughed, her heart lighter than it had been when she had first rung him.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate had only just put the phone down when Laura came back into the office, she looked a little sheepish, almost sneaking into the room. Kate tried not to laugh at the sight, and was only successful because she was so confused.

    “Sorry, I thought you were on the phone.”

    “I was. I just finished,” she held up her phone as proof, “How’s Maria?”

    “She’s good, they’re in the countryside so she doesn’t really have a signal, why’re- Oh ! I wasn’t on the phone to her, why’d you think…?” She trailed off, Laura was smiling knowingly, but Kate had no idea what exactly her mother was knowing. “It was Cooper… I felt bad about… well, everything.”

    Laura’s knowing smile was replaced with a soft one, “How’d the call go?”

    “Good. I apologised to him, and then we just chatted.”

    “About anything in particular?”

    Kate could “And I’m sure that you told your little brother to go easy on your Aunt Maria.”

    Katie had to hide her smile from the new knowing look Laura sent her, “Of course! I’m the best influence, I would never encourage him or anyone doing anything wrong.”

    Laura chuckled, “God you’re all too like your father.”

    Kate beamed.

    “I’m glad you’re bonding with your brother, it’s nice seeing you getting close, even if you’re as good an influence as he is.”

    There was a small knock at the office door, then Clint’s face popped through, he looked even more sheepish than Laura had, Kate felt bad for him when she saw the disappointed look. She watched him walk fully into the office, closing the door behind him.

    His eyes drifted around the room automatically, they landed on the photo frame she had clocked earlier, the one of the person who was clearly Barney’s girlfriend.

    He scrubbed his hand through his hair on the back of his head, “Hey.”

    “Hey,” Kate said when Laura just sat there. “Are you…okay? I didn’t hear you guys killing each other but...” She tried to tell herself to shush, but she couldn’t stop herself from trying to fill the awkward silence, but Clint sent her a soft smile, he knew that she couldn’t help it. “I’m good, kiddo, so’s Barney, we talked it out.” He sucked in a breath, still looking ashamed despite his attempt to lighten the mood. “Katie, I’m sorry that I started a fight with your uncle.”

    “It’s fine,” she shrugged it off.

    Clint shook his head, “It’s not, I know that you’re not comfortable with confrontation, I’m sorry I made you feel stressed at all, I didn’t think, that was dumb of me, especially after today.”

    Kate was still shocked by someone, an actual adult, a parent apologising to her, despite it not even being the first time that day that it happened.

    She tried to send him a small smile, the only way she could think of answering him, but it was a fragile thing. He was her dad, and that was something which was still wondrous to her, not because he was the original Hawkeye, but like Cooper said, it became a wondrous fact because Clint was Clint .

    “It’s okay, really, Clint.” She told him when she saw the concern on his face as he looked closely at her. She sent him a smile, before standing up, walking slowly over to him, and pulled him into a hug. He was clearly surprised as it took him a couple of seconds to wrap his arms around her in return. “Thanks for the apology though,” she whispered to him, “I guess you’re not as emotionally constipated as I thought you were,” she smiled as she felt him laugh.

    When she pulled out of the hug she sent him another smile, then glanced at Laura who was watching them with a soft smile identical to her own. Kate looked back at Clint and sent him a sympathetic smile, “I’ll leave you two to talk.”

    “What-?” He glanced at Laura who was still sitting in her seat, “We’re good, right honey?” he sent Laura an almost begging look, “We don’t need a whole talk-” he swallowed when she sent him a stern look, then sent a look to Kate.

    Kate didn’t know whether to feel bad for her father or to laugh at the fact that The Hawkeye was scared of his wife who was clearly going to make him talk about his emotions… okay she wouldn’t have wanted that either, but she still chose the latter option, laughed and ducked her head, she shook it, then headed to the door.

    “Good luck!” She called back in a sing-song voice.

    “Traitor!”

    She laughed at Clint’s retort, but carried on walking.

    The kitchen was empty when she walked into it, she glanced around for any sign of Barney, Clint had said everything was okay between them now, but she was still cautious, it wasn’t like she knew the man, and the CSI didn’t exactly have the best track record on not killing people at random…

    There was no sign of her uncle, so she drifted towards the coffee pot, she placed her hand on the side, “Fuck,” she let out a hiss and pulled her hand back fast before she could get burnt. “Careful, that’s a new pot.”

    Her head popped up as Barney walked through the front door, he sent her a closed smile, like people did on the street (okay not New York, but somewhere surely did).

    “Oh, erm, do you want some?”

    He looked surprised but smiled slightly more genuinely and nodded his head, “Please, there’s some creamer in the fridge if you want some, some flavoured something one, maybe peppermint, or gingerbread?”

    Kate smiled and poured two mugs of coffee, she slid one over to Barney, and opened the fridge, grabbing a gingerbread creamer, she poured some into her coffee, then held it out to Barney, he shook his head so she placed it back into the fridge.

    “I’m guessing the themed creamer is your girlfriend’s?”

    Barney looked surprised, and Kate wondered how both Barney and Clint were some kind of superspies… “Er, yeah, yeah it’s Simone's.”

    “She looks nice, I saw her picture in your office, with the two kids?”

    She could have sworn that Barney’s face turned into that of a goofy cartoon character, complete with heart eyes, “Charlie and Ben,” he sounded proud of them. “They’re good kids, I met her while she was still pregnant with Ben. Charlie liked me for some reason, and so did Simone. I’ve been in their lives ever since.”

    Kate frowned slightly, “Why’re you surprised that they like you?”

    He shrugged, “I was never really around kids; I mean obviously my dumbass brother,”

    luckily he sounded fond when he said that, “but otherwise I never really had friends with kids or worked anywhere near them. I think the first time I held a baby was you”

    Kate smiled ever so slightly as she sipped her drink, she smiled wider when she saw the look on his face at the mere idea of the flavoured creamer in her coffee.

    He shook his head with the same fondness he had used while talking about Clint just before, he slid into a stool at the island, and didn’t say anything as Kate pulled herself up to sit on the counter. “I’m sure you’re getting tired of people saying they met you when you can’t remember it.”

    Kate shrugged, “It’s… a little weird? Y’know, that loads of people knew me, but I don’t think it could be any weirder than finding out who my dad was, after a few days of him helping me clean up the mess in the city.” She liked how he smiled when she made sure to point out that Clint helped her not the other way around.

    He nodded slowly, “I can see how that’d be weird. I… I know it’s weird people knowing you before you knew them, but I just wanted to… You’re the reason Clint and I still talk.”

    “What do you mean, ‘cause I fucked up and we had to crash here? Thanks but don’t say that in front of my parents or they’ll give me those disappointed looks again.”

    Barney let out a snort of laughter, “Well, yes today, but also when you were a baby; we hadn’t talked in a long time, years in fact, we’d got in a lot of arguments when we were teenagers, I tried to get straight and Clinton wasn’t so keen to follow in my footsteps. We had a big fight one time, he was just a kid, barely eighteen, but it was a bad fight. We didn’t speak in years; I didn’t realise he even had my number, but he told me he needed me, so I went, and then when I got to his place in DC he was holding you. He had his family, and he wanted me to still be part of it, he said you would need an uncle.”

    Kate smiled, but it faltered slightly, “I’m sorry that you guys fell out… again.”

    Barney let out a laugh, “You didn’t grow up with any siblings did you?”

    She shook her head.

    “You can be a huge dick to your siblings, they’ll forgive you,” he told her sagely, “I’ve been one to Clint, and he’s been one to me, often. We forgive each other, because there’s no other option. I’m sure you’ll get to know that with your brothers and sister.”

    Kate thought about her phone call with Cooper, she thought that maybe Barney was right.

    She couldn’t imagine having to forgive any of them for anything, but she couldn’t help but hope that she would have the opportunity to, it was better than having to be forgiven.

    “Kate?”

    Her head snapped to Barney, she realised that she must have been stuck in her own head.

    “I just wanted to say sorry, that you had to see me and your dad argue.” He glanced towards his office as though worried Clint and Laura would hear his apology.

    “Oh, it’s, it was nothing.”

    He shook his head every so slightly, which only made her keep her eyes on him out of curiosity.

    “Seriously, Barney, it’s fine.”

    “I know that feeling, the… the drop , in your stomach, and your heart, when people start arguing, when you had that kinda thing around when you’re growing up.” she must have looked at him a certain way because he carried on talking, “Your dad didn’t say anything, he wouldn’t do that, I can just tell; experience . I’m just sorry you had to grow up with that shit. If you want me to kill that lady for you then I will, just say the word.”

    She smiled ever so slightly, “Thanks, but I don’t want her killed. Hey Barney?”

    “Yeah, kid?”

    “If I start naming people will you tell me if the CIA actually killed them?”

    He let out a sigh, “Kate, I can’t just-”

    “JFK?” She asked, completely ignoring him, “Actually I already know that one, how about-” Barney threw his head back and let out a loud groan.

    Kate was half glaring at Barney when Clint and Laura walked back in, holding hands, they both gave her a confused look.

    “He won’t tell me who the CIA have assassinated,” She pouted, smiling as Laura laughed, clearly relieved that was all that was wrong, and Clint shared a look with her which clearly showed that he had tried to ask the same thing, probably multiple times.

    “You don’t have clearance,” Barney pointed out, almost apologetically.

    “You’re now my least favourite uncle.”

    “I’m your only uncle!”

    “And now my least favourite.”

    Barney glared at Clint, as though he had said it, “I don’t know why you needed a DNA test, she’s clearly completely yours.”

    Kate grinned with pride, as did Clint.

    She sat in another one of Clint’s stolen hoodies, and some sweats Laura had brought with her, along with her favourite Laika shirt, she sat on a lounge chair, watching her uncle take his turn.

    The game had been made up years ago, according to Clint, in a children's’ home he and Barney had been in, and over the years it had evolved, depending on where it was being played.

    Laura and Barney had both insisted on waylaying the climbing up to the top of the building using only their arms pulling themselves up the fire escape, pointing out that they still had to be careful, though Clint pointed out that it was because his brother was old.

    They had done a shots contest, using some weird flavoured spirits which Barney was desperate to get rid of, Kate was impressed when her mom had won, without seeming drunk at all, Kate had come second, though she already knew that she could easily beat Clint drinking.

    The eating contest had been impossible to recreate how they had played it in the past: They used to eat crap leftovers, especially when any combination of the three of them were playing, but as Laura had pointed out Barney didn’t have any month old leftovers like they had when she and Clint were on mission with Natasha.

    Kate would have to tell Yelena all about it, she knew that she would like to hear about her sister, to know that she had fun, and perhaps to remind her that Natasha and Clint had been best friends… just to be on the safe side. Kate had to admit that it was nice to see Clint and Laura talk about Natasha without looking sad. And she had already gone through her fair share of eating food which was growing mould.

    She had dominated when they had played the alternative version of the eating contest though, it was pretty much a traditional eating contest, in fact she had eaten eight plates full of food, then an hour later was searching for dessert.

    Laura was the overall best, particularly at the races and obstacle courses, and the ‘defuse the potato bomb’ game, and any code breaking games, and the charades and scrabble which somehow made their way into the game, probably Laura’s ‘suggestion’ knowing that she would dominate.

    Kate was currently dead last, but not too many points behind her parents and uncle, she was mostly just happy to be involved in the game, apparently it was something which not everyone was invited to take part in.

    “How many points do I get for hitting a bus with this thing?”

    “None!”

    “Zero!”

    “That’s not in the rules and you know it!”

    She, Laura, and Clint shouted respectively.

    Barney rolled his eyes, “That’s ridiculous.”

    “A bus is bigger than a taxi, why would you get any extra points?”

    Kate let out a tiny snort of a laugh at how competitive Clint and Barney were.

    She shared a look with Laura, who was looking cocky, “You’re not going to step in?” She asked her quietly yet amused.

    “Nope, Barn knows that your dad’s not gonna let him have it,” she took a sip of her coffee, “and neither of them can beat me, so they can argue all they like.”

    Kate laughed and shook her head, but she couldn’t shake her wide smile, she knew that she was dead last with no chance of winning, but something about her mom being so cocky made her smile, it was like seeing a glimpse of what Laura might have been like when she was Kate’s age.

    “Get on with it!” Kate heckled her uncle.

    Barney rolled his eyes and grumbled something under his breath, but Kate could see the glint in his eye.

    She watched carefully as he drew his hand back, a silver disk a little bigger than a quarter held between his fingers. He swung his arm towards the direction of the road, at the very last moment he flicked his hand.

    The coin hurtled towards the road; the coin hit the top of the first cab, it bounced to the taxi behind, and did the same with the one behind that, and the one behind that, before falling onto the road and rolled through the traffic and down a storm drain.

    “Ohhhhh! So close Barn! Yet soooo far!”

    Kate couldn’t help but laugh at her father’s reaction, but Barney just rolled his eyes but didn’t raise to the bait.

    “Looks like your lot is finally gone, Clinton, that means you can’t cheat.”

    “Pshaw,” Clint let out, accompanying it with a brushing off gesture.

    “What do you mean? How can you even tell they’re gone?”

    “Haven’t you noticed a lot of taxis around?” Clint asked.

    Kate felt almost like she had failed in Hawkeye training, “It’s New York . There’s more taxis than humans, until it's raining and you need one.”

    “There’s more than usual,” Barney sounded bored but he was glaring at the storm drain as though it was the drain’s fault for him only getting four points.

    “It’s literally New Years Eve, everyone wants to be here to freeze their asses off to watch a ball drop.”

    “Have you noticed more cabs than usual?” Laura asked her, curiously, “ones not taking people because they’re full, or the lights not on?”

    Kate thought for a second, “I guess, I just figured they were busy.” She glanced at Barney, Clint, then back at Laura, “That’s how they stay undercover? Watching us?”

    Laura smiled and nodded, “We had some surveillance from the air, but it's been lessening, Coulson’s team was keeping an ear out for anyone communicating with,” she paused and tried not to sneer, she failed, “ her .”

    Clint walked over, he placed his hand on Laura’s back, “There’s been nothing, they’re still keeping an ear out but it seems that everything's okay. Which means that we should be able to head home tomorrow.”

    Kate grinned, “Seriously?”

    “Yeah, I mean we’ll still keep working together to figure out what happened,” he sent her a meaningful look, almost an olive branch, even though she didn’t need one.

    “I’ll be ready to go whenever you three make your plan,” Barney told them, looking at Kate. Kate wondered if this was what it was like to be an Avenger, or part of Shield, getting to be part of a team, doing something meaningful . She sent her uncle a grateful smile and a small nod. “That sounds good. It’ll be nice seeing the kids again,” She had been talking to them all regularly, particularly Cooper and Lila. It had been nice, but she was missing seeing them, and had promised Nate that she would watch about a hundred different movies with him.

    Clint smiled, then grabbed one of the silver disks from the table. He began weaving it through his fingers without even noticing it, “Did I ever tell you that I almost quit Shield and became a taxi driver?”

    “You did not!” Laura’s voice sounded amused despite the groan she protested his statement with.

    Kate looked from her mom back to her dad, “Why would you leave Shield to drive a taxi?”

    “Because Shield pay used to be shit, especially before the magical floating cities and flying billionaire shit.” He told her with absolute certainty. “We were undercover, your mom, Nat, and me in taxis around Manhattan, this was back when Nat was still being taught not to kill everyone she didn’t like.”

    Kate saw the look Laura sent Clint.

    Clint rolled his eyes at the look, “Yeah, yeah I know, I’m one to talk ,” he turned his attention back to Kate. “ Anyway , we were stuck surveilling some jackass for something or other. I don't know, I didn’t really listen to the briefing, but we were in cabs looking out.”

    “Your father was being punished for a prank,” Laura told Kate, “so he was forced to stay low instead of being somewhere up high.”

    “Coulson overreacted to a food fight,” Clint grumbled. “But I was driving, your mom or Nat were passengers often, when we needed to talk, but the rest of the time I had to blend in, so I drove real passengers, I got people places fast, and most people tipped for that. I made so much money from that, I seriously debated just sticking with it.”

    “You wouldn’t,” Kate told him certainly, “you’d be bored to death.”

    Clint smiled ever so slightly and nodded his head, he was understood, fully, by his oldest daughter.

    He took his turn with the disk, Kate watched in awe as the disk bounced off of five cars in a row, then the side of a bus, and the disk changed direction, then several more taxis before the coin hit the sidewalk, and rolled to the feet of a teenage boy, maybe a few years younger than Kate, the boy picked it up, and seemed happier since finding his little treasure, there was a pep in his step.

    “Shit.” Barney threw himself into a chair, dramatically, but the man was smiling, clearly impressed by his little brother.

    “Clint, that was awesome!” Kate’s mouth stayed open in awe even when she looked away from the street. “You gotta teach me how to do that!”

    Clint smiled, wrapping his arm around Laura who came to his side, she kissed his cheek leaving a little mark on his skin.

    “It’s just a lotta practice, we can set up a practice run for you when we’re home, if you like?” “Yeah,” she grinned but walked to the table and picked up a disk, “I mean do you know how many free drinks I could win with that party trip?”

    Kate took her turn, hitting four taxis, one more than Barney, putting her only two points behind him in the overall score.

    She would be lying if she claimed that she didn’t want to win, or that she hadn’t imagined doing so. But she reminded herself that she had come only a couple of points behind a fully trained CIA agent, and not too many more behind The Hawkeye, and her mom who was clearly one of the best Shield Agents, Kate was beginning to realise.

    “I accept Starbucks gift cards as my reward for being The Winner.”

    “You cheated,” Clint tried to goad, grinning the same way Kate did when she was causing mischief.

    Laura pulled a tongue at him.

    Kate couldn’t help but smile at them; her parents.

    They were talking as they ate lunch about everything and anything, mostly stories, some of Clint’s childhood, some of Laura’s, they talked about some missions which they said they weren’t telling her because she’s not meant to know but they weren’t going to stop her from listening to. There were some stories about her as a baby, the time she played hide and seek without Clint knowing, and he freaked out thinking he had lost her.

    Barney asked questions about Kate’s life, Laura and Clint listened closely as she answered, Kate was happy to tell them, she didn’t think it was that interesting, but they seemed interested so she spoke at length about things she didn’t think were that interesting.

    Clint talked about what he was planning on doing to the house, insisting that it needed fixing up, ignoring Laura informing Kate that Clint was never done with renovating the house, but she stared at him lovingly as she said it.

    Kate’s phone buzzed.

    Kate was used to Cooper and Lila phoning her now, they probably rang her more often than they rang their parents, often to just talk about anything and everything, mostly about how annoying the other one was.

    She reached for the phone, but paused when she realised it was neither Lila nor Cooper.

    “What’s wrong?” Clint became alert, his back straightened, and he looked around the room for a threat before glancing at her phone.

    But Kate picked it up before he could read the name, she knew she was blushing, and smiling, and there was no way she was picking the call up surrounded by her parents and uncle. “I’m just gonna…” She shot to her feet, and began to retreat to the office she’d been sleeping in.

    She felt everyone’s eyes on her, but she didn’t care, she just pressed answer. She was glad that no one could see the goofy smile on her face as she said, “Hey Lena.”

    “Happy New Year, Kate Bishop Barton!”

    Kate let out a small laugh at the name Yelena had settled on, and it was weird to think that Yelena was talking to her from a different year entirely, “Happy New Year's Eve, Yelena Belova.”

    There was silence but Kate could swear that Yelena was smiling against her phone, the same as Kate was doing.

    Kate grabbed a remote from beside the couch, she clicked it and music played from Barney’s music player. She needed music, she needed this to be private, without any possibility of others hearing them.

    Counting Crows began to sing about a parking lot.

    “Oh I love this song!” Yelena basically shouted down the phone, “Big Yellow Taxi!”

    Kate flopped onto the couch, grinning, “Yeah, Counting Crows are cool, I mean they’re on the Shrek soundtrack so of course they’re awesome.”

    “Shrek does have good music. Like Twilight, they have the best songs playing.”

    “Lena…,” Kate sang out her name, “... are you telling me that you had a Twilight phase?”

    “I was rebelling!”

    “Oh my god I can’t believe you had a… actually I think that checks out, I mean you did sneak into my apartment without me knowing, like the stalking vampire does.”

    “You are not to tell anyone about that.”

    “Hmmm… maybe I will, maybe I won’t.”

    “You are going to blackmail me, aren’t you?”

    “It’s not blackmailing, it's…”

    “Extortion “ “No, it’s just… me getting you to make me Mac ‘n’ cheese.”

    “You are incorrigible.”

    “That’s why you love me.”

    Kate could have kicked herself, why the fuck would she say that? It was bad enough the stray thoughts of Yelena, but the words coming out of her mouth. She was going to jump into the Hudson and stay there for at least several months.

    “You do keep me on my toes. I think that is a good thing.”

    Thank fuck, Kate thought, perhaps she hadn’t heard her words, not that Kate meant anything by it; Yelena was an amazing assassin (former assassin???), and completely unachievable.

    “How is your uncle's apartment?”

    Kate let out a breath, and smiled.

    She told Yelena all about the game they’d been playing the past few days, and about Barney, even if she was sure that Yelena would have probably hacked the CIA or something, she even told her all about some of the stories she’d been told about her parents as kids, missions they’d been on with Natasha, and the stories she’d been told about herself.

    “It must be weird,” Yelena said when Kate told her the last one she knew, “to have these stories about you that you are only just learning.”

    “I guess, I mean I didn’t hear any stories about me as a kid from Derek and Eleanor even from when I was living with them… but it’s kinda nice? Like, I like seeing their faces when they talk about this kinda stuff, and I like knowing it, it’s like having a past, a real past which wasn’t a lie.”

    “I understand, and I am glad that you are getting a past, and a future with the Bartons.” Kate could tell from Yelena’s voice that she really did understand, “How’s it been being back with your family?”

    “It is… it is good, but complicated. They didn’t approve of me going after your father.”

    Kate stifled a laugh as she could hear the eye roll in her tone.

    “Do not laugh!”

    Kate gave in and laughed.

    “I had to suffer many, many lectures from my parents, Katherine Bishop Barton!”

    Kate laughed even more, soon she could hear Yelena’s laugh on the other side of the phone, she pressed the phone closer to her cheek, she wished Yelena was there with her; she wanted to see the light in Yelena’s eyes again.

    She wanted to see Yelena again.

    “Hey, Lena?” Kate said when the pair finished laughing, “I’m glad that you rang, I’ve missed you. I mean, like because-”

    “I have missed you too, Kate Bishop Barton.”

    Kate allowed herself a small smile, a private one, as the Counting Crows song played on a loop around her.

    “I can talk for longer, if you are not busy?”

    “I have loads of time. Tell me about Russia?”

    “Well… it is very old, there is a population of-”

    “You’re a fucking asshole,” Kate laughed, “You know what I mean.”

    “I know what you said.” Yelena countered, but obliged, “I can tell you about the Christmas Markets I visited with some of my widows?”

    Yelena had a way with words, clearly, as she painted the scene perfectly for Kate, she pulled her knees up to her chest and listened closely to Yelena’s words, imagining that she was there with Yelena.

    An hour later Kate turned off the music and finally emerged from the office, everyone was gathered around the fridge in the kitchen.

    Laura sent her yet another knowing smile, as she did more other than Kate cared to admit, “How’s Yelena?”

    Kate smiled at her name, before dampening the smile as much as she could, “She’s good, she’s been talking to a lot of the former Widows she helped free from the fog, and she’s been spending a lot of time with her parents, they held a service for Natasha.”

    Both Clint and Laura smiled sadly. “I’m glad they’re getting some closure,” Laura told her, “and that Yelena’s doing okay.”

    Kate sent her a quick smile but looked away when her mom looked at her as though she was trying to figure something out. “What’s for dinner?”

    “Takeout,” Barney told her, “Chinese. And apparently you and I have to get it as we’re the ‘losers’’,” he did air quotes around the word, and rolled his eyes at Laura and Clint.

    Kate grinned, frankly she was happy to be able to get out of the apartment for the first time in days, not counting the rooftop garden. “Can I get-”

    “I already ordered your favourite,” Clint told her.

    “I thought you weren’t listening to me when we were at that restaurant?” She glared at him when he just smiled, “You faked not being able to hear me!”

    He shrugged, still smiling, “I’m sure you’ll get over it.”

    “Nuh uh, I’m going to hold this over you for years!”

    “Good luck with that,” Clint laughed, but followed her to the office as she went to find some shoes and a coat.

    “Hey, kid? Before you go I wanted to show you this,” he reached into his pocket and pulled out a metal square, before unfolding it to make a StarkPad.

    “That’s cool.”

    He paused, “Yeah, but I meant what's on the tablet, not the tablet itself. He held it out to her. It took her a second to realise that it was a re-enrollment form for her college, to continue technically, but that was scribbled in the notes as it was probably rare to be allowed to re- enrol after breaking a belltower. It had all her information: her previous grades, her double majors, her minors, the extracurriculars she had been involved in, everything looked familiar, but this one had her real date of birth on it, the emergency contacts were Clint, Laura, and somewhat surprisingly Barney and her name was simply ‘Katherine’ in Clint’s handwriting.

    “I had to explain it to the school, as much as they needed to know anway,” Clint told her, “I had to give them your birth certificate, and I filled out anything I knew, and put me and your mom down there, and Barney because he’s closer if there’s an emergency. I should have guessed sooner that one of your majors would be communications.”

    “I thought you’d figure that earlier, I mean I was giving you great advice about your branding.”

    “I was too stunned by your art to think too hard about it,” he jabbed, “I can see if they can sign you up for art classes too, if you like, I mean if they have really beginners classes-” he was cut off by a laugh as she punched his shoulder.

    “You suck, I’m telling Barney that you went Larping and loved it.”

    “Nah you love me, and not if you want Christmas presents next year.”

    Kate rolled her eyes but didn’t protest.

    “I wasn’t sure what names you wanted to use, I thought I’d leave it to you, we have about two weeks to get it done, so there’s time to think.”

    Kate bit her lip and looked down at the tablet, “What names do you want me to use?”

    He gently tilted her chin up to look him in the eye, “Your mom and I want you to use anything that you want to, whatever you’re most comfortable with, we won’t be offended if you choose Katherine Bishop, or if you decided to use any combination of your two names.

    We want you to be happy and comfortable, so this choice is entirely up to you, Hawkeye.”

    Kate smiled at him, then gave in and wrapped him in a hug. “Thanks,” she paused, not letting another name for him slip out, because if she did it changed everything, and things had already changed so much, “Hawkeye.”

    “Anytime.”

    She smiled at him as they pulled out of the hug.

    “Katie, I’m sorry but… you’re gonna have to do online classes, just until it’s safe, not just for you but for other students, and-”

    “ Clint ,” she cut him off, “it’s okay, I get it, you’re right. I mean I’mma also need to see if there’s any dorms I can move into, if you guys don’t mind me hanging around for a bit longer?”

    Clint sent her a look to remind her of the many times he and Laura had told her that they liked her being around, “I also have a stipulation, though Laura said to say it was a request.” “Go on…”

    “You have to come home at least one weekend a month. I’ll taxi you back and forth from the airports or driving or whatever, but at least once a month, or if you’re not busy, or want to visit, whenever, just let me know and I’ll drive… your brothers and sister will miss you .” “Just them, or you and mom?”

    Clint smiled at her, letting out a breath of a laugh, “We might miss you a bit… I’m gonna miss you.”

    He pulled her into a hug and Kate wrapped her arms around him without hesitating. She was hit with a preemptive pang of homesickness, she wasn’t sure that she was ready to leave her new home, a feeling she’d never experienced at the Bishop mansion.

    They only pulled apart when they heard Barney telling them to move their ass’ from the other room.

    Kate stared at the tablet, alone in the office while she pulled her boots on. She wasn’t sure yet what name she’d write on it: she was Bishop for so long that she felt some form of obligation, or perhaps it was just familiarity, to write that, but it didn’t seem to fit her anymore, not really.

    It was like a sweater which she was always wearing, perhaps not her prettiest one, but her most comfortable one, that she’d eventually thrown into the wash, and it had shrunk, it now rode up when she moved, and the cuffs were no longer touching her wrists. But it was familiar, it was hard to throw away.

    “Go safe!” Laura called to them as they headed out the door.

    “Oh Katie!” Clint called, “Grab some extra fortune cookies for us all, and for your siblings, here!”

    She caught his wallet with ease, she pocketed it. “You still use cash? How old are you?” But she grinned, and waved to her parents, “See ya in a few.”

    The streets of New York were busy as she and Barney walked, but both were more than used to it, even on New Years eve.

    “Did Clint tell you that he put you down as one of my college emergency contacts?”

    “I volunteered,” He told her, sending her a small smile, “I’m normally in the city these days, I figured it made sense.”

    Kate smiled hearing the words that he wasn’t saying, that he wanted to be there if she was sick or hurt, that he didn’t want her to be alone. “So if another clock tower happens to fall near me… will you be able to make sure that my parents don’t hear about it?”

    Barney let out a snort of laughter, “I would, but Simone will one hundred percent make sure that either you tell them or I do, and somehow you doing it will be my fault.”

    Kate laughed, “That sounds like my mom and dad.” he smiled at her and she realised that it was probably the first time she’d said that word in regards to Clint out loud.

    It felt good.

    It felt right.

    “When’s Simone heading back?”

    “Tomorrow, she’s going to fly in tomorrow morning, her folks don’t care much about New Years so they won’t be doing anything tonight that she’ll need to rest for.”

    “Wait, will she be back before we go?”

    Barney grinned, “Yeah, your mom’s already asked that, but you guys will be able to meet her and the boys for a few hours before your flight.”

    “Awesome, hey Barney? When are you gonna ask her to marry you?”

    “Finally a taxi, c’mon” he oh so subtly dodged the question. He held his hand up and whistled, the taxi was a little way off from them but headed their way.

    But it didn’t slow down, not even slightly.

    “Kate-” he pushed her away from his side, so she was firmly on the sidewalk.

    The taxi hit him.

    He let out a strangled scream as he flew up into the air, he landed heavily on the road.

    The taxi didn’t hesitate: it reversed over him.

    Before Kate could take a step towards her uncle, or even scream, she felt something collide with the back of her head, there was a millisecond where her head stung…

    Then the world faded to black.

    Dance Chapter Summary Clint and Laura dance..... oh and then they wonder where kate and barney are Chapter Notes nothing filled me with more joy than the anger you all had (that sounds sarcastic but its not i genuinely love it)

    have some cute Clint and Laura and then also obviously the angst <3 See the end of the chapter for more notes Laura shoved some plates into the oven to take the chill off of them.

    She was looking forward to the meal with her family, but she was also looking forward to being back in their home, with all of her kids safe with her and her husband.

    She had tried not to be overprotective over Kate, she was an adult who had grown up fending for herself, Laura knew that, but it didn’t stop the maternal instinct to want to protect her, to hide any plans and fears away from her, especially after all she’d been through. But Laura had ignored them, purposely, and had sat with Kate planning how to get more information about who took Kate originally, and how to bring them down.

    She let out a sigh, she had been retired for years now, and Clint had retired about a half dozen times, they should be just getting to know Kate, becoming her parents again, not putting her in any danger.

    She felt a hand around her waist and it was only her training and decades of marriage which stopped her from flipping her husband onto his back.

    He had put on music, and the next thing she knew she was being twirled into his arms.

    “Four months undercover in a dance school and you’ve still got it,” he commented as they stared face to face, their hands slipping into one another’s as they began to slow dance standing in place.

    She smiled as they swayed together, “And ballet classes when I was three.”

    “Didn’t you quit those after two sessions?”

    “It was three and my mom loved telling everyone that story,” she rolled her eyes.

    “How you staged a protest in class because your teacher wouldn’t teach you the male part in a show your mom had taken you to?” he smiled with fond amusement, “You should tell Kate about that… actually she already thinks you’re cooler than me, don’t give her another reason.”

    “She only thinks that because it’s true, honey,” she teased him, but softened the blow with a kiss to his lips.

    He rolled his eyes but agreed with her. His eyes glanced towards the window as though he could see across the city, he quickly looked back at Laura, ready to play it off until he saw that she was looking at the door with the same face.

    He smiled ever so slightly and nudged her cheek with his nose before pressing a kiss to her jawline. “She’ll be okay.”

    “I know. “ she didn’t sound convinced so she tried again, “I know, I know that we’ve been careful, that we had Phil and Melinda, and everyone else surveilling everyone who’s even looked at her wrong, and making sure we’re safe, and that it was safe to be out again, and that she’s got Barney with her… but….”

    He pressed a firmer kiss to her cheek and quietly admitted, “It’s like the first time we left her with any babysitter, even when it was your mom first looking after her, like you can’t breathe until you see her again, and make sure that she’s okay, I know. It’s how I’ve felt since, hell, since she found me, it was easier to ignore then, before she became Nicky.”

    Laura let out a small breath, “Yeah. I know she’s an adult, that she can take care of herself, but she’s still our kid. It's easier with the others, we know them and they know us, we all know the lines and limits, and how to fix things with them, but with Kate it’s like we’re having to learn how she fights so we can work together. I don’t want to mess this up, babe.” Clint nodded, “You’re not going to mess this up, Laur, Kate adores you, and you’re amazing with her. And she’s like her momma; she’s clever and brave and kind and she can take down anyone she wants. And she’s safe.”

    The far away look finally left her eyes, she pressed her lips to his, and kissed him softly, “This is the first time we’ve been alone since before Nate, now we have a house full of kids, a dog, and a Russian assassin… Y’know what I wanna do?”

    Clint smirked, “You read my mind…”

    They collapsed onto the couch, Laura leaning back against Clint, his arms around her, in one hand he held the remote to the television; finally able to watch some tv in peace.

    The tv they chose was a trashy hallmark Christmas movie, one which was almost identical to every single other one that was made, it was both of their guilty pleasure, though neither had the time to watch any this year.

    The buzzing of Clint’s phone woke Laura, she elbowed him, he stirred with a frown. He reached for the coffee table for his cell, he smiled ever so slightly as a photo from Lila came through, of her and her brothers with a New Year's filter, he passed his phone to Laura to look at.

    He yawned and got to his feet to stretch, he frowned as he caught sight of the clock. “Have you heard Barn or Katie?”

    “Huh?” Laura was still smiling as she looked away from the photo to her husband, she processed his words and a small frown formed. She looked around where she sat, “No,we were asleep for longer than I…” she glanced at the screen, they had been going to watch a movie, but they had clearly slept through it all judging by the credits crawling up the screen. “No… honey…?”

    Clint frowned as Laura got to her feet, without words the two split up, he headed towards the bathroom and office, while Laura tackled the bedrooms.

    The bathroom door was slightly open, as it always was, yet he still checked it out, there was nothing there other than neatly placed toiletries and some stray toys which Clint had never thought he’d see in his brother’s home. But no Barney, or Kate.

    He turned on his feet, and strode to the office.

    Kate's things were all around the office, her bag half unpacked, one of the hoodies he had given her was on the arm of the chair. The pillow and blankets she’d been using were on the couch where she’d been sleeping. But her boots she’d gone out in weren’t there, nor the hoodie she’d gone out in.

    He felt cold.

    He wasn’t sure his heart was beating, but he knew that he needed to be logical, so he rushed from the office back to the living area, Laura was standing there looking how he felt.

    “They’re not…?” He trailed off but she shook her head, answering his unsaid question.

    He sucked in a breath.

    It didn’t help.

    He couldn’t be Clint right then, he had to be Hawkeye, not the archer but the trained and controlled Shield agent. He locked eyes with his wife, he could see that she was doing the same. “I’m gonna go search for them.”

    “I’ll search the roof, but then I’ll be back here,” she grabbed his cellphone from the living room couch, she threw it to him, he caught it without even looking, as he grabbed his boots. “I’ll call you if they come back here.”

    He nodded his head, sucking in a breath, he laced up his boots tightly, “They’ll be… they’ll be on their way back,” he knew it was a lie, and that neither he nor Laura believed it.

    “Yeah… it’s New Years Eve, the chinese restaurant is probably busy…”

    “Yeah… there was probably a screw up,” he agreed. He pulled on his coat and zipped it up. “I’ll bring her home,” he promised, not for the first time.

    Clint chose the way through the park to the restaurant, he was pretty sure that between this way then a cab or the subway that his older brother would have picked the quieter route.

    He kept his eyes open as he walked fast, looking out for Kate and Barney. He was waiting for them to walk up to him, for his daughter to roll her eyes at him coming to find them, then she’d talk a mile a minute while they walked back to the apartment.

    But there was also the part of him who knew that one of them would have phoned, or at least texted him. So he also scanned the ground and the bushes, for bodies.

    There was no sign of them in the park, he tried to think of that as a good sign, but his anxiety only grew.

    As soon as he left the park he was met by too many cop cars, and people gathering around. He had heard the sirens, of course, but he put it down to it being New York. Everything in his training told him to carry on walking, that this was New York, there was always shit going on, but it was unlikely it had anything to do with Barney and Kate… but his instincts… the ones which told him not to fire at Thor when he first saw him, not to shoot at Nat, to take a chance on Wanda and Pietro….

    He craned his head.

    Behind the tape was the road, a taxi had been crashed into the fire hydrant, the driver motionless in the front, no signs of trauma or injuries from the crash, but they were clearly dead.

    A little further on was blood drenching into the tarmac of the road.

    And the outline of a body.

    “‘Scuse me?” He called to the nearest cop, “what’s goin’ on?”

    “I’m sorry sir, I can’t- Wait, holy shit you're the bird one, aren’t you? Lemme get a selfie man, my niece loved the avengers.”

    “Clint!”

    Clint’s head shot towards where his name was called from, Matt, dressed in a suit, with his glasses and cane. Clint took the out and rushed over to the lawyer. “What’s going-?”

    “Do you have a brother?”

    Clint’s blood ran cold, “Why?”

    “I just heard them, I was going to come find you, they found the guy’s name on his drivers licence, Barn-”

    “Fuck,” Clint breathed out, “fuck, Matt what happened? What the fuck- where’s my brother? Where’s Kate?”

    “Wait, Kate was here?”

    “They were together, they were getting takeout, Matt where are they?!”

    “Your brother was run over by the guy in the taxi, Clint… it didn’t sound good, I got here a while ago, they were rushing him off in an ambulance… I don’t think it’s looking good.”

    Clint couldn’t breathe, he wanted to run to the hospital right away, he wanted to threaten doctors and nurses and any fucking person who he could to save his brother. “Kate?”

    Clint could have sworn Matt paled.

    “Clint, it was just him, there was no sign of her.”

    Clint couldn’t breathe.

    Kate’s head burnt with pain as she came too.

    Her throat was dry, her ears had popped, and she could barely see, wherever she was it was dark, with only tiny blinking lights.

    She could hear something, fireworks... that was right, it was New Years Eve, well, New Year now clearly.

    She couldn't remember....

    She was struck by an overwhelming urge to move, to get home, to get to her parents.

    Barney.

    She sucked in a breath as she remembered what had happened, he had been…

    There was a sharp pinch at the side of her neck, just out of view, she felt the cold liquid being injected inside of her, before she faded out of consciousness.
