    See the end of the chapter for more notes Clint couldn’t sit in the waiting room, he was the opposite to his wife in this way, he needed constant motion, to pace, to tap his foot, or drum his fingers, he needed an outlet for his fear and frustration: the opposite than when he was taking aim.

    He was usually fine with waiting, with biding his time, with sitting still in a nest, calm and cool, ready to make the hard choices at a second's notice.

    But not like this.

    Barney was in surgery, that was all they had been told, but they were waiting in the special ICU waiting room and the nurses kept glancing nervously at them. It was like a slow march to death, but in this case death would be the news that his brother was dead.

    They were also waiting for Coulson to get there, the man had been on a mission across the country but was apparently racing back, Shield were wisely leaving Coulson to deal with them.

    He passed by Laura again, but this time she caught his hand, and tugged him to a stop.

    “He’s going to be okay.”

    Clint shook his head ever so slightly. “They don’t seem to think so.”

    For her due, Laura didn’t try and argue, they were both well trained in reading body language, they knew the news they were expecting.

    “Where’s Coulson?” He asked.

    “When’s Coulson meant to call about the mission tomorrow?” Clint asked, not looking at Laura. Instead he smiled at his daughter, Nicky was playing with a soft toy bow and arrow he had found for her. He couldn’t believe how old she was getting already, it felt like “You think there’s still time for me to tell him I’m sick? Me and Nick are doing really important work here, aren’t we Hawkeye Junior?” he dug his fingers into Nicole’s side, making her squeal with laughter.

    “As he’s stood right behind you, that probably won’t work.”

    “Shit,” Clint mumbled.

    “‘Hit!”

    Clint grimaced as soon as the word had left Nicky’s mouth, “Sure you copy that word but not the ones I actually try to teach you,” he turned around to face Coulson, looking up at him from the ground.

    “I would tell Fury that you’re not well but we’ve had a heads up about that Russian assassin, we’re gonna need you. Today .”

    At least Coulson had the decency to look apologetic, in fact the Shield Handler was smiling down at the toddler who was now using the toy bow as a hammer.

    Clint gently stopped her, “No hitting things, buddy… unless it’s bad guys,” he pulled her into his arms and stood up so that he was no longer lounging on the floor of his daughter’s room. “You wanna say hi to uncle Phil, sweetheart?”

    “Hi!”

    Clint grimaced as she shouted practically down his ear, “I’m gonna be deaf by the time you turn two.” He pressed a kiss to her cheek, and passed her to Phil, who was more than happy to cuddle her. “Is Laur-?”

    “She’s gonna be on this too,” Coulson told his friend, only grimacing slightly as a wet kiss was pressed to his cheek, he sent his goddaughter a small smile, “Thank you very much, Nicole. She’s ringing her mom to come look after this one.”

    Clint nodded slowly, “How long we got?”

    “Thirteen minutes.”

    Clint pulled a face, “Shi-shoot,” he quickly corrected, “her mom’s not in DC... Give me a min.”

    He rushed to the bedroom where Laura had changed into her uniform, “Coulson said-”

    “I know,” she cut him off not unkindly, “I think we’re going to have to drop her off at the creche, I know we said we didn’t want her to go there but we haven’t really got any options.” Clint frowned, “I know… let me just,” he searched his head for a way to fix this, or at the very least control it, “let me ring ‘round, Osborne’s signed off, right? And Nicky loves him, just ‘til your mom gets here.”

    Laura smiled at him, clearly relieved, “That sounds like a good idea, if he’ll do it, tell him we’ll owe him one. You give him a call and say bye to Nicole, I’ll make sure you’re all packed up.”

    Clint pecked her on the cheek, he grabbed the portable landline and made a beeline back to Nicky’s room.

    He scooped “Coffee?” Coulson offered, close enough to the Bartons to feel at home making his way around their home.

    “Please,” he pressed a kiss to Nicky’s forehead as he dialled in Darren’s number from memory, “You wanna stay with uncle Darren, buddy? Much safer there than Shield, with all its recent security breaches, hey Nicky, can you say 'security breach'?”

    “You go?”

    “Only for a little while buddy,” he promised even though she wouldn’t understand that, only that he was going to leave her, “Maybe grandma will take you to see the animals in the zoo.” “Doggie,” she insisted.

    “Sure.” he pressed a kiss to her forehead, then pressed the call button.

    It took a second for Clint to realise that Laura had answered him, stuck in the memory playing in his head, “Huh?”

    “I said May told me he’s on his way, he’s not far away. He'll be here any minute.”

    Clint slowly nodded while he let out a smokers breath. He felt the hair on the back of his neck stand, he swiftly turned, reaching for the daggers he had hidden in his sleeves.

    But it was just a doctor.

    He relaxed, but just barely, just enough to not attack.

    “You’re the family of Charles Barton?”

    Clint tensed, whenever he heard Barney’s real name he knew it was bad news, it meant that Barney wasn’t around to tell them to shut the fuck up. He nodded, “Brother,” his voice came out hoarse, “he’s my brother.”

    The doctor nodded, looking hurried, “As you’re probably aware your brother is in surgery now trying to stop any internal bleeding and damage.”

    Laura stood up and took hold of Clint’s hand. “ Is there any damage?”

    “We can’t be sure of the extent of it until he gains consciousness, if he gains consciousness. To be frank, Mr Barton, it’s nothing short of a miracle that he’s alive now.”

    “But… he could make it, right?”

    The doctor sent Clint a regretful smile at his small and hopeful voice. “I think it would be best that you prepare for the worst, it’s very unlikely that he’ll wake up again, and if he does there will be damage.”

    “What kind of damage?”

    “His spine suffered a lot of damage when he was run over, we’re certain that he will at the very least be paralysed in his lower body, possibly even his arms, but that’s not as likely. We’re currently working on his spleen, kidneys, liver, as well as anything else which may arise.”

    “But, he could wake up, right?” Laura asked, “It’s still a possibility.”

    The doctor let out a gentle sigh, “I really do urge you to prepare for the worst possible outcome.”

    Clint clenched his jaw, “I understand.”

    “C’mon hit me. Pull your arm back, keep your thumb outside of your fist, put everything you got into it, don’t think about how much it’s gonna hurt, then throw it, hit with your fingers not your knuckles, keep your wrist solid or you’re gonna break it.”

    Clint pushed Barney away from him, “I’m not gonna hit you Barney.” He looked down away from him, turning his head ever so slightly so that his deaf ear was towards him, he didn’t want to hear him.

    Barney placed a hand on either side of Clint’s face and roughly turned it so he was facing him, able to hear him in one ear “I can take it Clint, you need to know this, turn everything into a weapon, become a weapon and hit with it. Hit ‘em ‘til they stop.”

    Clint swallowed, he could hear his dad’s truck pull up, but they were far enough away, in an abandoned barn, that they wouldn’t have to hide from him just yet.

    “C’mon, bud,” Barney insisted, he pressed his forehead to Clint’s forehead, then moved away from him, spreading his arms out to his sides, “punch me.”

    Not a minute after the doctor left them Coulson appeared.

    Clint sucked in a breath.

    Coulson spoke first, “I’m so sorry.”

    Clint clenched his hand into a fist, “Have you found her?”

    Coulson hesitated, “Not yet. The people who took her are very well funded, they’ve got resources and technology.”

    “They used a fucking taxi to get Barney, how much technology are we talking?” Laura demanded.

    “Daisy got as much footage as she possibly could, she’s got the best angle ready, she’s also searching for more, but if you want you can watch what happened.” His eyes flicked to the doors which led to the surgery room, “It’s not pleasant.”

    Clint felt Laura tense beside him, he gently squeezed her hand, “I’ll watch it, I need to.” “I will too.”

    Clint looked at his wife, “Honey you-”

    “ We need to .” She told him firmly.

    Clint nodded.

    Coulson held out a tablet to Laura, “I’m going to make sure that there’s security around for your brother, have you contacted Dr Cho?”

    “I text her earlier,” Clint nodded his eyes on the tablet, the only thing on the screen was a play button, “She’s on her way from Hong Kong, she’s not sure if she’ll be able to get here in time…”

    Coulson nodded slowly, biting his lip, “I’ll talk to her, and I’ll talk to Simmons, see if she can help here in the meantime.”

    “I saw the CIA around before, I think they’re trying to help guard him or something,” Laura told him.

    Coulson nodded his head slowly, for once not taking a shot at how inept he found working with them typically, he nodded his head at the tablet and then walked away.

    Clint braced himself, then looked at Laura, “You ready?”

    “Nope,” Laura sent him a doubtful look, “Are you?”

    “Not at all.” He pressed play.

    Clint sucked in a breath as Barney and Kate appeared on the sidewalk outside of the park. The two were walking side by side, they couldn’t see it but Clint could tell by the way Kate was walking that she was laughing, or at the very least smiling, so was Barney. Barney was walking on the outside of Kate, Clint remembered how she had assumed he was being polite when he had walked on that side of her, but he knew that Barney was doing it out of his own training.

    Barney kept putting his hand out for taxis, but most of them going past had people in them, or simply weren’t stopping.

    Until one started heading to them, if Clint didn’t know what was going to happen then he would have assumed that they were simply going to get in the taxi, but the taxi didn’t slow down.

    Barney pushed Kate far away from the road. He saved her.

    The car hit Barney, he flew into the air, further onto the eerily empty road. Clint sucked in a breath.

    The taxi stopped on the road… then reversed.

    Laura let out a ragged breath, “I’m gonna be-”

    She stopped as the two watched someone young with a baseball bat hit Kate over the head.

    A van skidded down the road, it stopped at them and people jumped out, then with Baseball Bat Guy they bundled her into the back of the van. One of them pushed BBG yelling something at him, but they both got into the van with the rest of them. The guy in the taxi waved his hand at them out the window. Then the taxi slowly rolled into the fire hydrant, Clint knew from Matt that they were already dead.

    The van sped off, only to turn invisible just before it left the screen.

    “The fuck ?” Laura all out demanded.

    “That did just turn invisible, right?”

    “Since when do shitty looking old vans have cloaking devices?”

    “Since it’s a good van made to look shitty,” Clint guessed, his eyes were still on the screen, where Barney led in the road.

    Someone approached him and Clint bristled, but the person on the screen bent down and tried to help him.

    A few more people also approached to try to help, but none of them would be able to.

    “You gotta stop getting into fights.”

    Clint just rolled his eyes.

    “I’m serious, dumbass .”

    Clint rolled his eyes again, “They were being an asshole to some girls who didn’t want them to try shit on them, what was I supposed to do?”

    “Not get the shit kicked outta you by six guys twice your size? And you’re doing that wrong.” “It’s over my cut, Barn,” He protested, but he did stop trying to put the steristrip across the cut.

    “Yeah, it’s meant to hold it shut, c’mere.”

    Clint sighed but didn’t push Barney away.

    Barney pinched Clint’s cut cheek, Clint let out a hiss of pain, glaring at Barney who smirked. “You’re gonna get chucked outta here if you don’t stop getting in fights, or you’re gonna be thrown in juvie.”

    “I’m not just gonna let assholes get away with shit like that, Barn, you taught me that.” Barney gave him a look, “Yeah, when dad beat the hearing outta you. Now you’ve gotten thrown outta like five homes.”

    “Six, actually,” Clint told him, completely uncaring. “And so I’m only supposed to throw punches to help myself?” He scoffed. “Fuck off man.” He tried to get up but Barney pushed him back to sit on the bed.

    “I’m not saying that, I’m saying stop throwing hands first and thinking second, you’ve got four more years until you’re eighteen then you can get outta here. You’re not dumb you just look it, you could go to college, or join the army, or the air force, you could do whatever you want. Just keep your head down.”

    “And ignore assholes?”

    Barney hesitated.

    Clint scoffed again, this time glaring at Barney, he stood up, shoving his brother away from him, “I’m not gonna turn into a dick like you.”

    “I just want you to live past eighteen you asshole! If you don’t keep control of your temper you’re gonna wake up one day and realise you turned into dad!”

    Clint just walked away from him. His hand clenched into a fist at his side.

    They watched it twice more before Coulson walked over to them.

    Clint kept his eyes on the tablet, his hand in a fist at his side, “Where’s the van. Where’s Katie.” He demanded.

    Coulson hesitated and Clint looked up, he could see Laura glaring at their friend, which was probably why he was hesitating.

    “We’re not sure, not yet, we’re running down some leads-”

    “ What leads?” Laura demanded.

    “There’s a few options about where she was taken to, Canada for one-”

    “And for other?”

    “We’re not sure how big their group is, but if they’re as big as we think they are then one team could have taken Kate somewhere else while the other team…”

    Clint forced himself to breathe as Coulson trailed off, “What aren’t you saying Coulson?” Coulson sucked in a breath, “Part of the team broke Eleanor Bishop out of jail, we’re not sure when, the jail didn’t notice it, and we’re not sure where she is eit-”

    His breath was knocked out of him as Clint shoved him hard against a wall, holding him by his suit lapels.

    “Barton-!”

    “You promised!” Clint growled, “You promised she would be safe, that we were all safe. You stopped watching, even when I asked you to continue, I believed you because you promised me . Now my brother’s gonna die!” Tears stung his eyes and he had to try hold in sobs, “ And my kid is- is-”

    Clint didn’t realise that the phone was ringing right away; he had been busy trying not to laugh about something one of the newbies had said, while Phil was clearly laughing behind his clipboard. Apparently no one else noticed the phone because it was Fury who got up and answered it.

    Clint glanced over, sure that they were all about to be yelled at. But Fury went pale.

    Clint glanced at Laura, she’d noticed it too, so had Maria, and Phil, when Clint looked back at Fury he could see the director staring right at him, pure fear on his face.

    Fury only said a few words before Laura and Clint were racing out of the room, not even pausing to catch the keys Coulson threw to Clint.

    They flew as fast as they could, parking the plane in the park they took Nicole to so she could feed the ducks every Sunday.

    Laura’s mom was still there, blood on her hands, as paramedics raced to help Darren, whose blood stained their dining room chair, his hands were still tied together.

    “She’s gone!” Laura’s mom, Liza, wailed.

    It was then that their worlds crumbled.

    And the solid foundation was replaced with sand.

    “Clint!” Laura shouted, placing her hand on his shoulder and tugging him back.

    Clint dropped his hold on Coulson.

    “Cut this shit,” she told her husband firmly, but took hold of his hand again, she squeezed it gently, as she glared at Coulson. “If anything happens to my little girl, after you assured us that taking the watch off was the right thing to do… we all know that it’s not Clint who’s going to need to be held back.”

    To his credit Coulson nodded. “I can’t begin to say how sorry I am, I would never let any harm come to her,” he looked imploringly at his friends, reminding them that they had chosen him to be Nicole’s god-father alongside Fury, and that it was Coulson who worked himself to the bone to try and find her, for years. “And we are going to find her this time. She’s strong, and brave, she won’t let anything happen, and we’re going to have her back soon, I promise. But you both need to work with me, like the old days; no going off on your own, it’s the only way .”

    Clint tried to swallow, but there was a lump in his throat, so he squeezed Laura’s hand, and wiped a stray tear from his own cheek. He straightened, and when he spoke he wasn’t Clint, but Agent Barton, “What do we know?”

    Chapter Summary Clint and Laura meet with Coulson's team to try and save Kate Kate meets with a baseball fan Chapter Notes Hows everyones week going? I'm 25 and finally bought a tamagotchi and its the best decision ive made since i was like 5 but forreal i love you all and please stay safe whereever you are, but especially if you're in Ukraine, or if you have friends or family there, I cannot image what you're going through, I hope this can give you even a moment of peace See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate’s mouth was dry when she woke up, not just dry, but like it had been filled with sand for years. She tried to swallow but all she could do was cough.

    Someone pressed a cup against her lips, she tried to move her head back and was met with a low laugh.

    “If I wanted you to be knocked out,” a man’s voice said, “I would just inject you again.” She couldn’t argue with that, and she had realised that she was still tied up, so she swallowed, hungrily.

    He only moved the cup away when Kate had finished.

    Kate looked around the room, the same way Clint had when they went somewhere new, he had taught her to take in all the details of the place, so she did: it was dark, barely enough light to see, the windows were boarded, but there was a little grey light coming in, the daylight of a grey winter day. The walls were grey, paint peeling up the high walls, the floorboards were wooden, stripped as though they were in a house in the process of being done up.

    The room was empty except the high backed chair she was in: A dentist's chair.

    She had a bad feeling about it.

    The man had noticed her looking around, he seemed pleased about it, his smile was smug, and hungry. Her skin crawled.

    “I think you need a new interior designer,” she croaked, “creepy murder houses aren’t in fashion anymore.”

    “What can I say? I’m a trailblazer.”

    Kate stared at him as he took a step back into the light; he had dark hair long, shaggy, and fluffy, designer stubble, and eyes which were a blue so light they were practically grey. He was dressed in dark clothes but ones which wouldn’t be out of place at a dinner party. He was younger than Kate expected, maybe a little older than herself.

    He was still smiling his wolf’s smile.

    “I think you’re probably just a dick,” she made herself sound bored, and subtly tugged her wrist but the metal cuffs held tight.

    He pulled over a stool from the corner of the room and set it in front of her, still smiling as he sat on it, staring at her expectantly.

    “If this is some shitty blind date then I’m out,” she drawled, “People kidnapping me are only sometimes my type.”

    “I don’t want to date you.”

    “That really doesn’t offend me.” She glared. She moved her head and winced.

    “Are you in pain?”

    “Why have you taken me?” She asked, ignoring his question entirely.

    “Because I was ordered to,” he said it so casually, like he was telling her it was raining. She frowned at how little information she was being given, “Is this something to do with my parents? My mom and my dad?” She tried not to allow herself to feel the pain of missing Clint and Laura, but that didn’t work.

    He tilted his head to the side, like a confused puppy, “Your biological parents?”

    “They’re my parents,” she told him hotly, “period. Or is it to do with Eleanor and Derek?” “You stopped seeing them as your parents that quickly? You found some new ones and no longer care about your family who raised you?”

    Kate frowned, he sounded confused and hurt, and Kate couldn’t figure out why. “They’re the ones who made me not their family,” she glared but at the Bishops not him.

    “We knew each other once, you and I.”

    “What-“ but she was cut off when there was a sound of a key turning in a locked door, and the door opening.

    The man in front of her lost his soft confusion, and straightened up, his eyes hardened in a familiar way.

    Kate braced herself.

    Coulson had somehow acquired them a meeting room in the ICU wing, not long after that May turned up, and the rest of Coulson’s team, May had hugged Clint and Laura tightly, and the rest had awkwardly assured them that they would both find Kate and that Barney would be okay, but it wasn’t convincing.

    Clint bounced his leg up and down as he took a tablet from Daisy, “This is a different angle?” “There’s a few different ones,” She told him, unpacking a laptop from a bag, “just swipe for more angels, there’s some which you can barely see them on, but I didn’t know if you’d be able to see anything.”

    Clint nodded once at her, “Any possibility of you being able to hack into any private cameras?”

    Daisy sent him an almost excitedly look, before she remembered what the footage was of, “I’m about to do that now, I’ve got to go through all the networks separately, there’s no one system which consolidates them, and there’s a lot of them nowadays so it’s going to take a while.”

    Clint nodded approvingly, “See if you can track any orders online, or physical purchases using credit cards or whatever, if the footage isn’t linked to a system we might have to send people to go and get it in person.”

    “Good idea,” she nodded, she slipped on a pair of headphones, and her fingers began to fly across the keyboard.

    Clint stood up and walked over to his wife’s side, barely listening as she, May, and Fitz talked tech options for searching. He pressed play on the first video, trying to forget that it was Barney and Kate on the screen. He grabbed a notebook from the side table and a pen and began to scribble observations.

    “You know you can just take notes on the tablet, right?”

    Clint tried not to be offended at the way the young scientist said that, clearly doubting that Clint knew, “My ideas come out better when they’re flowing and I’m not staring at a blinking cursor.”

    “You can set it so the cursor doesn’t blink.”

    “ Fitz ,” Coulson’s tone made it clear that it really wasn’t the time.

    Fitz flushed ever so slightly, “Simmons’ has said summit about absorbing information using paper is easier…. studies and all…” his muttering trailed off as he stared at his own screen. Clint allowed a flicker of a smile at the younger man, until out of the corner of his eye he watched Barney get hit by the taxi again.

    “Are there any good shots of the baseball guy?” Laura’s tone was tight, she was holding all of her stress inside, her eyes locked on the screen, on Kate.

    “Not yet, Laur, Daisy’s workin’ on that,” he paused before wrapping his arms around her, he brought her into a tight hug, “We’re gonna find her, we’re gonna do it, we’ll bring her home, and never let her out of our sight again.”

    She hugged in return. “You promised that last time.”

    “Then I won’t promise,” he swore, “I’ll just find her. I pr-” he shook his head to stop the promise from slipping out, he pulled back to be able to see Laura, he could see Kate in her so easily, in the way she spoke, and acted, and looked. It made his heart ache.

    Laura gave a small laugh, “You can’t stop yourself from being a hero, Clint. I just… I hope she’s okay, they knocked her out with a bat, that could do anything to her, and that’s just her being taken, what’s happening to her now?”

    Clint bit his lip and looked down, he swallowed and looked up, meeting his wife’s eyes. “I think we should probably not even try to think about that, not with all the things we’ve seen in our lives… but we should be prepared for anything.”

    Three new people entered the room, Kate’s eyes flickered from their dark figures to the guy, and back again, but he was on her mind. He looked scared, he wasn’t showing it, but she could still see it.

    She couldn’t see much of the people until they stepped into the light, pushing the guy back into the shadows.

    There were two men and a woman, none of whom had any scars or tattoos or anything distinguishing, other than the fact they were clearly rich, and judging from the fact that she was tied to a dentist’s chair and they were smiling… they probably weren’t good people.

    They stared at her, her eyes started at them, trying to take in any details, but she just made herself dizzy. So she settled on the woman in the middle.

    “Guys if you were gonna talk you could have just continued watching me through the cameras in the room,” she added boredom to her tone. The man on the left looked a little surprised, only for a brief second, which confirmed her suspicions that there were cameras in there, but that had been an easy guess.

    “Nicole Barton, finally. Or do you go by Katherine Barton now?”

    Kate hesitated,“Wait… are you from Hogwarts? Is this where I get my letter and go to magic school?”

    The woman pursed her lips as though it would rid herself of the posh English accent she spoke with, “You are so like your father.”

    That made Kate shut her mouth. She sat up straighter in the chair, or at least tried to. When they didn’t work she clenched her teeth, the muscle in her jaw jumped. “I get that a lot. How do you know my dad?”

    “I knew both of them.”

    Kate refused to let herself be shaken by that. She was silent for only a second. “I only have one. You didn’t answer my question.”

    “He doesn’t know me, which is what I think the real question was. I am Henrietta Rushmore.”

    Kate sucked in a breath, “You took me before.”

    “You remember me?”

    “No. But it was an easy guess, unless I’m the most unlucky person in the world and have been kidnapped twice… and I don’t think the Barton luck is that crappy.” She wasn’t sure if Clint would agree with that, not after losing all of them at least once, or Barney…. Barney… The woman smiled good naturedly, in saying only, of course. “You have a lot of, what do you american’s call it? Spunk?” She pulled a face at the word.

    “What?”

    The man who spoke looked just as disgusted, he had a thick european accent, but Kate couldn’t figure out where abouts.

    The other man spoke to him in a different language, Kate didn’t recognise the lady, Henrietta, looked annoyed, and said something to them in a very clipped tone, the same language, but with her accent firmly in place. They both shut up and she looked back at Kate, with a gleam in her eyes.

    “I looked after you, the first time I raised you , well, for a little while. But now you’re back. We can get back to it.”

    “Back to what? Why did you take me? The first time. Why did you take me from my family?”

    “You’re the child of two spies, excellent tacticions, one became an ‘Avenger’, the other… Well, she’s completed missions which would impress even the great Natasha Romanoff.

    Which is why we first took you.”

    “I knew that part.” She said, though she supposed she had asked. “I was ‘faulty’ so you guys didn’t want me.”

    The woman nodded, “Eleanor had always phrased it like that, but it wasn’t quite so drastic, it was only a hole in your heart.”

    Kate’s eyes bugged open, “I have a hole in my heart?!”

    “It’s not a big deal,” she waved her hand off, “but we thought it would be, or it could be, you were on medication for most of your childhood and we developed an injection which your doctor gives you.”

    “What the- I, I don’t get any injections from my doctor!” She received a look. “None which I know about anyway…”

    “It was beneficial that you moved into your own flat, not that american ‘roommate’ system.” Kate froze. The suggestion hung in the air. The idea of someone coming into her apartment while she was asleep and injecting her with gods knows what… It left her feeling cold.

    “They were clearly wrong to decide to take you away from our program, I’ve been saying that since you began to compete professionally, but that’s not for me to decide.” She didn’t sound like she thought it wasn’t for her to decide. “After your show on Christmas Eve in New York City we were given the go ahead to bring you back to the program.”

    “No. Thanks but no, I’m all good, I’m really not into being a Nazi. So you can let me go now.”

    “We are not Hydra,” The first man insisted, sounding deeply offended.

    “Yes!” The second man sounded angry. “We are an offshoot.”

    “You come from the same thing, right? Until like how long ago?”

    “Until the Avengers took most of them out, why?” The first guy asked, still not getting it. The queen of England rolled her eyes, “Lockheart, Hague.” She nodded her head at the door, “Me and Kate are going to catch up.”

    Kate tried not to gulp at the sight of her smile, “If you’re saying that to try and sound menacing… it’s only working ‘cause you’re an evil kidnapper. And ‘cause I’m probably a little concussed.”

    Lockheart and Hague, the two men, left swiftly, the younger man on their heels.

    Until England whistled.

    “Stay.” She ordered.

    Her pet did as he was told.

    Coulson had mostly avoided Clint for the past couple of hours, wisely. Until Clint stood to the side, away from the computer screens and maps and files, everyone else was working and talking, Clint was just still. Coulson stood beside him, he crossed his arms over his chest. “What do you see?”

    Clint was surprised at the soft question, he hadn’t actually been looking at anything, he had been staring into space. But then he frowned a little, apparently his mind had been working in the background. “The kid, the one who washed up on the beach in Mozambique, who we were told was Kate…”

    “The one approximately the same size, similar enough features considering the decay?”

    Clint nodded. “Where’d she come from? I mean we always thought that she was Nicky, but she wasn’t. It’s just…” he paused as he thought through his words, “It doesn’t sit right with me, two kids missing, around the same age… what if they’re linked?”

    “If another child was taken?”

    “Not just a child,” Clint answered slowly, and quietly, he finally looked away from the meeting table to Coulson, “What if there were more?”
